Real Twistings are 2-Line Bundles
Tim LÜDERS a, Lynn OTTO b and Konrad WALDORF b

Abstract
We construct and study a bicategory of super 2-line bundles over graded Lie groupoids, providing a unified framework for geometric models of twistings of (real) K-theory. The core of our work is to exhibit a wide range of models from the literature as special cases, among them several variants of bundle gerbes (real/equivariant/Jandl), Freed–Moore’s twisted groupoid extensions, Freed–Hopkins–Teleman’s K-theory twistings, Moutuou’s real twistings, Freed’s invertible algebra bundles, and Distler–Freed–Moore’s orientifold twistings.



keywords
twistings of K-theory; real twistings; Lie groupoid; 2-line bundle; bicategory

AMS
19L50; 22A22; 53C08; 18N10

1
Introduction

Over the last few decades, twisted K-theory has attracted considerable interest, much of it driven by applications in theoretical physics, e.g.,
[
5
,
10
,
20
,
27
,
34
,
55
]
. Twisted cohomology theories in general can be approached via homotopy-theoretic methods, and twisted K-theory has additionally elegant operator-algebraic descriptions, e.g.,
[
29
,
48
]
.
However, when one wishes to make close contact with physical models, differential-geometric realizations of the twistings and the twisted K-theory classes become very useful.

It is well known that twistings are often easier to describe geometrically than the corresponding twisted K-theory classes, which can be difficult to treat in a strictly finite-dimensional, smooth framework. The focus of this article lies in differential-geometric models for the twistings, and, more specifically, on their typical low-degree part located in the Čech cohomology groups

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{0}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt).

(1.1)

Here, the first factor represents the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading of twisted K-theory (for
\mathrm{KO}
-theory it would have values in
\mathbb{Z}_{8}
). We remark at this point that we work without hermitian structures, which makes no difference on the cohomological level since
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 18.00162pt}}}\end{picture}\operatorname{U}(1)\hskip-0.42677pt)\cong\mathrm{H}^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.

A range of geometric models for these twists already exists. Classical work by Donavan and Karoubi

[
13
]
characterizes the torsion components of (
1.1
) via central simple super algebra bundles. The non-torsion part – i.e., general classes in

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)

– is elegantly realized by bundle gerbes
[
4
,
8
,
42
]
, objects that naturally appear in conformal field theory and higher gauge theory
[
21
,
38
]
. More recently, nice geometric objects with precisely the classification (
1.1
) have been introduced:
super
2
-line bundles
, a categorified version of super line bundles

[
31
,
36
]
. Super 2-line bundles can be viewed as a combination of bundle gerbes and central simple algebra bundles, and contain both structures as special cases.
The basic idea is that a super 2-line consists of locally defined central simple super algebra bundles, which are glued together along Morita equivalences in a consistent way. Super 2-line bundles over
X
form a bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)
, and satisfy a gluing law (they form a 2-stack).
Moreover, they have a symmetric monoidal structure
capturing precisely the ring structure of twistings in K-theory. Thus, super 2-line bundles are a complete and nice differential-geometric model for twistings on manifolds
X
.

Twisted K-theory arises naturally in string theory when D-branes wrap submanifolds of the target space and B-fields necessitate a “twist” of Chan–Paton gauge bundles
[
21
,
27
,
55
]
. Further generalizations of twisted K-theory become relevant when the target space admits additional “orbifold” symmetries. These may be implemented by group actions, or more generally, by replacing target manifolds
X
by Lie groupoids
\Gamma
. For example, equivariant versions of bundle gerbes – pioneered by Gawȩdzki–Reis and Meinrenken
[
22
,
35
]
– play a key role in describing WZW-models on non-simply connected groups.
Likewise, the Verlinde ring of loop group representations can be identified with a twisted K-theory associated to the action Lie groupoid
G/\!\!/G
, as shown in the seminal work of Freed, Hopkins, and Teleman
[
17
,
18
,
19
]
. They introduced a new model for twistings over groupoids, based on central extensions of groupoids.

The full set of twistings (again, below degree three) on a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
is classified by the Lie groupoid Čech cohomology groups

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{0}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(\Gamma,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt),

(1.2)

of which equivariant bundle gerbes realize the last factor, while
the model of Freed–Hopkins–Teleman realizes the last two factors (the first factor, the grading, is added manually).

A further layer of complexity arises in “orientifold” setups, where certain symmetries must act by complex conjugation rather than in the usual
\mathbb{C}
-linear way. This leads to so-called
graded Lie groupoids

(\Gamma,\phi)
, in which each morphism
\gamma
carries a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-label
\phi(\gamma)
indicating if
\gamma
acts linearly or anti-linearly. Graded Lie groupoids contain the previously considered cases of manifolds and Lie groupoids, as well as further configurations such as manifolds with an involution (“real spaces”) and Lie groupoids with an involution functor (“real groupoids”). One can extend the notion of Lie groupoid cohomology to such graded situations, thereby obtaining classification data of the form

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{0}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((\Gamma,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt).

(1.3)

We remark that the grading
\phi
has necessarily no effect on the first two factors.
Variations of bundle gerbes (so-called Jandl gerbes)
[
23
,
50
]
, real
[
41
]
or “
\phi
-twisted” groupoid extensions
[
20
]
, and further constructions
[
11
,
12
,
24
]
have been developed to handle twistings in these graded contexts. They appear, for instance, also in the classification of topological insulators, where Bloch bundles over the Brillouin torus become twisted depending on symmetry types
[
20
]
.

In this paper, we propose a new, comprehensive differential-geometric model for twistings over graded Lie groupoids, one which unifies
all
of the aforementioned versions. Specifically, we extend super 2-line bundles – which already capture all twistings on ordinary manifolds, (
1.1
) – first to ordinary Lie groupoids and then to graded Lie groupoids. The passage from manifolds to Lie groupoids follows a standard homotopy-limit construction applicable to any presheaf (of sets, categories, or higher categories) on the site of manifolds. The extension to graded Lie groupoids uses a further “involution” ingredient, but remains formally similar.

We thus arrive in Definition
3.7
at a bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
of super 2-line bundles over a graded Lie groupoid
(\Gamma,\phi)
that reduces – for a trivial grading
\phi=1
– to the canonical homotopy limit construction of super 2-line bundles over Lie groupoids, and moreover – for a Lie groupoid with only identity morphisms – to the ordinary bicategory of super 2-line bundles over manifolds. Up to isomorphism, super 2-line bundles over graded Lie groupoids, over Lie groupoids, and over manifolds are classified by the cohomologies (
1.3
), (
1.2
), and (
1.1
), respectively (see Theorem
3.22
). A crucial feature of our bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
is that it is invariant under Morita equivalences of graded Lie groupoids (see Theorem
3.16
).

After setting up our formalism, we show how super 2-line bundles recover or generalize several well-known types of twistings from the literature; the most important ones are

•

Jandl and other real or equivariant/anti-equivariant bundle gerbes
[
23
,
45
,
50
]
, see Theorem
5.6
. This result comes essentially from the fact that already over manifolds bundle gerbes are a subclass of 2-line bundles.

•

Freed–Moore’s
\phi
-twisted groupoid extensions
[
20
]
, see Theorem
5.15
. This result is obtained by a detailed comparison of the relevant definitions.

•

Freed–Hopkins–Teleman’s twistings by central extensions of groupoids
[
17
]
, see Theorem
5.26
. Our work here is to compare spans and refinements of weak equivalences of Lie groupoids (used by Freed–Hopkins–Teleman) with spans and refinements of surjective submersions (used in our definition of super 2-line bundles).

•

Moutuou’s real groupoid picture of twistings
[
41
]
, extending the concept of real gerbes and real central extensions, see Theorem
5.44
.

Hence, super 2-line bundles provide a single, coherent framework that not only reproduces these and many further special cases but clarifies their underlying geometry and consistency conditions.
We hope that this unification will be useful for researchers working on twisted K-theory, higher geometry, and related applications in physics.

In order to keep the present paper more transparent, we do not consider additional structures (e.g., hermitian metrics or connections) on our super 2-line bundles. We plan to include such structures in forthcoming work. Likewise, we devote little attention to the symmetric monoidal structure on super 2-line bundles over graded Lie groupoids. Finally, although we
carry out some comparisons with the twisted vector bundles belonging to each of the models, we do not delve too far into the discussion of the corresponding twisted K-theory groups.

This article is organized in the following way. Section
2
contains a comprehensive introduction to super 2-line bundles. In Section
3
, we set up the formalism of super 2-line bundles over graded Lie groupoids, and provide related results. We present in Section
4
our list of twistings from the literature that we recover. Section
5
consists of separate sections in which detailed comparison work from the list of Section
4
is carried out. Finally, we include an appendix about Lie groupoids and weak equivalences, which appear at several places.

2
Super 2-line bundles

In this section, we set the stage for the fundamental objects studied in this paper,
2
-line bundles
, i.e., categorified line bundles. In the first two subsections, we recall material derived from a broader discussion of 2-vector bundles in
[
31
]
. In the third subsection, we introduce the notion of complex conjugate 2-line bundles.

2.1
Definitions

We recall from
[
31
,
36
]
the bicategory of super 2-line bundles
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)
over a smooth manifold
X
. Throughout, we use the field of complex numbers
\mathbb{C}
as our ground field.

Definition 2.1
.

A
super
2
-line bundle
over
X
is a quadruple
\mathscr{L}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
consisting of a surjective submersion
\pi\colon Y\to X
, a central simple super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
over
Y
,
an invertible bimodule bundle
\mathcal{M}
over
Y^{[2]}
whose fibre
\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{2}}
over a point
(y_{1},y_{2})\in Y^{[2]}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{y_{2}}
-
\mathcal{A}_{y_{1}}
-bimodule, and
an invertible even intertwiner
\mu
of bimodule bundles over
Y^{[3]}
, which restricts over each point
(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})\in Y^{[3]}
to an
\mathcal{A}_{y_{3}}
-
\mathcal{A}_{y_{1}}
-bimodule intertwiner

\mu_{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}}\colon\ \mathcal{M}_{y_{2},y_{3}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{2}}\to\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{3}}.

This structure is subject to the condition that

\mu
is
associative, i.e., the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{y_{3},y_{4}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y_{3}}}\mathcal{M}_{y_{2},y_{3}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\mu_{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{y_{2},y_{3},y_{4}}\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{y_{2},y_{4}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{y_{1},y_{2},y_{4}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{y_{3},y_{4}}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}_{y_{3}}}\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{y_{1},y_{3},y_{4}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{y_{1},y_{4}}}

is commutative for all
(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},y_{4})\in Y^{[4]}
.

Remark 2.2
.

•

Definition
2.1
is almost the definition of a super 2-
vector
bundle given in
[
31
]
, just that we require here that the algebra bundle has central simple fibres, corresponding to the condition of having rank one for ordinary line bundles.

•

For precise definitions of algebra bundles and bimodule bundles we refer to
[
30
]
. In particular, all algebras are associative and unital. Due to the restriction to central simple algebra bundles, the “insidious” problems in the construction of the relative tensor product of bimodule bundles that have been observed in
[
30
]
do not appear here.

•

If
\mathscr{L}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
is a super 2-line bundle, and if
\Delta\colon Y\to Y^{[2]}
denotes the diagonal map, then there exists a canonical invertible intertwiner
\Delta^{*}\mathcal{M}\cong\mathcal{A}
of
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule bundles over
Y
. Second, if
s\colon Y^{[2]}\to Y^{[2]}
swaps the factors, then
s^{*}\mathcal{M}
is inverse to
\mathcal{M}
.

We suppose that
\mathscr{L}_{1}=(\pi_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{1},\mu_{1})
and
\mathscr{L}_{2}=(\pi_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{2},\mu_{2})
are super 2-line bundles over
X
.

Definition 2.3
.

A
1
-morphism

\mathscr{P}\colon\mathscr{L}_{1}\to\mathscr{L}_{2}

is a triple
\mathscr{P}=(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
consisting of a surjective submersion
\zeta\colon Z\to Y_{1}\times_{X}Y_{2}
, a bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}
over
Z
, whose fibre
\mathcal{P}_{z}
over a point
{z\in Z}
with
\zeta(z)=:(y_{1},y_{2})
is an
(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}
-
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
-bimodule, and
an invertible even intertwiner
\phi
of bimodule bundles over
Z^{[2]}
, which restricts over a point
(z,z^{\prime})\in Z^{[2]}
with
\zeta(z^{\prime})=:(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime})
to an
(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}^{\prime}}
-
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
-intertwiner

\phi_{z,z^{\prime}}\colon\ \mathcal{P}_{z^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}\to(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{z}.

This intertwiner is a “homomorphism” with respect to the intertwiners
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
, in the sense that the diagram

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\phi_{z,z^{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{z}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

(2.1)

is commutative for all
(z,z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})\in Z^{[3]}
, where
\zeta(z^{\prime\prime})=:(y_{1}^{\prime\prime},y_{2}^{\prime\prime})
.

Finally, we recall how the 2-morphisms are defined. Consider two 1-morphisms between the same super 2-line bundles,

\textstyle{\mathscr{L}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{P}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{P}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{L}_{2},}

with all structure denoted and labelled as above.
For abbreviation, we set
Y_{12}:=Y_{1}\times_{X}Y_{2}
.

Definition 2.4
.

A
2
-morphism

\mathscr{P}\Rightarrow\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
is an equivalence class of pairs
(\rho,\varphi)
consisting of a surjective submersion
\rho\colon W\to Z\times_{Y_{12}}Z^{\prime}
, and
an intertwiner
\varphi
of bimodule bundles over
W
that restricts over a point
w\in W
with
\rho(w)=:(z,z^{\prime})
to an intertwiner

\varphi_{w}\colon\mathcal{P}_{z}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{z^{\prime}}

of
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
-
(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}
-bimodules, where
\zeta(z)=\zeta^{\prime}(z^{\prime})=:(y_{1},y_{2})
. This intertwiner is subject to the condition that it commutes with the intertwiners
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
, in the sense that the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{z}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{\tilde{y}_{1}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},\tilde{y}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\tilde{w}}\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{z,\tilde{z}}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},\tilde{y}_{2}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\varphi_{w}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\tilde{z}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{\tilde{y}_{1}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{y}_{1}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime}_{z^{\prime},\tilde{z}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{y}_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y^{\prime}_{2}}}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{z^{\prime}}}

is commutative for all
(w,\tilde{w})\in W\times_{X}W
, where
\rho(w)=(z,z^{\prime})
,
\rho(\tilde{w})=(\tilde{z},\tilde{z}^{\prime})
.
Two pairs
(\rho,\varphi)
and
(\rho^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
are equivalent if the pullbacks of
\varphi
and
\varphi^{\prime}
coincide over
W\times_{Z\times_{Y_{12}}Z^{\prime}}W^{\prime}
.

The structure collected above forms the bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)
of super 2-line bundles over
X
.
We recall the following result about the invertibility of 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms
[
31
, Lemma 2.3.4]
.

Proposition 2.5
.

1.

A
1
-morphism
\mathscr{P}=(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
is invertible if and only if its bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}
is invertible in the bicategory of central simple super algebra bundles.

2.

A
1
-morphism
\mathscr{P}=(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
has a right
(
left
)
adjoint if and only if its bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}
has a right
(
left
)
adjoint in the bicategory of central simple super algebra bundles.

3.

A
2
-morphism
(\rho,\varphi)
is invertible if and only if its intertwiner
\varphi
is invertible.

We also recall the following classification result
[
31
, Theorem 4.4.4]
.

Theorem 2.6
.

The set of isomorphism classes of super
2
-line bundles over
X
is in bijection with the set

\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathrm{BW}_{\mathbb{C}})\times\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\mathrm{H}^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z}),

where
\mathrm{BW}_{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is the Brauer–Wall group of Morita equivalence classes of central simple super algebras over
\mathbb{C}
.

Remark 2.7
.

We recall how the classification of Theorem
2.6
comes about by extracting cocycles from a super 2-line bundle
\mathscr{L}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
; see
[
31
, Section 4]
and
[
36
, Section 2.2]
. We choose an open cover
\mathcal{U}=(U_{i})_{i\in I}
of
X
with simply-connected open sets
U_{i}
that allow sections
{s_{i}\colon U_{i}\to Y}
.
The pullback
s_{i}^{*}\mathcal{A}
is then trivializable,
s_{i}^{*}\mathcal{A}\cong U_{i}\times A_{i}^{\prime}
, where
A_{i}^{\prime}
is a central simple super algebra. We fix for each element in
\mathrm{BW}_{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
a representative; for instance, the neutral element may be represented by
A=\mathbb{C}
(purely even), and the non-trivial element may be represented by a super algebra
A=\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}u
, where
u
is an odd element and
{u^{2}=1}
.
Let
A_{i}
denote the representative of the class of
A_{i}^{\prime}
. Then, with
\mathcal{A}_{i}:=U_{i}\times A_{i}
, we obtain an invertible
s_{i}^{*}\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{A}_{i}
-bimodule bundle
\mathcal{N}_{i}
over
U_{i}
. We may also view
\mathcal{N}_{i}
as an isomorphism
\mathscr{N}_{i}\colon\mathcal{A}_{i}\to\mathscr{L}|_{U_{i}}
in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(U_{i})
, a local trivialization. We obtain a (constant) map
{\alpha_{i}\colon U_{i}\to\mathrm{BW}_{\mathbb{C}}}
with value
A_{i}
.
On a double overlap
U_{ij}:=U_{i}\cap U_{j}
, we consider the corresponding section
s_{ij}:=(s_{i},s_{j})\colon U_{ij}\to Y^{[2]}
, and the
s_{j}^{*}\mathcal{A}
-
s_{i}^{*}\mathcal{A}
-bimodule bundle
s_{ij}^{*}\mathcal{M}
.
We obtain the invertible
\mathcal{A}_{j}
-
\mathcal{A}_{i}
-bimodule bundle
\mathcal{M}_{ij}:=\mathcal{N}^{-1}_{j}\otimes_{s_{j}^{*}\mathcal{A}}s_{ij}^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes_{s_{i}^{*}\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{N}_{i}
over
U_{ij}
.
We may also view
\mathcal{M}_{ij}=\mathscr{N}_{j}^{-1}\circ\mathscr{N}_{i}\colon\mathcal{A}_{i}\to\mathcal{A}_{j}
as the “transition function” for the local trivializations
\mathscr{N}_{i}
. We also remark that
\mathcal{M}_{ij}\cong U_{ij}\times M_{ij}
, with
M_{ij}
an invertible
A_{j}
-
A_{i}
-bimodule. Since
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
are – by construction – both the fixed representative of the same Morita equivalence class, we have
A:=A_{i}=A_{j}
and
\alpha_{i}=\alpha_{j}
. Now,
M_{ij}
is an invertible
A
-
A
-bimodule of the central simple super algebra
A
, whose well-known classification implies that either
M_{ij}\cong A
or
{M_{ij}\cong\Pi A}
, the grading reversal of
A
.
Writing
\varepsilon A
to mean either
A
(when
\varepsilon=1
) or
\Pi A
(when
\varepsilon=-1
), we obtain a map
\epsilon_{ij}\colon U_{ij}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
such that
M_{ij}\cong\varepsilon_{ij}A
. On a triple overlap
{U_{ijk}:=U_{i}\cap U_{j}\cap U_{k}}
, the isomorphism
\mu
becomes, under the above isomorphisms, an invertible intertwiner
\mu_{ijk}^{\prime}\colon U_{ijk}\times(\varepsilon_{jk}A\otimes_{A}\varepsilon_{ij}A)\to U_{ijk}\times\varepsilon_{ik}A
, where
A:=A_{i}=A_{j}=A_{k}
. Thus, we have
\varepsilon_{jk}A\otimes_{A}\varepsilon_{ij}A\cong(\varepsilon_{jk}\varepsilon_{ij})A
; this implies
\varepsilon_{jk}\varepsilon_{ij}=\varepsilon_{ik}
.
Automorphisms of the
A
-
A
-bimodule
A
are given by multiplication with elements in
Z(A^{\times})
, and since
A
is central,
\mu^{\prime}_{ijk}
becomes a smooth map
\mu_{ijk}\colon U_{ijk}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. The associativity condition in Definition
2.1
implies the cocycle condition for
\mu_{ijk}
. The triple
(\alpha_{i},\varepsilon_{ij},\mu_{ijk})
is a Čech cocycle representative of the class of
\mathscr{L}
under the classification of Theorem
2.6
(under the usual identification
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(X,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)\cong\mathrm{H}^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})
).

Remark 2.8
.

A non-super version of super 2-line bundles is included into our discussion as those super 2-line bundles where the odd components of all involved super algebra bundles and bimodule bundles are zero. This sets up the bicategory
2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)
of 2-line bundles, and the classification of Theorem
2.6
reduces to the set
\mathrm{H}^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, as the Brauer group of
\mathbb{C}
is trivial.

If one restricts Definition
2.1
to the trivial algebra bundle with fibre
\mathbb{C}
,
\mathcal{A}=Y\times\mathbb{C}
, the bimodule bundles in Definitions
2.1
and
2.3
become just super vector bundles. Moreover, in Definition
2.1
, the requirement that the bimodule bundle
\mathcal{M}
is invertible implies that
\mathcal{M}
has rank one, i.e., it is a super line bundle.
Thus, above definitions reduce to the ones of (super) bundle gerbes
[
42
]
, and we obtain the following result, see
[
31
, Proposition 3.2.2]
.

Proposition 2.9
.

There are, respectively, a fully faithful functor and an equivalence of bicategories,

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)\qquad\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)

that embed
(
super
)
bundle gerbes into
(
super
)

2
-line bundles. Under the classification of Theorem
2.6
, their images correspond to the subsets
\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\mathrm{H}^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and
\mathrm{H}^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, respectively.

We denote by
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}(X)
the bicategory of central simple super algebra bundles over
X
, with bimodules as the 1-morphisms.
The definition of the bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)
recalled above was obtained in
[
31
]
by applying the plus construction of
[
45
]
to the presheaf
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}
. Recall that the classical plus construction of Grothendieck assigns to a presheaf
\mathcal{F}
a sheaf
\mathcal{F}^{+}
, its sheafification. The sheafification functor
\mathcal{F}\mapsto\mathcal{F}^{+}
can be characterized abstractly as the left adjoint to the inclusion functor of sheaves into presheaves, or can be described by a concrete model. In the concrete model,
\mathcal{F}^{+}(X)
consists of matching families of local sections of
\mathcal{F}
modulo a suitable equivalence relation: two families are identified if they agree on a common refinement.

Nikolaus and Schweigert
[
45
]
have generalized this concrete description to presheaves of bicategories. Intuitively,
\mathcal{F}^{+}
assembles all compatible collections of locally defined objects together with the gluing data relating them.
Applying their construction here yields the definition

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X):=\bigl(\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\bigr)^{+}(X).

In other words, super 2-line bundles over
X
are objects obtained by gluing central simple super algebra bundles along invertible bimodule bundles.
As a sheafification, the plus construction comes with an embedding of the underlying presheaf; see
[
31
, Section 3.3]
.

Proposition 2.10
.

There are fully faithful functors

\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{bi}(X)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)\qquad\mathrm{cs}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{bi}(X)\to 2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)

that embed central simple
(
super
)
algebra bundles into
(
super
) 2
-line bundles. Under the classification of Theorem
2.6
, their images corresponds to the subsets

\mathrm{H}^{0}(X,\mathrm{BW}_{\mathbb{C}})\times\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\operatorname{Tor}\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})\bigr)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\operatorname{Tor}\bigl(\mathrm{H}^{3}(X,\mathbb{Z})\bigr),

respectively.

The functors in Proposition
2.10
have the simple description to use the identity surjective submersion
\pi=\operatorname{id}_{X}
, the given algebra bundles, and identity bimodules and intertwiners.
We remark that the cohomological classification of the bicategories
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{bi}(X)
and
\mathrm{cs}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{bi}(X)
reproduces a classical result of Donavan–Karoubi
[
13
]
.

Remark 2.11
.

Theorem
2.6
can be enhanced to a group isomorphism by equipping the bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)
with a symmetric monoidal structure. Then, by
[
31
, Corollary 4.4.10]
, every super 2-line bundle is the tensor product of a central simple algebra bundle (under the functor of Proposition
2.10
) and of a super bundle gerbe (under the functor of Proposition
2.9
).

Another consequence of the definition of (super) 2-line bundles as the sheafification is the following result
[
31
, Theorem 2.3.3]
, which we state for completeness.

Theorem 2.12
.

(
Super
)

2
-line bundles form a
2
-stack on the site of smooth manifolds.

2.2
Framing

Many bicategories provide a way to induce 1-morphisms from a simpler class of morphisms that compose with strict associativity. We handle this situation within the following framework.

Definition 2.13
.

A
framed bicategory
consists of a bicategory
\mathscr{B}
, a category
\mathscr{C}
with the same objects,
and a functor
F\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{B}
such that

•

F
is the identity on the level of objects and

•

the image of every morphism of
\mathscr{C}
admits a right adjoint in
\mathscr{B}
.

In this definition, by functor we mean a
strong pseudofunctor
, i.e., when
f
and
g
are composable morphisms in
\mathscr{C}
, it comes equipped with a 2-isomorphism
\eta_{f,}\colon F(g)\circ F(f)\Rightarrow F(g\circ f)
, and these are coherent on triples of composable morphisms. There is a relation between framed bicategories and double categories
[
52
]
; see
[
30
, Remark 2.1.7]
.
Synonymously to Definition
2.13
, we also use the terminology that “
\mathscr{B}
is framed under
\mathscr{C}
”, and that “
F
is a framing of
\mathscr{B}
”.

Example 2.14
.

The bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}
of super algebras is framed under the category
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}
, by assigning to a homomorphism
\varphi\colon A\to B
between super algebras the
B
-
A
-bimodule
B_{\varphi}
, whose underlying vector space is
B
, the left
B
-action is multiplication, and the right
A
-action is multiplication along
\varphi
. The “insidious” nature of the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}(X)
prevents this framing to carry over to algebra bundles, see
[
30
, Section 3.3]
. However, the bicategory
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}(X)
of
central simple
super algebra bundles is framed under the category of central simple super algebra bundles.

Example 2.15
.

The bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X)
of super bundle gerbes over
X
is framed under a category
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\mathrm{ref}}(X)
.
Recall from Section
2.1
that a super bundle gerbe is a super 2-line whose super algebra bundle is the trivial one with fibre
\mathbb{C}
, in the following denoted by just
\mathscr{G}=(\pi,\mathcal{M},\mu)
. Objects in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\mathrm{ref}}(X)
are super bundle gerbes
\mathscr{G}=(\pi,\mathcal{M},\mu)
over
X
, and morphisms
\mathscr{G}_{1}\to\mathscr{G}_{2}
in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\mathrm{ref}}(X)
are pairs
(\rho,u)

consisting of a smooth map
\rho\colon Y_{1}\to Y_{2}
and an invertible super line bundle morphism
u\colon\mathcal{M}_{1}\to\rho^{*}\mathcal{M}_{2}
over
Y_{1}^{[2]}
such that
\pi_{2}\circ\rho=\pi_{1}

and the following diagram commutes:

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 66.7658pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-45.96776pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y),\rho(y^{\prime\prime})}.}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

(2.2)

The morphisms in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\mathrm{ref}}(X)
are called
refinements
. We will not describe the framing functor
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\mathrm{ref}}(X)\to\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X)
here; instead,
we provide in Definition
2.16
below a more general construction that can easily be reduced to the case of super bundle gerbes.

In the following we describe a canonical framing for the bicategory of super 2-line bundles over
X
, deduced in
[
31
, Section 3.5]
as a feature of the plus construction.

Definition 2.16
.

Let
\mathscr{L}_{1}=(\pi_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{1},\mu_{1})
and
\mathscr{L}_{2}=(\pi_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{2},\mu_{2})
be super 2-line bundles over
X
.
A
refinement

\mathscr{R}\colon\mathscr{L}_{1}\to\mathscr{L}_{2}
is a triple
\mathscr{R}=(\rho,\varphi,u)
consisting of a smooth map
\rho\colon Y_{1}\to Y_{2}
such that
\pi_{2}\circ\rho=\pi_{1}
, of a homomorphism
\varphi\colon\mathcal{A}_{1}\to\rho^{*}\mathcal{A}_{2}
of super algebra bundles over
Y_{1}
and
of an invertible bundle morphism
u\colon\mathcal{M}_{1}\to\rho^{*}\mathcal{M}_{2}
over
Y_{1}^{[2]}

that over a point
(y,y^{\prime})\in Y_{1}^{[2]}
restricts to an intertwiner

u_{y,y^{\prime}}\colon(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y,y^{\prime}}\to(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y),\rho(y^{\prime})}

along the algebra homomorphisms
\varphi_{y^{\prime}}\colon(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y^{\prime}}\to(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{\rho(y^{\prime})}
and
\varphi_{y}\colon(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y}\to(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{\rho(y)}
, and renders the diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 81.97456pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-56.65305pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 132.37364pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 132.37364pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 154.23253pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y^{\prime}),\rho(y^{\prime\prime})}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{\rho(y^{\prime})}}(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y),\rho(y^{\prime})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.97462pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 121.97462pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y),\rho(y^{\prime\prime})}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

commutative for all
(y,y^{\prime},y^{\prime\prime})\in Y_{1}^{[3]}
.

Given two refinements

\mathscr{R}_{12}=(\rho_{12},\varphi_{12},u_{12})\colon\mathscr{L}_{1}\to\mathscr{L}_{2}
,

\mathscr{R}_{23}=(\rho_{23},\varphi_{23},u_{23})\colon\mathscr{L}_{2}\to\mathscr{L}_{3}
,
their composition is given by

\mathscr{R}_{23}\circ\mathscr{R}_{12}:=\bigl(\rho_{23}\circ\rho_{12},\rho_{12}^{*}\varphi_{23}\circ\varphi_{12},\bigl(\rho_{12}^{[2]}\bigr)^{*}u_{23}\circ u_{12}\bigr),

and the identity morphism of
\mathscr{L}
is
(\operatorname{id}_{Y},\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{A}},\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{M}})
.
This defines the category
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}_{k}(X)
.

The framing

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}(X)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)

(2.3)

is defined as follows. On the level of objects, it is the identity. On the level of morphisms, they associate to a refinement
\mathscr{R}=(\rho,\varphi,u)\colon\mathscr{L}_{1}\to\mathscr{L}_{2}
the following 1-morphism
(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
. We define
{Z:=Y_{1}\times_{X}Y_{2}}
and
\zeta=\operatorname{id}_{Z}
. Consider the smooth map
\tilde{\rho}\colon Z\to Y_{2}^{[2]}
with
\tilde{\rho}(y_{1},y_{2}):=(\rho(y_{1}),y_{2})
.
We define the bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}:=(\tilde{\rho}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\varphi}
over
Z
, in which the right
\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}_{1}
-action is along the algebra homomorphism
\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\varphi\colon\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}_{1}\to\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\rho^{*}\mathcal{A}_{2}
.
Over a point
(y_{1},y_{2})\in Z
, its fibre is
\mathcal{P}_{y_{1},y_{2}}=((\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}),y_{2}})_{\varphi_{y_{1}}}
, which is an
(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}
-
(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}}
-bimodule.
Finally, we define the intertwiner
\phi
fibre-wise over a point
((y_{1},y_{2}),(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime}))\in Z^{[2]}
as the following composite:

\textstyle{\hskip-20.00003pt\mathcal{P}_{y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}=((\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}^{\prime}),y_{2}^{\prime}})_{\varphi_{y_{1}^{\prime}}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{1})_{y_{1}^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes u_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}^{\prime}),y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}^{\prime})}}((\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}),\rho(y_{1}^{\prime})})_{\varphi_{y_{1}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mu_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}),\rho(y_{1}^{\prime}),y_{2}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{((\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}),y_{2}^{\prime}})_{\varphi_{y_{1}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mu_{2})_{\rho(y_{1}),y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}^{-1}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}((\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\rho(y_{1},)y_{2}})_{\varphi_{y_{1}}}=(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes_{(\mathcal{A}_{2})_{y_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{y_{1},y_{2}}.\hskip-200.0003pt}

It is shown in
[
31
, Proposition 3.5.2]
that this is indeed a framing.

For later use, we state the following result.

Lemma 2.17
.

A refinement
\mathscr{R}=(\rho,\varphi,u)
induces an invertible
1
-morphism if the algebra bundle morphism
\varphi
is invertible.

Proof.

The induced 1-morphism is by Proposition
2.5

1
invertible if and only if its bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}=(\tilde{\rho}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{2})_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\varphi}
over
Z
is invertible. In turn, a bimodule bundle is invertible if and only if it is fibre-wise invertible
[
30
, Lemma 4.2.8]
.
This is the case when
\mathcal{M}_{2}
is invertible and
\varphi
is an isomorphism.
∎

Example 2.18
.

A standard source for refinements are situations in which a super 2-line bundle
\mathscr{L}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
is given together with a refinement of its surjective submersion
{\pi\colon Y\to X}
, i.e., given is another surjective submersion
\pi^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\to X
together with a smooth map
\rho\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
such that
\pi\circ\rho=\pi^{\prime}
. Then, one may define a second super 2-line bundle

\mathscr{L}^{\rho}:=\bigl(\pi^{\prime},\rho^{*}\mathcal{A},\bigl(\rho^{[2]}\bigr)^{*}\mathcal{M},\allowbreak\bigl(\rho^{[3]}\bigr)^{*}\mu\bigr)

and observe that
\mathscr{R}^{\rho}:=(\rho,\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})
is a refinement
\mathscr{L}^{\rho}\to\mathscr{L}
that induces by Lemma
2.17
an isomorphism
\mathscr{L}^{\rho}\cong\mathscr{L}
in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)
.

For later use, we record the appropriate notion of morphisms between framed bicategories.

Definition 2.19
.

Let
F\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{B}
and
F^{\prime}\colon\mathscr{C}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{B}^{\prime}
be framed bicategories. A
framed functor
consists of a pair of functors
G_{1}\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}^{\prime}
and
G_{2}\colon\mathscr{B}\to\mathscr{B}^{\prime}
and a pseudonatural equivalence

A framed functor is called an
equivalence of framed bicategories
if
G_{1}
and

are equivalences.

Example 2.20
.

There are natural and evident functors

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\mathrm{ref}}(X)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}(X)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}(X),

which are framed functors, see
[
31
, Section 3.5]
. We remark that all vertical functors in these diagrams are in fact fully faithful.

2.3
Complex conjugation

Let
V
be a finite dimensional complex vector space. We recall that the
complex conjugate
vector space
\overline{V}
consists of the same additive group

V
and the scalar multiplication given by
\lambda\cdot v:=\bar{\lambda}v
. Further, we recall that any linear map
f\colon V\to W
between complex
vector spaces is a linear map
\overline{V}\to\overline{W}
, for which we will use the notation
\overline{f}
in order to emphasize the changed domain and codomain.

If
A
is a complex algebra, then we define the complex conjugate algebra
\overline{A}
to have the complex conjugate of the underlying vector space, and the same multiplication (which is, as a map
\overline{A}\times\overline{A}\to\overline{A}
, bilinear). Similarly, in the situation of bimodules, we complex conjugate the underlying vector space and leave the actions as they are. This way, complex conjugation becomes a functor

\overline{(..)}\colon\ \mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}}\to\mathrm{s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}^{\mathrm{bi}},

which is strictly involutive.
It is clear that complex conjugation extends to algebra bundles and bimodule bundles, hence to the bicategory of algebra bundles, and moreover even to a morphism of presheaves of bicategories.

It is now straightforward
to define the
complex conjugate
of a super 2-line bundle
\mathscr{L}=(\pi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{M},\mu)
: indeed, we use the same surjective submersion

\pi
, the complex conjugate algebra bundle
\overline{\mathcal{A}}
, the complex conjugate bimodule bundle
\overline{\mathcal{M}}
, and the intertwiner
\overline{\mu}
.
The complex conjugate super 2-line bundle defined in this way is denoted
\overline{\mathscr{L}}
. Similarly, we define complex conjugate 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms of super 2-line bundles.
It is clear that this construction extends to a 2-functor

\overline{(..)}\colon\ \mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X),

(2.4)

which is strictly involutive, and again a morphism of presheaves of bicategories.

3
Super 2-line bundles over graded Lie groupoids

The purpose of this section is to introduce a very general theory
of super 2-line bundles over
graded Lie groupoids
, a notion that includes various structures that have appeared before in the literature. We provide a descent result and a cohomological classification.

3.1
Graded Lie groupoids

Lie groupoids encode all sorts of equivariance; they include orbifolds
and even more general structures. Traditional equivariance, where a Lie group
G
acts on a smooth manifold
X
, is included by considering the action groupoid
X/\!\!/G
. The case of no equivariance is included by associating to a smooth manifold
X
the Lie groupoid
X_{\rm dis}
with only identity morphisms.

If
\Gamma
is a Lie groupoid, we will denote by
s,t\colon\Gamma_{1}\to\Gamma_{0}
the source and target maps, by
\Gamma_{k}:=\Gamma_{1}\hskip-1.49994pt\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{s}}}}\times^{{\kern-15.53206pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern the smooth manifold of
k
-tuples of composable morphisms, and by
{c\colon\Gamma_{2}\to\Gamma_{1}}
the composition map.

Every presheaf
\mathscr{F}
on the category of smooth manifolds extends canonically to a presheaf on the category of Lie groupoids (whose morphisms are smooth functors), via the homotopy limit over the associated simplicial manifold,

\mathscr{F}(\Gamma):=\mathrm{holim}(\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern (3.1)

see, e.g.,
[
45
]
. In particular, as super 2-line bundles form a sheaf of bicategories on the site of smooth manifolds (see Theorem
2.12
), we obtain a bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma)
of super 2-line bundles over a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
. If
G
is a Lie group acting on
X
, then, by definition, a
G
-equivariant super 2-line bundle over
X
is a super 2-line bundle over the action groupoid
\Gamma=X/\!\!/G
.

In this paper, we need a yet more general version of equivariance, namely, equivariance with respect to
graded
Lie groupoids. These appeared in
[
17
, Definition 2.4]
(in a topological setting).

Definition 3.1
.

A
graded Lie groupoid
is a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
equipped with a smooth functor

\phi\colon\Gamma\to\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, where the latter denotes the groupoid with a single object and two morphisms.

We use the convention that
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\{1,-1\}
. Saying that
\phi
is a smooth functor is equivalent to saying that
\phi\colon\Gamma_{1}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is a locally constant map such that
\phi(\gamma_{2}\circ\gamma_{1})=\phi(\gamma_{2})\phi(\gamma_{1})
holds for all composable morphisms
\gamma_{2}
and
\gamma_{1}
, and
\phi(\operatorname{id}_{x})=1
holds for all objects
x
. If
(\Gamma,\phi)
is a graded Lie groupoid, we denote by
(\Gamma,\phi)^{\rm even}
the Lie groupoid with the same objects and only those morphisms
\gamma
with
\phi(\gamma)=1
; this is a sub-Lie groupoid of
\Gamma
.
The general idea is that the sub-groupoid
(\Gamma,\phi)^{\rm even}
captures all sort of “orbifold”-equivariance, for instance, with respect to group actions, while the additional morphisms with
\phi=-1
parameterize possible “orientifold”-equivariance (a.k.a. anti-equivariance, real-equivariance, etc.).

Example 3.2
.

We have the following main examples of graded Lie groupoids in mind:

1.

Smooth manifolds
X
, considered as groupoids with only identity morphisms,
\Gamma=X_{\rm dis}
. Note that necessarily
\phi=1
in this case.

2.

Graded Lie groups
(G,\varepsilon)
, i.e., Lie groups
G
with a smooth group homomorphism
\varepsilon\colon G\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. We consider
G
as a groupoid with a single object,
\Gamma=\mathrm{B}G
, and equip it with the grading
\phi:=\varepsilon
.

3.

Graded action groupoids, formed by a smooth manifold
X
, a graded Lie group
(G,\varepsilon)
, and a smooth (right) action of
G
on
X
. Then,
\Gamma:=X/\!\!/G
is the usual action groupoid, and the grading
\phi\colon X\times G\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is
\phi:=\varepsilon\circ\operatorname{pr}_{G}
. We also write
X/\!\!/(G,\varepsilon)
for the graded action groupoid. It reduces to
1
when
G=\{e\}
and to
2
when
X=\{\ast\}
.

4.

Real manifolds
(X,\tau)
, i.e., smooth manifolds
X
with a smooth involution
\tau\colon X\to X
. We consider
\tau
as an action of the group
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
on
X
, and we consider
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
graded under the identity map
\varepsilon:=\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
. Then, we form the graded action groupoid
\mathrm{Gr}(X,\tau):=X/\!\!/(\mathbb{Z}_{2},\operatorname{id})
of
3
.

Analogous to the extension of presheaves on manifolds to (ordinary, ungraded) Lie groupoids, one can extend them to
graded
Lie groupoids whenever the presheaf is equipped with a strictly involutive presheaf automorphism. In the following subsection, we illustrate this process for the presheaf of super 2-line bundles, whose automorphism is given by complex conjugation (see Section
2.3
).

3.2
Main definitions

We fix the convention that an upper index
(..)^{\phi}
on super 2-line bundles, 1-morphisms, or 2-morphisms, where
\phi\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is a smooth map, means the application of the complex conjugation functor (
2.4
) on connected components with
\phi=-1
, and has no effect on components with
\phi=1
.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
(\Gamma,\phi)
be a graded Lie groupoid. A
super
2
-line bundle
over
(\Gamma,\phi)
is a triple
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
consisting of

•

a super 2-line bundle
\mathscr{L}
over
\Gamma_{0}
,

•

a 1-isomorphism of super 2-line bundles

\mathscr{P}\colon\ s^{*}\mathscr{L}\to(t^{*}\mathscr{L})^{\phi}

(3.2)

over
\Gamma_{1}
, and

•

a 2-isomorphism of super 2-line bundles

\psi\colon\ (\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathscr{P})^{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\phi}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}\Rightarrow c^{*}\mathscr{P}

(3.3)

over
\Gamma_{2}
,

such that the following diagram over
\Gamma_{3}
is commutative

(3.4)

Here, and in what follows, unlabelled arrows represent the canonical 2-isomorphisms arising from either the bicategorical structure or the presheaf structure of super 2-line bundles.
Whenever appropriate, we will also call
(\mathscr{P},\psi)
a
graded-equivariant structure
on the super 2-line bundle
\mathscr{L}
over
\Gamma_{0}
. In fibre-wise notation, we have

\mathscr{P}_{\gamma}\colon\ \mathscr{L}_{s(\gamma)}\to\mathscr{L}_{t(\gamma)}^{\phi(\gamma)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\psi_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\colon\ \mathscr{P}_{\gamma_{1}}^{\phi(\gamma_{2})}\circ\mathscr{P}_{\gamma_{2}}\Rightarrow\mathscr{P}_{\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2}}.

Remark 3.4
.

The pullback of
\mathscr{P}
along
\operatorname{id}\colon\Gamma_{0}\to\Gamma_{1}
is a 1-isomorphism
\operatorname{id}^{*}\mathscr{P}\colon\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{L}
, and the pullback of
\psi
to
\Gamma_{0}
gives a 2-isomorphism
(\operatorname{id}^{*}\mathscr{P})^{2}\cong\operatorname{id}^{*}\mathscr{P}
. This shows that
\operatorname{id}^{*}\mathscr{P}\cong\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{L}}
; moreover, under this identification the pullback of
\psi
along either of the maps
\gamma\mapsto(\gamma,\operatorname{id}_{s(\gamma)})
and
\gamma\mapsto(\operatorname{id}_{t(\gamma)},\gamma)
is
\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{P}}
.

Definition 3.5
.

Let
(\mathscr{L}_{1},\mathscr{P}_{1},\psi_{1})
and
(\mathscr{L}_{2},\mathscr{P}_{2},\psi_{2})
be
super 2-line bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
. A
1
-morphism

(\mathscr{L}_{1},\mathscr{P}_{1},\psi_{1})\to(\mathscr{L}_{2},\mathscr{P}_{2},\psi_{2})
is a pair

(\mathscr{B},\eta)
consisting of

•

a 1-morphism of super 2-line bundles

\mathscr{B}\colon\ \mathscr{L}_{1}\to\mathscr{L}_{2}

(3.5)

over
\Gamma_{0}
, and

•

a 2-isomorphism of super 2-line bundles

\eta\colon\ \mathscr{P}_{2}\circ s^{*}\mathscr{B}\Rightarrow(t^{*}\mathscr{B})^{\phi}\circ\mathscr{P}_{1}

(3.6)

over
\Gamma_{1}
,

such that the following diagram over
\Gamma_{2}
is commutative

(3.7)

The identity 1-morphism of a super 2-line bundle
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
over
(\Gamma,\phi)
is
(\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{L}},\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{P}})
.
In the situation of three super 2-line bundles
(\mathscr{L}_{1},\mathscr{P}_{1},\psi_{1})
,
(\mathscr{L}_{2},\mathscr{P}_{2},\psi_{2})
, and
(\mathscr{L}_{3},\mathscr{P}_{3},\psi_{3})
over
(\Gamma,\phi)
, the composition of two 1-morphisms

Definition 3.6
.

Let
(\mathscr{L}_{1},\mathscr{P}_{1},\psi_{1})
and
(\mathscr{L}_{2},\mathscr{P}_{2},\psi_{2})
be
super 2-line bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
, and let
(\mathscr{B},\eta)
and
(\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})

be 1-morphisms
(\mathscr{L}_{1},\mathscr{P}_{1},\psi_{1})\to(\mathscr{L}_{2},\mathscr{P}_{2},\psi_{2})
.
A 2-morphism

\xi\colon\ (\mathscr{B},\eta)\Rightarrow(\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})

is a 2-morphism

\xi\colon\mathscr{B}\Rightarrow\mathscr{B}^{\prime}

over
\Gamma_{0}
such that the following diagram over
\Gamma_{1}
is commutative

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{t^{*}\mathscr{B}^{\prime\phi}\circ\mathscr{P}_{1}.}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(3.8)

Vertical and horizontal composition of 2-morphisms of super 2-line bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
are given by the
vertical and horizontal composition in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma_{0})
, respectively. Thus, super 2-line bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
form a bicategory. It is the central structure of this article.

Definition 3.7
.

The bicategory of super 2-line bundles over a graded Lie groupoid
(\Gamma,\phi)
is denoted by
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Remark 3.8
.

Direct consequences of the definitions are the following statements about invertibility:

1.

A 2-morphism
\xi\colon(\mathscr{B},\eta)\Rightarrow(\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
is invertible if and only if
\xi\colon\mathscr{B}\Rightarrow\mathscr{B}^{\prime}

is invertible in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma_{0})
.

2.

A 1-morphism
(\mathscr{B},\eta)
is invertible if and only if
\mathscr{B}
is invertible in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma_{0})
.

3.

A 1-morphism has
(\mathscr{B},\eta)
has a right (left)
adjoint if and only if
\mathscr{B}
has a right (left) adjoint in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma_{0})
.

Remark 3.9
.

If
\phi
is trivial (i.e.,
\phi=1
), we also just write

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma)
, and by inspection, we observe that this reproduces the homotopy limit construction (
3.1
), i.e., the canonical definition of super 2-line bundles over Lie groupoids. In particular, when
\Gamma=X_{\rm dis}
is a smooth manifold, we have a canonical equivalence of bicategories

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X_{\rm dis})\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)
,
i.e., we reproduce the bicategory of super 2-line bundles over
X
described in Section
2.1
.

Definition 3.10
.

We introduce several sub-bicategories
of
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
:

1.

Restricting Definition
3.3
along the fully faithful embedding
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}
of super bundle gerbes, we obtain the full sub-bicategory

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma,\phi)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)

of super bundle gerbes over
(\Gamma,\phi)
.

2.

Restricting Definition
3.3
along the fully faithful embedding
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}
of central simple super algebra bundles, we obtain the full sub-bicategory

\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}(\Gamma,\phi)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)

of central simple super algebra bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
.

3.

Restricting Definition
3.3
to the case where the super 2-line bundle
\mathscr{L}
is the
trivial
super 2-line bundle
\mathscr{I}
, we obtain the full sub-bicategory

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma,\phi)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)

of “graded-equivariant structures” on the trivial super 2-line bundle.

4.

Restricting Definitions
3.3
,
3.5
, and
3.6
, as well as the sub-bicategories of (a), (b), and (c) to the non-super case, we obtain bicategories
2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
,
\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma,\phi)
,
\mathrm{cs}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}(\Gamma,\phi)
, and
2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma,\phi)
.

All together, we have – for each graded Lie groupoid
(\Gamma,\phi)
– a commutative diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 65.56955pt\raise-89.98004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
