3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

of functors, in which all non-vertical functors are fully faithful.

Remark 3.11
.

Similarly to Definition
3.10

3
one can define bicategories
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma,\phi)
and
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma,\phi)
; however, as the embeddings of super bundle gerbes and central simple super algebra bundles into super 2-line bundles are fully faithful (see Propositions
2.9
and
2.10
) these yield the same bicategory, i.e.,

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma,\phi)=\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma,\phi)=\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma,\phi).

We also generalize the framing of super 2-line bundles recalled in Section
2.2
from manifolds to graded Lie groupoids.

Definition 3.12
.

Let
(\Gamma,\phi)
be a graded Lie groupoid, and let
(\mathscr{L}_{1},\mathscr{P}_{1},\psi_{1})
and
(\mathscr{L}_{2},\mathscr{P}_{2},\psi_{2})

be super 2-line bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
. A
refinement
of super 2-line bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)

between them consists of the following data: a refinement
\mathscr{R}\colon\mathscr{L}_{1}\to\mathscr{L}_{2}

of super 2-line bundles over
\Gamma_{0}
together with a 2-isomorphism

\eta\colon\ \mathscr{P}_{2}\circ s^{*}F(\mathscr{R})\Rightarrow t^{*}F(\mathscr{R})^{\phi}\circ\mathscr{P}_{1}

over
\Gamma_{1}
, such that diagram (
3.7
) is commutative.
Here,
F
is the framing (
2.3
) of the bicategory of super 2-line bundles.

We denote the category of super 2-line bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
and refinements by
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Proposition 3.13
.

The bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
is framed under
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Proof.

The framing functor sends a refinement
(\mathscr{R},\eta)
to the 1-morphism
(F(\mathscr{R}),\eta)
. Combining
[
31
, Proposition 3.5.2]

and Remark
3.8
,
(\mathscr{R},\eta)
has a right adjoint in
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
.
∎

The framing restricts to all other seven bicategories in Definition
3.10
in a straightforward way.

Remark 3.14
.

The symmetric monoidal structure of super 2-line bundles described in
[
31
, Section 3.6]
canonically extends from manifolds to graded Lie groupoids.
This follows from an extension of the argument in
[
31
]
, noting that pullbacks and complex conjugation are compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure of super
2-line bundles over manifolds.

3.3
Functoriality

Graded Lie groupoids form a category whose morphisms are
even functors
, i.e., functors that strictly respect the grading.
Any even functor
F\colon(\Gamma,\phi)\to(\Gamma^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
clearly induces, via pullback, a 2-functor

F^{*}\colon\ \mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi).

This way, super 2-line bundles form a presheaf of bicategories over the category of graded Lie groupoids.
We prove in the next lemma that naturally isomorphic functors induce naturally equivalent pullback functors. In the ungraded case, this is

[
45
, Proposition 6.2]
.

Lemma 3.15
.

Suppose
F,G\colon(\Gamma,\phi)\to(\Gamma^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are even functors, and
\xi\colon F\Rightarrow G
is an even smooth natural transformation, i.e.,
\phi^{\prime}(\xi_{x})=1
for each
x\in\Gamma_{0}
. Then, there is a canonical natural equivalence
F^{*}\cong G^{*}
.

Proof.

Let
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
be a super 2-line bundle over
(\Gamma^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
. We construct a 1-isomorphism
(\mathscr{B},\eta)\colon F^{*}(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)\to G^{*}(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
. The 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{B}
over
\Gamma_{0}
is

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 29.8646pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.8646pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F_{0}^{*}\mathscr{L}=\xi^{*}s^{*}\mathscr{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.26735pt\raise 6.35265pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.99155pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\xi^{*}\mathscr{P}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.8646pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.8646pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\xi^{*}(t^{*}\mathscr{L})^{\phi^{\prime}\circ\xi}=G_{0}^{*}\mathscr{L},}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

and the 2-morphism
\eta\colon G^{*}\mathscr{P}\circ s^{*}\mathscr{B}\Rightarrow(t^{*}\mathscr{B})^{\phi}\circ F^{*}\mathscr{P}
is patched together from two applications of
\psi
. This defines the component of a natural equivalence
F^{*}\Rightarrow G^{*}
at the object
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
. It is straightforward to provide the component at a 1-morphism, and to complete the proof.
∎

If an even functor
F\colon(\Gamma,\phi)\to(\Gamma^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
between graded Lie groupoids is a strong equivalence (of the underlying Lie groupoids, see Appendix
A
), then any inverse functor
G\colon\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
is automatically even, and as a consequence of Lemma
3.15
,
F^{*}
and
G^{*}
establish an equivalence of bicategories

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
.

The above equivalence persists in fact when
F\colon\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
is only a
weak
equivalence (see Definition
A.1
). More precisely, we have the following result.

Theorem 3.16
.

Suppose
F\colon(\Gamma,\phi)\to(\Gamma^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
is an even functor between graded Lie groupoids, such that the underlying functor
F\colon\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
is a weak equivalence. Then,

F^{*}\colon\ \mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi).

is an equivalence of bicategories.

Proof.

For ungraded super 2-line bundles over Lie groupoids, this follows from Theorem
2.12
and a result of
[
45
]
recalled in Appendix
A
as Proposition
A.3
. Here, we generalize this to graded Lie groupoids.
By
[
45
, Proposition 5.7]
, the weak equivalence
F\colon\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
factors through another Lie groupoid
\Omega
, via a
strong
equivalence
S\colon\Gamma\to\Omega
(see Appendix
A
) and a
covering functor

T\colon\Omega\to\Gamma^{\prime}
, i.e.,
T
is smoothly fully faithful in the sense of Definition
A.1
and a surjective submersion on the level of objects. In this situation,
\Omega
becomes graded under
\omega:=\phi\circ S^{-1}
, in such a way that
S
and
T
are even functors. Pulling back along
S
and
S^{-1}
induces an equivalence
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Omega,\omega)
. It remains to show that
T^{*}
induces an equivalence
{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Omega,\omega)\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})}
.

To establish an inverse to
T^{*}
, we intend to use descent along
T
, and for this, we need to work on the fibre product Lie groupoids (recalled in Definition
A.9
)

\Omega^{[k]}:=\Omega\times_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\Omega\times_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\times\dots\times_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\Omega.

We require two
facts about covering functors. The first is that covering functors are not only surjective submersions on the level of objects, but also – automatically – surjective submersions on the level of morphisms. Therefore, the fibre products
\Omega^{[k]}
can be formed strictly, i.e., separately on the level of objects and on the level of morphisms. Second, the diagonal functors
{\Delta^{k}\colon\Omega\to\Omega^{[k]}}

are strong equivalences
[
45
, Lemma 8.1 (ii)]
. Let
\operatorname{pr}_{1},\operatorname{pr}_{2}\colon\Omega^{[2]}\to\Omega
be the projection functors. We have
\operatorname{pr}_{1}\circ\Delta^{2}=\operatorname{id}_{\Omega}=\operatorname{pr}_{2}\circ\Delta^{2}
. By
[
45
, Lemma 5.3]
, it follows that
\operatorname{pr}_{1}
and
\operatorname{pr}_{2}
are strong equivalences, too, and both inverses of
\Delta^{2}
; in particular, there is a natural isomorphism
{\operatorname{pr}_{1}\cong\operatorname{pr}_{2}}
.

We note that the fibre products
\Omega^{[k]}
are canonically graded. Indeed, if
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\in\Omega_{1}\times_{\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}}\Omega_{1}
is a morphism in
\Omega^{[2]}
, then we have, since
T
is even,

\omega(\alpha_{1})=\phi^{\prime}(T(\alpha_{1}))=\phi^{\prime}(T(\alpha_{2}))=\omega(\alpha_{2}),

so that the grading is unambiguously defined through either of the morphisms. Moreover, all projection functors
\Omega^{[k]}\to\Omega^{[l]}
and the diagonal functors
\Delta^{k}
are even, and the natural transformation
\operatorname{pr}_{1}\cong\operatorname{pr}_{2}
is even, too.

Now let
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
be a super 2-line bundle over
(\Omega,\omega)
. By Lemma
3.15
, there is a canonical 1-isomorphism

(\mathscr{B},\eta)\colon\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)\to\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)

over
\Omega^{[2]}
. Similarly, we obtain a canonical 2-isomorphism

\xi\colon\operatorname{pr}_{23}^{*}(\mathscr{B},\eta)\circ\operatorname{pr}_{12}^{*}(\mathscr{B},\eta)\Rightarrow\operatorname{pr}_{13}^{*}(\mathscr{B},\eta)

over
\Omega^{[3]}
, satisfying a coherence condition over 4-fold fibre products. This establishes a canonical descent structure on
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
, and it remains to perform the descent.

Separating structure,
\mathscr{L}
is a super 2-line bundle over
\Omega_{0}
,
\mathscr{B}\colon\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathscr{L}\to\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathscr{L}
is a 1-isomorphism on
\Omega_{0}\times_{\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}}\Omega_{0}
, and
\xi
is a 2-isomorphism on
\Omega_{0}\times_{\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}}\Omega_{0}\times_{\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}}\Omega_{0}
; hence, a descent object for the 2-stack of super 2-line bundles. Thus, by Theorem
2.12
, there exists a super 2-line bundle
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
on
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}
together with a 1-isomorphism
T_{0}^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}\cong\mathscr{L}
.
Next, we consider the 1-isomorphism
{\mathscr{P}\colon s^{*}\mathscr{L}\to(t^{*}\mathscr{L})^{\omega}}
over
\Omega_{1}
, and the 2-isomorphism

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 19.54521pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.80211pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}s^{*}\mathscr{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.94794pt\raise 6.35265pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.99155pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathscr{P}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.19107pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.54521pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{s^{*}\mathscr{B}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.61414pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 59.19107pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}(t^{*}\mathscr{L})^{\omega}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.11276pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t^{*}\mathscr{B}^{\omega}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.11276pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-17.80211pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}s^{*}\mathscr{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.3591pt\raise 0.9333pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.3591pt\raise-0.9333pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.49629pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.3591pt\raise 0.9333pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.3591pt\raise-0.9333pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.33151pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.3591pt\raise 0.9333pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.3591pt\raise-0.9333pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.3591pt\raise 0.9333pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.3591pt\raise-0.9333pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.2535pt\raise-38.35263pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.99155pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.80217pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 57.80217pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}(t^{*}\mathscr{L})^{\omega}.}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Together with the compatibility condition (
3.8
) between
\eta
and
\xi
, this means precisely that
\mathscr{P}
descends along
T_{1}
to a 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P^{\prime}}\colon s^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}\to(t^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime})^{\phi^{\prime}}
over
\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}
.
Finally, the compatibility condition (
3.7
) means that
\psi
descends to a 2-isomorphism

\psi^{\prime}\colon\ (\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathscr{P}^{\prime})^{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\phi^{\prime}}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\Rightarrow c^{*}\mathscr{P}^{\prime},

which then automatically satisfies the associativity condition (
3.4
). This shows that
(\mathscr{L}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
is a super 2-line bundle over
(\Gamma^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
. By construction, it is an essential preimage of
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
under the pullback functor
T^{*}
.

This proves that
T^{*}
is essentially surjective, which is the most important part of our theorem. Similarly, one proves that it is also fully faithful.
∎

Remark 3.17
.

Theorem
3.16
restricts to all sub-
sheaves
of
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}
that we have described in Definition
3.10
, namely, to
\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}
,
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}
, and
2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}
. It does not restrict to
\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}
and versions thereof.

3.4
Cohomological classification

If
\Gamma
is a Lie groupoid and
\mathcal{F}
is a sheaf of abelian groups on the category of smooth manifolds, one defines the Čech cohomology groups
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{k}(\Gamma,\mathcal{F})
using a semi-simplicial open cover
\mathcal{U}=\bigl(\mathcal{U}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
of the simplicial smooth manifold
N(\Gamma)
, i.e.,
\mathcal{U}^{k}=\bigl(U^{k}_{i}\bigr)_{i\in I^{k}}
is an open cover of
\Gamma_{k}
, and for each face map
f\colon\Gamma_{k}\to\Gamma_{l}
there is a map
\tilde{f}\colon I^{k}\to I^{l}
such that
f\bigl(U^{k}_{i}\bigr)\subseteq U^{l}_{\tilde{f}(i)}
.
Then, one can consider the double complex

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 22.95833pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\vdots}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 66.23616pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\vdots}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 120.65288pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-22.95833pt\raise-29.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\check{C}^{1}\bigl(\mathcal{U}^{0},\mathcal{F}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.33334pt\raise-34.39166pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.95833pt\raise-29.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.95833pt\raise-29.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\check{C}^{1}(\mathcal{U}^{1},\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.23616pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.90288pt\raise-29.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 116.90288pt\raise-29.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ldots}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-22.95833pt\raise-62.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\check{C}^{0}\bigl(\mathcal{U}^{0},\mathcal{F}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.63474pt\raise-45.75pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-37.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.33334pt\raise-67.89166pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.95833pt\raise-62.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.95833pt\raise-62.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\check{C}^{0}(\mathcal{U}^{1},\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 115.51399pt\raise-62.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.60143pt\raise-45.75pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.23616pt\raise-37.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 115.51399pt\raise-62.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ldots,}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

whose columns are the usual Čech complexes of the open covers
\mathcal{U}^{0}
,
\mathcal{U}^{1}
, etc., and the differential
{\Delta\colon\check{C}^{n}\bigl(\mathcal{U}^{l},\mathcal{F}\bigr)\to\check{C}^{n}\bigl(\mathcal{U}^{l+1},\mathcal{F}\bigr)}
in the rows is defined by

\Delta:=\sum_{p=0}^{l+1}(-1)^{p}\partial^{*}_{p},

where
\partial_{p}\colon\Gamma_{l+1}\to\Gamma_{l}
is the
p
-th face map. Explicitly,
\partial_{p}\colon\Gamma_{1}\to\Gamma_{0}
are
\partial_{0}=s
and
\partial_{1}=t
, and
{\partial_{p}\colon\Gamma_{l+1}\to\Gamma_{l}}
is given by

\partial_{p}(\gamma_{0},\dots,\gamma_{l}):=\begin{cases}(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{l}),&p=0,\\
(\gamma_{0},\dots,\gamma_{p-1}\circ\gamma_{p},\dots,\gamma_{l}),&0<p<l+1,\\
(\gamma_{0},\dots,\gamma_{l-1}),&p=l+1.\end{cases}

For instance,
\Delta|_{\check{C}^{n}(\mathcal{U}^{0},\mathcal{F})}=s^{*}-t^{*}
.
As usual, we form the total cohomology of this double complex with differential
D|_{\text{\v{C}}^{p}(\mathcal{U}^{q},\mathcal{F})}:=\delta+(-1)^{p}\Delta
.
There is a partial order on simplicial open covers, and the direct limit over the total cohomology of the above double complex is called the
Čech cohomology of the Lie groupoid
\Gamma
with values in
\mathcal{F}
and is denoted by
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{n}(\Gamma,\mathcal{F})
. We refer to
[
54
]
for more details and background on Lie groupoid cohomology. We note that the projection to the first column in the double complex induces a map
p_{0}\colon\check{\mathrm{H}}^{k}(\Gamma,\mathcal{F})\to\check{\mathrm{H}}^{k}(\Gamma_{0},\mathcal{F})
to the ordinary Čech cohomology of the manifold
\Gamma_{0}
. We also note the following special cases:

1.

If
X
is a smooth manifold, then
p_{0}\colon\check{\mathrm{H}}^{k}(X_{\rm dis},\mathcal{F})\to\check{\mathrm{H}}^{k}(X,\mathcal{F})
is an isomorphism
[
54
, Proposition 4.8]
.

2.

If
G
is a Lie group, and
\mathrm{B}G=\ast/\!\!/G
is the corresponding Lie groupoid,
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{k}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathcal{F})
is the (“Segal–Mitchison”, “Brylinski”) smooth group cohomology of the Lie group
G
;
this follows, e.g., by comparing the constructions above with those in
[
7
]
.

As usual, if
A
is an abelian Lie group, we will denote by the same letter the corresponding sheaf of
locally constant

A
-valued functions, and by
\underline{A}
the corresponding sheaf of
smooth

A
-valued functions.

Example 3.18
.

Let us examine the Čech cohomology
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{n}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})
in lowest degrees:

1.

A class in
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{0}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})
is represented by a family
\alpha=(\alpha_{i})_{i\in I^{0}}
of smooth maps
\alpha_{i}\colon U_{i}^{0}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. The cocycle condition
\delta\alpha=0
requires
\alpha_{i}=\alpha_{j}
on any intersection
U_{i}^{0}\cap U_{j}^{0}
of open sets of
\mathcal{U}^{0}
, and the cocycle condition
\Delta\alpha=0
requires, for each open set
U_{i}^{1}
of
\mathcal{U}^{1}
that
s^{*}\alpha_{s(i)}=t^{*}\alpha_{t(i)}
. In other words,
\alpha
is a sign for each connected component of
\Gamma_{0}
, with signs coinciding whenever there is a morphism between two components.

2.

A class in
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})
is represented by a pair
(\epsilon,\sigma)
of a family
\epsilon=(\epsilon_{ij})_{i,j\in I^{0}}
, with
\epsilon_{ij}\colon U_{i}^{0}\cap U_{j}^{0}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, and a family
\sigma=(\sigma_{i})_{i\in I^{1}}
with
\sigma_{i}\colon U_{i}^{1}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, satisfying three conditions:

•

\delta\epsilon=0
: the usual Čech cocycle condition

•

\delta\sigma-\Delta\epsilon=0
, i.e.,
s^{*}\epsilon_{s(i),s(j)}-t^{*}\epsilon_{t(i),t(j)}=\sigma_{j}-\sigma_{i}
on
U^{1}_{i}\cap U_{j}^{1}
,

•

\Delta\sigma=0
, i.e.,
\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\sigma_{\operatorname{pr}_{2}(i)}-c^{*}\sigma_{c(i)}+\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\sigma_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}(i)}=0
.

We note the following result, which is merely a corollary of its graded version (to be proved below as Theorem
3.22
), obtained by considering the case
\phi=1
there.

Proposition 3.19
.

Super
2
-line bundles over a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
are classified by the Čech cohomology of

\Gamma
, in such a way that there is a canonical bijection

\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma))\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}^{0}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(\Gamma,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt),

where
\mathrm{h}_{0}
denotes the set of isomorphism classes of objects.
Under this bijection, the subsets
\mathrm{h}_{0}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma)
and
\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma))
correspond to the subsets
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(\Gamma,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)
and
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{0}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times p_{0}^{-1}\bigl(\operatorname{Tor}\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(\Gamma_{0},\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)\bigr)
, respectively.

The reduction of this result to
\Gamma=X_{\rm dis}
reproduces Theorem
2.6
.

Our aim is now to extend Čech cohomology for Lie groupoids to graded Lie groupoids. For this purpose, we require that the sheaf
\mathcal{F}
is equipped with a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action by morphisms of sheaves of abelian groups. We denote this action by
\varepsilon\triangleright f
, for
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
and some
f\in\mathcal{F}(U)
.
Now we modify the setup described above in the following way. The row’s differential
\Delta
is replaced by a differential
\Delta^{\phi}\colon\check{C}^{n}\bigl(\mathcal{U}^{l},\mathcal{F}\bigr)\to\check{C}^{n}\bigl(\mathcal{U}^{l+1},\mathcal{F}\bigr)
defined by

\Delta^{\phi}:=\sum_{p=0}^{l+1}\varepsilon^{\phi}_{l+1,p}\triangleright(-1)^{p}\partial^{*}_{p},

where the function
\varepsilon^{\phi}_{l,p}\colon\Gamma_{l}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\{\pm 1\}
is defined for
l>0
and
0\leq p\leq l
by

\varepsilon^{\phi}_{l,p}(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{l}):=\begin{cases}1,&p<l,\\
\phi(\gamma_{l}),&p=l.\end{cases}

One can check that this satisfies
\Delta^{\phi}\circ\Delta^{\phi}=0
, and that
\Delta^{\phi}
commutes with (the unchanged) Čech coboundary operator
\delta
.
Thus, we obtain, via the same procedure as in the ungraded case, the
Čech cohomology
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{n}((\Gamma,\phi),\mathcal{F})
of the graded Lie groupoid
(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Remark 3.20
.

•

If the
\mathcal{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
-action on
\mathcal{F}
is the trivial one, then the Čech cohomology of the graded Lie groupoid
(\Gamma,\phi)
coincides with the one of the ungraded Lie groupoid
\Gamma
.

•

Since the modification for the grading
\phi
does not affect the Čech differential
\delta
, the projection
p_{0}\colon\check{\mathrm{H}}^{n}((\Gamma,\phi),\mathcal{F})\to\check{\mathrm{H}}^{n}(\Gamma_{0},\mathcal{F})
is still well-defined.

•

If
G
is a graded Lie group, i.e., a Lie group equipped with a homomorphism
{\varepsilon\colon G\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
, then
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{n}((\mathrm{B}G,\varepsilon),\mathcal{F})
defines a generalization of the (“Segal–Mitchison”, “Brylinski”) smooth group cohomology to graded Lie groups.

Example 3.21
.

Let us examine the relevant Čech cohomology group
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((\Gamma,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)
, where the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action on
\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt
is given by complex conjugation.
A class in
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((\Gamma,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)
is represented by a triple
(\mu,\eta,f)
consisting of a family
\mu=(\mu_{ijk})_{i,j,k\in I^{0}}
of smooth maps
\mu_{ijk}\colon U^{0}_{i}\cap U^{0}_{j}\cap U^{0}_{k}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, of a family
\eta=(\eta_{ij})_{i,j\in I^{1}}
of smooth maps
\eta_{ij}\colon U^{1}_{i}\cap U^{1}_{j}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, and of a family
f=(f_{i})_{i\in I^{2}}
of smooth maps
f_{i}\colon U^{2}_{i}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, satisfying the following four conditions:

•

\delta\mu=0
, the usual Čech cocycle condition.

•

\delta\eta+\Delta^{\phi}\mu=0
: for all
\gamma\in U^{1}_{i}\cap U^{1}_{j}\cap U^{1}_{k}
,

\mu_{s(i),s(j),s(k)}(s(\gamma))\cdot\eta_{jk}(\gamma)\cdot\eta_{ik}(\gamma)^{-1}\cdot\eta_{ij}(\gamma)=\phi(\gamma)\triangleright\mu_{t(i),t(j),t(k)}(t(\gamma)).

(3.9)

•

\delta f-\Delta^{\phi}\eta=0
: for all
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in U^{2}_{i}\cap U^{2}_{j}
,

\displaystyle f_{j}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\cdot f_{i}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})^{-1}\cdot\eta_{c(i),c(j)}(\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad=\phi(\gamma_{2})\triangleright\eta_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}(i),\operatorname{pr}_{1}(j)}(\gamma_{1})\cdot\eta_{\operatorname{pr}_{2}(i),\operatorname{pr}_{2}(j)}(\gamma_{2}).

(3.10)

•

\Delta^{\phi}f=0
: for
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})\in U_{i}^{3}
,

\displaystyle f_{\partial_{0}(i)}(\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})\cdot f_{\partial_{2}(i)}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\circ\gamma_{3})=f_{\partial_{1}(i)}(\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})\cdot\phi(\gamma_{3})\triangleright f_{\partial_{3}(i)}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}).

(3.11)

Under the usual procedure of classifying bundle gerbes by Čech cohomology (see, e.g.,
[
23
,
53
]
), one can construct from the Čech cocycle
\mu
a bundle gerbe
\mathscr{G}
over
\Gamma_{0}
. Moreover, one can construct from
\eta
– using (
3.9
) and that the complex conjugate cocycle constructs the complex conjugate bundle gerbe – a 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P}\colon s^{*}\mathscr{G}\to t^{*}\mathscr{G}^{\phi}
over
\Gamma_{1}
. Finally, one can construct from
f
– using (
3.10
) – a 2-isomorphism
\psi\colon(\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathscr{P})^{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\phi}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}\Rightarrow c^{*}\mathscr{P}
over
\Gamma_{2}
, and (
3.11
) implies an equality

(c\times\operatorname{id})^{*}\psi\bullet(\operatorname{pr}_{12}^{*}\psi^{\operatorname{pr}_{3}^{*}\phi}\circ\operatorname{id})=(\operatorname{id}\times c)^{*}\psi\bullet(\operatorname{id}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{23}^{*}\psi)

of 2-isomorphisms over
\Gamma_{3}
, which is nothing but the commutative diagram (
3.4
).
Summarizing, we have described the reconstruction of a bundle gerbe over
(\Gamma,\phi)
(see Definition
3.10
) from a cocycle. It is standard to show that this establishes a bijection

\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((\Gamma,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)\cong\mathrm{h}_{0}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma,\phi)
;
in other words,
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((\Gamma,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)
classifies bundle gerbes over
(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Theorem 3.22
.

Super
2
-line bundles over a graded Lie groupoid
(\Gamma,\phi)
are classified by the Čech cohomology of

(\Gamma,\phi)
, namely, there is a canonical bijection

\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi))\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}^{0}((\Gamma,\phi),\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}((\Gamma,\phi),\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((\Gamma,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt),

where the sheaves
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
are equipped with the trivial involution, and the sheaf
\underline{\mathbb{C}^{\times}}
is equipped with the involution given by complex conjugation. The subsets
\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma,\phi))
and
\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi))
correspond, respectively, to the subsets

\displaystyle\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}((\Gamma,\phi),\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((\Gamma,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)\qquad\text{and}\qquad

\displaystyle\check{\mathrm{H}}^{0}((\Gamma,\phi),\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}((\Gamma,\phi),\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times p_{0}^{-1}\bigl(\operatorname{Tor}\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(\Gamma_{0},\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)\bigr).

Here we recall, see Remark
3.20
, that
p_{0}^{-1}\bigl(\operatorname{Tor}\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(\Gamma_{0},\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)\bigr)
denotes classes in
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((\Gamma,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt))
whose image under
p_{0}
is torsion.

Proof.

The main point is that extracting local data from a super 2-line bundle
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
over
(\Gamma,\phi)
, as described for smooth manifolds in Remark
2.7
, yields the correct cocycle data, in particular, the correct signs for the grading
\phi
. Indeed, we collect the data
(\alpha_{i},\varepsilon_{ij},\mu_{ijk})
extracted there for an open cover
\mathcal{U}^{0}
of
\Gamma_{0}
. Next, we work with respect to an open cover
\mathcal{U}^{1}
on
\Gamma_{1}
. We first consider the restriction of
\mathscr{P}
to an open set
U^{1}_{i}
and consider the composite 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P}_{i}:=t^{*}\mathscr{N}_{t(i)}^{\phi}\circ\mathscr{P}|_{U_{i}^{1}}\circ s^{*}\mathscr{N}_{s(i)}\colon s^{*}\mathcal{A}_{s(i)}\to t^{*}\mathcal{A}_{t(i)}^{\phi}
. Since the inclusion of central simple algebra bundles into super 2-vector bundles is fully faithful (see Proposition
2.10
),
\mathscr{P}_{i}
can be identified with an invertible
\bigl(t^{*}\mathcal{A}^{\phi}_{t(i)}\bigr)
-
(s^{*}\mathcal{A}_{s(i)})
-bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}_{i}
. In particular, this implies
t^{*}\alpha_{t(i)}=s^{*}\alpha_{s(i)}
, providing the remaining cocycle condition in Example
3.18

1
; thus,
\alpha
gives the class
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{0}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})=\check{\mathrm{H}}^{0}((\Gamma,\phi),\mathbb{Z}_{2})
. Moreover, since, by construction, the algebras
A_{i}
are fixed representatives of the Morita equivalence classes (see Remark
2.7
), we have
A_{s(i)}=A_{t(i)}^{\phi_{i}}
, where
\phi_{i}
is the value of
\phi
over
U_{i}^{1}
.
We can assume that the bimodule bundle
\mathcal{P}_{i}
is trivializable,
\mathcal{P}_{i}\cong U_{i}^{1}\times P_{i}
, where
P_{i}
is an invertible
\tilde{A}_{i}
-
\tilde{A}_{i}
-bimodule, where
\tilde{A}_{i}:=A_{t(i)}^{\phi_{i}}=A_{s(i)}
for any
i\in I^{1}
. Thus,
P_{i}\cong-\sigma_{i}\tilde{A}_{i}
, for a locally constant map
\sigma_{i}\colon U^{1}_{i}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, where
-A
stands for
\Pi A
, the grading reversal, see Remark
2.7
.
On a double overlap
U^{1}_{ij}:=U_{i}^{1}\cap U^{1}_{j}
, we observe that the intertwiner
\phi
of the 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P}
(see Definition
2.3
) induces an invertible intertwiner

\phi_{ij}\colon\ \mathcal{P}_{j}\circ s^{*}\mathcal{M}_{s(i),s(j)}\to t^{*}\mathcal{M}_{t(i),t(j)}^{\phi_{i}}\circ\mathcal{P}_{i},

where
\mathcal{M}_{ij}
is the invertible bimodule bundle over
U^{1}_{ij}
constructed in Remark
2.7
. Under the trivializations
\mathcal{P}_{i}\cong U_{i}^{1}\times P_{i}
and
\mathcal{M}_{ij}\cong U^{0}_{ij}\times M_{ij}
(where
M_{ij}
is an
A
-
A
-bimodule, for
A=A_{i}=A_{j}
), we obtain two consequences:

1.

We obtain an invertible intertwiner between the typical fibres,

M_{t(i),t(j)}^{\phi_{i}}\otimes_{\tilde{A}_{i}}P_{i}\cong P_{j}\otimes_{\tilde{A}_{j}}M_{s(i),s(j)}.

Using the isomorphisms
P_{i}\cong-\sigma_{i}\tilde{A}_{i}
and
M_{ij}\cong\varepsilon_{ij}A_{i}=\varepsilon_{ij}A_{j}
and the equality
\tilde{A}_{i}=A_{s(i)}
, this shows an equality
\varepsilon_{t(i),t(j)}-\sigma_{i}=-\sigma_{j}+\varepsilon_{s(i),s(j)}
, which is the cocycle condition
\delta\sigma-\Delta\epsilon
of
2
.

2.

Under above trivializations, the intertwiner
\phi_{ij}
becomes a smooth map
\eta_{ij}\colon U^{1}_{ij}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. The commutative diagram (
2.1
) for
\phi
then shows precisely condition (
3.9
), i.e.,
\delta\eta+\Delta^{\phi}\mu=0
.

Finally, we work with a cover
\mathcal{U}^{2}
of
\Gamma_{2}
, and there on an open set
U^{2}_{i}
. The 2-isomorphism
\psi
induces an isomorphism

(\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}(i)})^{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\phi_{i}}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{pr}_{2}(i)}\to c^{*}\mathcal{P}_{c(i)}.

Under the trivializations
P_{i}\cong-\sigma_{i}\tilde{A}_{i}
, it becomes an equality

\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\sigma_{\operatorname{pr}_{1}(i)}+\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\sigma_{\operatorname{pr}_{2}(i)}=c^{*}\sigma_{c(i)},

which provides the cocycle condition
\Delta\sigma=0
of Example
3.18

2
; thus showing that
(\varepsilon,\sigma)
defines a class in
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})=\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}((\Gamma,\phi),\mathbb{Z}_{2})
. Moreover, we obtain a smooth map
f_{i}\colon U_{i}^{2}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
satisfying (
3.10
). Finally, the commutativity of (
3.4
) implies the remaining cocycle condition
{\Delta^{\phi}f=0}
, showing that
(\mu,\eta,f)
defines a class in
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((\Gamma,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)
.

It is straightforward to generalize the reconstruction procedure explained in Example
3.21
for (ungraded) bundle gerbes to super 2-line bundles, and moreover, to show that this is inverse to the above extraction of local data.
∎

4
Twistings and twisted vector bundles

The following is the central definition and conveys the main point of this article.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
(\Gamma,\phi)
be a graded Lie groupoid. A
twisting
on
(\Gamma,\phi)
is a super 2-line bundle
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
over
(\Gamma,\phi)
in the sense of Definition
3.3
.

The following is the corresponding, canonical definition of a category of twisted vector bundles; these constitute the ingredients of the twisted K-groups belonging to the twistings of Definition
4.1
.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
be a twisting on
(\Gamma,\phi)
. An
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
-twisted super vector bundle
is a 1-morphism
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)\to(\mathscr{I},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})
in the bicategory
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
. Twisted super vector bundles form the category

\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)}(\Gamma,\phi):=\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)}((\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi),(\mathscr{I},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})).

Remark 4.3
.

Unwinding Definition
4.2
, an
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
-twisted super vector bundle is a 1-morphism of super 2-line bundles

\mathscr{B}\colon\mathscr{L}\to\mathscr{I}

over
\Gamma_{0}
together with a 2-isomorphism of super 2-line bundles
{\eta\colon s^{*}\mathscr{B}\Rightarrow(t^{*}\mathscr{B})^{\phi}\circ\mathscr{P}}

over
\Gamma_{1}
, such that the diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 118.46857pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern-26.577pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{B}^{\phi(\gamma_{2})}_{t(\gamma_{2})}\circ\mathscr{P}_{\gamma_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.24681pt\raise-41.0128pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.13391pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta_{\gamma_{1}}^{\phi(\gamma_{2})}\circ\operatorname{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.57706pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 66.57706pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{B}_{t(\gamma_{1})}^{\phi(\gamma_{1})\phi(\gamma_{2})}\circ\mathscr{P}_{\gamma_{1}}^{\phi(\gamma_{2})}\circ\mathscr{P}_{\gamma_{2}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

is commutative for all
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\Gamma_{2}
. If
\mathscr{L}=\mathscr{I}
is the trivial 2-line bundle, then
\mathscr{B}
is an ordinary super vector bundle.
If the twisting is completely trivial
(\mathscr{I},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})
, we obtain a category
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi):=\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{(\mathscr{I},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id})}(\Gamma,\phi)
of (untwisted) super vector bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
.

The purpose of this paper is to show that the following list of versions of twistings that have appeared in the literature consists of special cases of Definitions
4.1
and
4.2
.

1.

Central simple super algebra bundles
[
13
,
28
]
.
Central simple super algebra bundles for the field
\mathbb{C}
over a smooth manifold
M
are a special case of Definition
4.1
, see Proposition
2.10
:

\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathbb{C}}(M)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(M)\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(M_{\rm dis})=\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(M_{\rm dis},1).

The twisted vector bundles for a central simple super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
are precisely the super
\mathcal{A}
-module bundles over
M
.

2.

Bundle gerbes
[
42
]
.
Bundle gerbes over a smooth manifold
M
are a special case of Definition
4.1
, as

\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(M)\cong 2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(M)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(M)\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(M_{\rm dis})=\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(M_{\rm dis},1),

see Propositions
2.9
and Remark
3.9
. The twisted vector bundles are precisely the bundle gerbe modules.
Their role as twistings for K-theory has been described in

[
4
]
. Bundle gerbes are of course just a special case of the next item,
3
.

3.

Equivariant bundle gerbes
[
44
,
45
]
.
Equivariant bundle gerbes over Lie groupoids
\Gamma
are defined by the canonical homotopy limit construction outlined in (
3.1
), and are included as

\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma)\cong 2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma)=\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,1),

see Definition
3.10
and Remark
3.9
.
A similar construction of equivariant bundle gerbes can be found in
[
43
]
. Precursors have appeared for the case when
\Gamma
is an action groupoid, e.g., in
[
22
,
23
,
35
]
.

4.

Chern–Simons twistings
[
16
]
.
Every multiplicative bundle gerbe over a Lie group
G
is also
G
-equivariant with respect to the conjugation action of
G
on itself; this makes them a subclass of equivariant bundle gerbes and a special case of
3
. The twisted vector bundles of Definition
4.2
form (for a finite group
G
) the modular tensor category that belongs to the corresponding Chern–Simons theory (i.e., Dijkgraaf–Witten theory). We discuss this in detail in Section
5.5
.

5.

Jandl gerbes
[
50
]
.
Jandl gerbes live over a real manifold, i.e., a smooth manifold
M
with an involution
\sigma\colon M\to M
. They are the same as bundle gerbes
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{P},\psi)
over the corresponding graded groupoid
\mathrm{Gr}(M,\sigma)
(see
4
); thus,

\mathscr{J}\mathrm{dl}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(M,\sigma)\cong\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\mathrm{Gr}(M,\sigma))=2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\mathrm{Gr}(M,\sigma))\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\mathrm{Gr}(M,\sigma)).

This is a special case of
8
, which is investigated thoroughly in Section
5.1
.

6.

Real gerbes of Gomi–Thiang
[
25
]
.
These are Jandl gerbes
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{P},\psi)
where the surjective submersion of
\mathscr{G}
is an open cover to which the involution lifts. This is a subclass of
5
.

7.

Real bundle gerbes of Hekmati–Murray–Stevenson–Vozzo
[
26
]
.
These are Jandl gerbes
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{P},\psi)
where the 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P}
is (induced from) a refinement (see Definition
2.16
). This is another subclass of
5
.

8.

Equivariant Jandl gerbes
[
23
]
.
Equivariant Jandl gerbes live over a smooth manifold
M
with an action of a finite graded group
(G,\varepsilon)
, i.e., a group
G
with a homomorphism
\varepsilon\colon G\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, and we prove in Section
5.1
that they are the same as bundle gerbes over the corresponding graded action groupoid
{M/\!\!/(G,\varepsilon)}
(see Example
3.2

3
),

\mathrm{GSW}_{M}(G,\varepsilon)\cong\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(M/\!\!/(G,\varepsilon))\cong 2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(M/\!\!/(G,\varepsilon))\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(M/\!\!/(G,\varepsilon)).

For
(G,\varepsilon)=(\mathbb{Z}_{2},\operatorname{id})
, this is
5
. For arbitrary
G
and
\varepsilon=1
, it is a special case of
3
.

9.

Moutuou’s RG bundle gerbes
[
41
]
.
These are a super version of
7
; by a straightforward generalization of the discussion of
8
in Section
5.1
, they embed into super 2-line bundles over

\mathrm{Gr}(M,\sigma)
.

10.

Twistings of Freed–Hopkins–Teleman
[
17
]
.
Freed–Hopkins–Teleman’s twistings live over (ungraded) Lie groupoids
\Gamma
, and are the same as (equivariant) super bundle gerbes,

\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)\cong\mathrm{h}_{1}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma))\subseteq\mathrm{h}_{1}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma))
.
This needs again more elaboration, which is carried out in Section
5.3
.

11.

Twistings of Ángel–Gómez–Uribe
[
1
]
.
These live over action groupoids
X/\!\!/G
, and are a special
case of
10
.

12.

Freed–Moore’s twisted groupoid extensions
[
20
]
.
These live over graded Lie groupoids
(\Gamma,\phi)
, and they are the same as super 2-line bundles
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
over
(\Gamma,\phi)
whose underlying super 2-line bundle
\mathscr{L}
is trivial; while the morphisms correspond to refinements

\mathrm{FM}(\Gamma,\phi)\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\text{ref}}(\Gamma,\phi)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
.
This needs more elaboration, which is carried out in Section
5.2
. The twisted vector bundles of
[
20
]
coincide precisely with our Definition
4.2
.

13.

Magnetic equivariant vector bundles of Serrano–Uribe–Xicotencatl
[
51
]
.
These are a special case of our twisted vector bundles from Definition
4.2
, reduced to graded Lie groupoids induced from graded Lie groups (see Example
3.2

2
), and reduced to trivial twistings. This is a special case of
12
.

14.

Moutuou’s real twistings
[
41
]
.
Moutuou’s real twistings live over real groupoids
(\Gamma,\tau)
, i.e., Lie groupoids with an involutive functor
\tau
. They can be viewed as a real analogue of Freed–Hopkins–Teleman’s twistings of
12
. Indeed, associated to any real groupoid
(\Gamma,\tau)
is a graded Lie groupoid
\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)
, and every
real twisting over
(\Gamma,\tau)
gives rise to a super 2-line bundle over
\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)
. This is discussed thoroughly in Section
5.4
.

15.

Berwick–Evans–Guo real twistings
[
3
]
.
These twistings are defined similarly to Moutuou’s real twistings of
14
but using topological stacks instead of topological groupoids. A comparison between both approaches can be found in
[
3
, Sections 3.4 and 3.5]
.

16.

Freed’s twisted invertible algebra bundles
[
15
]
.
These live over Lie groupoids
\Gamma
, and involve a certain “double cover” by a graded Lie groupoid. Under the assumption that the grading of the double cover is induced by a grading
\phi
on the Lie groupoid
\Gamma
,
Freed’s twisted invertible algebra bundles can be identified with super 2-line bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
. This is discussed in detail in Section
5.6
.

17.

Orientifold twistings of Distler–Freed–Moore
[
11
,
12
,
14
]
.
Distler–Freed–Moore’s orientifold twistings are similar to
16
, but feature instead of algebra bundles a certain
\mathbb{Z}
-valued grading function
d
. Under the same limitation as in
16
,
these twistings yield super 2-line bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
. This is discussed in detail in Section
5.7
.

18.

Gomi–Freed–Moore twistings
[
24
]
.
These live over graded groupoids
(\Gamma,\phi)
, and they are the same as the Distler–Freed–Moore-twistings of
17

with two constraints: first, they satisfy our assumption that the grading of the double cover is induced from one on the base, and second, that the grading function
d
is trivial. This is a special case of

17
, and yields all super bundle gerbes over
(\Gamma,\phi)
.

5
Comparison work

In this section, we carry out the comparison work announced in Section
4
.

5.1
Equivariant Jandl gerbes

The paper
[
23
]
introduced a general theory for B-fields in unoriented orbifold sigma models. The B-field is modelled by a so-called equivariant Jandl gerbe, combining an orbifolding process with the treatment of unoriented worldsheets. The following notion was introduced.

Definition 5.1
.

An
orientifold group
(
G,\varepsilon
) for a smooth manifold
X
is a finite group
G
acting smoothly from the left on
X
,
together with a group homomorphism
\varepsilon\colon G\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

This embeds into the more general notion of graded Lie groupoids (see Definition
3.1
) by associating to an orientifold group
(G,\varepsilon)
the action groupoid
X/\!\!/G
together with the map
\phi_{\varepsilon}\colon G\times X\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
,
(g,x)\mapsto\varepsilon(g)
.
We prove in Theorem
5.6
below that the equivariant Jandl gerbes of
[
23
]
for an orientifold group
(G,\varepsilon)
are the same as bundle gerbes over the graded Lie groupoid
(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})
, and hence, in particular, 2-line bundles over that groupoid.

For the geometric part, we consider the bicategory
\mathrm{h}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X)
of hermitian bundle gerbes over
X
, and therein the bigroupoid
\mathrm{h}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)
that contains only the invertible 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms. Forgetting the hermitian metrics yields the vertical functors in the following commutative diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.23615pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.23615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.23615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.23615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.09727pt\raise-24.46225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-23.45836pt\raise-32.46225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.61812pt\raise-32.46225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.61812pt\raise-32.46225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X).\hskip-60.00009pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

In
[
23
]
, the following constructions are made. Suppose
\mathscr{G}=(\pi,\mathcal{M},\mu)
is a hermitian bundle gerbe, i.e.,
\mathcal{M}
is a hermitian line bundle and
\mu
is an unitary bundle isomorphism. Then,
\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}:=\bigl(\pi,\mathcal{M}^{*},\mu^{tr-1}\bigr)
denotes the (usual) dual bundle gerbe:
\mathcal{M}^{*}
is the dual line bundle, equipped with the dual metric, and
\mu^{tr-1}
is the inverse of the transpose of
\mu
. If
\mathscr{A}=(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)\colon\mathscr{G}_{1}\to\mathscr{G}_{2}
is a hermitian bundle gerbe isomorphism, then we set
\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}:=\bigl(\zeta,\mathcal{P}^{*},\phi^{tr-1}\bigr)\colon\mathscr{G}_{1}^{\dagger}\to\mathscr{G}_{2}^{\dagger}
. Thus,
\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}
has the same surjective submersion
\zeta\colon Z\to Y_{1}\times_{X}Y_{2}
and the dual hermitian line bundle
\mathcal{P}^{*}
. We note that
\phi
is a unitary bundle isomorphism, over a point
(z,z^{\prime})\in Z^{[2]}
with
\zeta(z)=:(y_{1},y_{2})
and
\zeta(z^{\prime})=:(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime})
,

\phi_{z,z^{\prime}}\colon\ \mathcal{P}_{z^{\prime}}\otimes(\mathcal{M}_{1})_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}\to(\mathcal{M}_{2})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{z}.

Up to a reordering of tensor factors, this coincides with the conventions of
[
23
]
. Thus,
\phi^{tr-1}
is an isomorphism

\phi_{z,z^{\prime}}^{tr-1}\colon\ \mathcal{P}^{*}_{z^{\prime}}\otimes(\mathcal{M}_{1}^{*})_{y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}}\to(\mathcal{M}_{2}^{*})_{y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{z}^{*},

and one checks that it satisfies the required coherence property so that
\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}
is well-defined. Finally, if
\beta\colon\mathscr{A}_{1}\Rightarrow\mathscr{A}_{2}
is a 2-isomorphism, then
\beta^{\dagger}:=\beta^{tr-1}\colon\mathscr{A}_{1}^{\dagger}\Rightarrow\mathscr{A}_{2}^{\dagger}
is a new 2-isomorphism. These constructions define a 2-functor

(..)^{\dagger}\colon\ \mathrm{h}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)\to\mathrm{h}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)

that is involutive under a canonical natural isomorphism
(..)^{\dagger\dagger}\cong\operatorname{id}
.

The definitions in
[
23
]
are based on the 2-functor
(..)^{\dagger}
. Before we recall those, we want to relate
(..)^{\dagger}
to our complex conjugation functor
\overline{(..)}
of (
2.4
).

Lemma 5.2
.

The two
2
-functors

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 79.00696pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-79.00696pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)\to\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)\subseteq 2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.50696pt\raise 5.83888pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{(..)}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 103.00696pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 103.00696pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

and

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 26.23615pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.23615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.93056pt\raise 7.12001pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.37001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(..)^{\dagger}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.23615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.23615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)\to\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)\subseteq 2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X)}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

are canonically naturally isomorphic. In short: upon forgetting the hermitian metrics, the functor
(..)^{\dagger}
is complex conjugation.

Proof.

We recall that an inner product
\left\langle-,-\right\rangle
on a finite-dimensional complex vector space
V
(antilinear in the
first
component) induces an isomorphism

\overline{V}\to V^{*}\colon\ v\mapsto\left\langle v,-\right\rangle.

(5.1)

Moreover,
\overline{V}
is equipped with an inner product
\overline{\left\langle-,-\right\rangle}
, given by the complex conjugate of the original one. Also,
V^{*}
is equipped with an inner product, which is usually induced from
\overline{V}
along the isomorphism (
5.1
), so that (
5.1
) actually becomes an
isometric
isomorphism. Moreover, for an isometric isomorphism
\varphi\colon V\to W
, the relevant diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.00002pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.00002pt\raise-15.69293pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-25.2081pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.8889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.8889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.21529pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{W^{*}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(5.2)

is commutative. The isometric isomorphism (
5.1
) extends to hermitian vector bundles, and a diagram analogous to (
5.2
) exists for unitary isomorphisms between hermitian vector bundles.

If
\mathscr{G}=(\pi,\mathcal{M},\mu)
is a hermitian bundle gerbe, then we obtain a canonical isomorphism
\eta_{\mathscr{G}}\colon\overline{\mathscr{G}}\to\mathscr{G}^{\dagger}
, which is the refinement consisting of the identity
\operatorname{id}\colon Y\to Y
and the unitary line bundle isomorphism
u_{\mathcal{M}}\colon\overline{\mathcal{M}}\to\mathcal{M}^{*}
. The commutativity of (
5.2
) shows the one of the relevant diagram (
2.2
).

If
\mathscr{A}\colon\mathscr{G}_{1}\to\mathscr{G}_{2}
is a 1-isomorphism, then we construct a 2-isomorphism

We have to compute the two composites. Suppose
\mathscr{A}=(\zeta,\mathcal{P},\phi)
, so that
\overline{\mathscr{A}}=\bigl(\zeta,\overline{\mathcal{P}},\overline{\phi}\bigr)
and

. Then, we get
\mathscr{A}^{\dagger}\circ\eta_{\mathscr{G}_{1}}=\bigl(\zeta,\mathcal{P}^{*},\phi^{tr-1}\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes u_{\mathcal{M}_{1}})\bigr)

as well as

The claimed 2-morphism is given by

which is commutative because all subdiagrams are commutative: the triangular diagrams because
(
5.1
) is compatible with tensor products, and the one in the middle is (
5.2
). One can now check that
\eta_{\mathscr{G}}
and
\eta_{\mathscr{A}}
are the components of a natural isomorphism
\eta\colon\overline{(..)}|_{\mathrm{h}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}^{\times}(X)}\Rightarrow(..)^{\dagger}
.
∎

By Lemma
5.2
, we can replace occurrences of
(..)^{\dagger}
in
[
23
]
consistently by complex conjugation. Next we recall the following definition from
[
23
]
. Given an orientifold group
(G,\varepsilon)
and a bundle gerbe
\mathscr{G}
over
X
, we set for
g\in G

g\mathscr{G}:=\bigl(g^{-1}\bigr)^{*}\mathscr{G}^{\varepsilon(g)}
,
where
g^{-1}\colon X\to X
is the diffeomorphism assigned to
g^{-1}
via the action of
G
on
X
, and

(..)^{\varepsilon(g)}
refers to the convention fixed in Section
3.2
, i.e., it means nothing when
\varepsilon(g)=1
and it means complex conjugation when
\varepsilon(g)=-1
.
For a 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{A}\colon\mathscr{G}\to\mathscr{H}
between bundle gerbes, we define

g\mathscr{A}:=\bigl(g^{-1}\bigr)^{*}\mathscr{A}^{\varepsilon(g)}\colon\ g\mathscr{G}\to g\mathscr{H}.

Finally, for a 2-isomorphism
\beta\colon\mathscr{A}\Rightarrow\mathscr{A}^{\prime}
, we define

g\beta:=\bigl(g^{-1}\bigr)^{*}\beta^{\varepsilon(g)}\colon\ g\mathscr{A}\Rightarrow g\mathscr{A}^{\prime}.

These definitions promote each group element
g\in G
to a functor
F_{g}\colon\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X)\to\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X)
that is covariant at all levels and satisfies
F_{g_{2}}\circ F_{g_{1}}\cong F_{g_{2}g_{1}}
. The following is
[
23
, Definition 2.9]
.

.

(G,\varepsilon)
be an orientifold group for

and let

be a bundle gerbe over

.

consists of 1-isomorphisms

0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 67.12996pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 124.84152pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-32.40428pt\raise-65.28003pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern of 2-isomorphisms is commutative for all
g_{1},g_{2}\in G
.

We also recall the notion of 2-morphisms from
[
23
]
.

Definition 5.5
.

Suppose we have two
(G,\varepsilon)
-equivariant bundle gerbes
\bigl(\mathscr{G}^{1},\mathscr{A}^{1},\psi^{1}\bigr)
and
\bigl(\mathscr{G}^{2},\mathscr{A}^{2},\allowbreak\psi^{2}\bigr)

and two equivariant 1-morphisms
(\mathscr{B},\eta)
and
(\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})
between them. A
(G,\varepsilon)
-equivariant
2
-morphism

(\mathscr{B},\eta)\Rightarrow(\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})

is a 2-morphism
\xi\colon\mathscr{B}\Rightarrow\mathscr{B}^{\prime}
which is compatible with the 2-isomorphisms
\eta_{g}
and
\eta_{g}^{\prime}
in the sense that the diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 89.60562pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern-19.08493pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{g\mathscr{B}^{\prime}\circ\mathscr{A}^{1}_{g}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.93369pt\raise-39.52391pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.9117pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta^{\prime}_{g}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.08493pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 43.08493pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 73.08493pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{A}^{2}_{g}\circ\mathscr{B}^{\prime}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}

of 2-morphisms commutes for all
g\in G
.

(G,\varepsilon)
-equivariant bundle gerbes in the sense of
[
23
]
form a bicategory that we denote here for one moment by
\mathrm{GSW}_{X}(G,\varepsilon)
.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
(G,\varepsilon)
be an orientifold group for a smooth manifold
X
, and let
(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})
be the associated graded Lie groupoid.
The bicategory of
(G,\varepsilon)
-equivariant bundle gerbes of
[
23
]
is equivalent to the bicategory of bundle gerbes over
(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})
,

\mathrm{GSW}_{X}(G,\varepsilon)\cong\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})\cong 2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon}).

Proof.

The proof consists mainly of a conversion between the different conventions.
If
\mathscr{G}
is a bundle gerbe over
X
, and
(\mathscr{A}_{g},\varphi_{g_{1},g_{2}})
is a
(G,\varepsilon)
-equivariant structure on
\mathscr{G}
in the sense of Definition
5.3
, then, using that
G
is finite,

\mathscr{L}:=\mathscr{G},\qquad\mathscr{P}|_{\{g\}\times X}:=\mathscr{A}_{g^{-1}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\psi|_{\{g_{1}\}\times\{g_{2}\}\times X}:=\varphi_{g_{2}^{-1},g_{1}^{-1}}

is a bundle gerbe over
(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})
in the sense of Definition
3.10
.
Next, if
(\mathscr{B},\eta_{g})\colon\bigl(\mathscr{G}^{1},\mathscr{A}^{1},\varphi^{1}\bigr)\to\bigl(\mathscr{G}^{2},\mathscr{A}^{2},\varphi^{2}\bigr)
is a
(G,\varepsilon)
-equivariant 1-morphism, then

\mathscr{B}
,
\eta|_{\{g\}\times X}:=\eta_{g^{-1}}^{-1}

is a 1-morphism of the associated bundle gerbes
\bigl(\mathscr{L}^{1},\mathscr{P}^{1},\psi^{1}\bigr)\to\bigl(\mathscr{L}^{2},\mathscr{P}^{2},\psi^{2}\bigr)
over
(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})

in the sense of Definition
3.10
. Similarly, if
\xi\colon(\mathscr{B},\eta_{g})\to(\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\eta_{g}^{\prime})
is a
(G,\varepsilon)
-equivariant 2-morphism,
then
\xi\colon(\mathscr{B},\eta)\to(\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})
is a 2-morphism of the associated 1-morphisms of bundle gerbes over
(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})

in the sense of Definition
3.10
. This defines a functor
\mathrm{GSW}_{X}(G,\varepsilon)\to\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})
which is now easily checked to be an equivalence.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

[
23
, Proposition 3.1]
gives a cohomological classification of equivariant Jandl gerbes (with hermitian metrics and connections), by a “twisted-equivariant” version of smooth Deligne cohomology. Stripping off the connection data, the cocycle data for an equivariant Jandl gerbe satisfies conditions (3.3), (3.8), (3.10) and (3.11) in
[
23
]
. Under slight change of conventions, one can check that these coincide with the conditions for a 2-cocycle in
\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon}),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)
listed in Example
3.21
. Indeed, under the equivalence of Theorem
5.6
, Example
3.21
is precisely the classification of equivariant Jandl gerbes (without metrics and connections).

Remark 5.8
.

Descent for equivariant Jandl gerbes has already been considered in
[
23
]
.
We consider an orientifold group
(G,\varepsilon)
for a smooth manifold
X
, in such a way that the normal subgroup
G^{0}:=\mathrm{ker}(\epsilon)
acts without fixed points. Thus, the quotient
X^{\prime}:=X/G^{0}
is again a smooth manifold, for which the remaining group
G^{\prime}:=G/G^{0}
together with its induced grading
{\epsilon^{\prime}\colon G^{\prime}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
is an orientifold group. Then,
[
23
, Theorem 3.1]
proves an equivalence

\mathrm{GSW}_{X}(G,\epsilon)\cong\mathrm{GSW}_{X^{\prime}}\bigl(G^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime}\bigr).

(5.3)

In our current framework, this result follows from Theorem
3.16
and our equivalence of Theorem
5.6
.
To see this, we consider the graded Lie groupoids
(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})
and
(X^{\prime}/\!\!/G^{\prime},\phi_{\varepsilon^{\prime}})
, and the obvious projection functor

(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})\to(X^{\prime}/\!\!/G^{\prime},\phi_{\varepsilon^{\prime}})
.
This functor is even and one can easily prove that it is a weak equivalence, in fact, a covering functor. Hence, Theorem
3.16
implies the equivalence (
5.3
).

5.2
Freed–Moore’s twisted groupoid extensions

Freed and Moore introduced in
[
20
]
so-called twisted groupoid extensions to describe symmetry protected phases in condensed matter physics.
We show (see Theorem
5.15
) that Freed and Moore’s twisted groupoid extensions are the same as
graded-equivariant super 2-line bundles whose underlying 2-line bundle is trivial; hence, they are particular graded-equivariant super 2-line bundles.

We recall that we denote by
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
the complex conjugate of a complex line bundle
\mathcal{L}
.
We fix the following notation. Let
\mathcal{L}
be a line bundle and
\phi=\pm 1
. Then,

\mathcal{L}^{\phi}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{L},&\phi=+1,\\
\overline{\mathcal{L}},&\phi=-1.\end{cases}

(5.4)

Definition 5.9
.

Let
(\Gamma,\phi)
be a graded Lie groupoid.
A
\phi
-twisted super extension of
\Gamma
is a pair
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
consisting
of a super line bundle
\mathcal{L}
over
\Gamma_{1}
together with an isomorphism

\lambda\colon\ \operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\phi}\otimes\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L}\to c^{*}\mathcal{L}

of super line bundles over
\Gamma_{2}
, such that the diagram

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}}^{\phi(\gamma_{2}\circ\gamma_{3})}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{2}\circ\gamma_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-66.09792pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 62.98729pt\raise-66.09792pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2}\circ\gamma_{3}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}\end{gathered}

(5.5)

commutes for each composable triple of morphisms
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})\in\Gamma_{3}
.

Definition
5.9
coincides with
[
20
, Definition 7.23 (iii)]
under the dropping of hermitian structures and some notational reformulations. First, our notion of a graded Lie groupoid coincides with Freed–Moore’s notion of a “groupoid with a homomorphism to
{\pm 1}
”.
Second, our super line bundle
\mathcal{L}
is described there as a line bundle with a map
\vartheta\colon\Gamma_{1}\to\{\pm 1\}
.
Third, our notation
\mathcal{L}^{\phi}
(defined in (
5.4
)) coincides with Freed–Moore’s notation
{}^{\phi}\mathcal{L}
defined in
[
20
, equation (7.9)]
.
Then, the isomorphism
\lambda
is exactly as in
[
20
, equation (7.10)]
, and the diagram is precisely
[
20
, equation (7.11)]
.

Example 5.10
.

For any graded Lie groupoid
(\Gamma,\phi)
, there is a trivial
\phi
-twisted super extension
I_{\Gamma,\phi}
consisting of the trivial line bundle
\mathbb{C}\times\Gamma_{1}
, considered as purely even, and of the isomorphism
\lambda_{triv}
given in each fibre by
\mathbb{C}^{\phi}\otimes\mathbb{C}\cong\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}
, using the canonical isomorphism
\mathbb{C}\cong\overline{\mathbb{C}}
over points with
\phi=-1
.

Example 5.11
.

Let
(G,\varepsilon)
be a graded Lie group (see Example
3.2

2
); we consider the associated graded groupoid
(\mathrm{B}G,\phi)
with
\phi:=\varepsilon
. A

\phi
-twisted super extension of
\mathrm{B}G
is the same as a group extension

1\longrightarrow\operatorname{U}(1)\longrightarrow\hat{G}\longrightarrow G\longrightarrow 1
,
which for all
\hat{g}\in\hat{G}
and
\lambda\in U(1)
satisfies

\hat{g}\lambda=\begin{cases}\lambda\hat{g},\quad\phi(\hat{g})=+1,\\
\overline{\lambda}\hat{g},\quad\phi(\hat{g})=-1\end{cases}

together with a Lie group homomorphism
\vartheta\colon G\to\{\pm 1\}
.
The trivial
\phi
-twisted super extension
I_{\mathrm{B}G,\phi}
corresponds to the semi-direct product
G\ltimes\operatorname{U}(1)
,
where
G
acts on
\operatorname{U}(1)
via
\phi\colon G\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}\cong\mathrm{Aut}(\operatorname{U}(1))
, and the constant Lie group homomorphism
\vartheta=1
.
Twisted group extensions are discussed in
[
6
, Section 3.4]
and
[
20
, Section 1]
. The super version thereof appears in

[
20
, Section 3]
.

Freed–Moore arrange
\phi
-twisted super extensions of
\Gamma
in a category under the following notion of morphisms, which we copy from
[
20
, Definition 7.27]
.
We call these morphisms “refinements” for reasons that will become clear later.

Definition 5.12
.

Let
(\Gamma,\phi)
be a graded Lie groupoid and let
(\mathcal{L}_{1},\lambda_{1})
and
(\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})
be
\phi
-twisted super extensions of
\Gamma
.
A
refinement

(\mathcal{L}_{1},\lambda_{1})\to(\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})
is an isomorphism

\eta\colon\mathcal{L}_{1}\to\mathcal{L}_{2}

of super line bundles over
\Gamma_{1}
such that the diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 46.39015pt\hbox{
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.53535pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{\gamma_{1}}^{\phi(\gamma_{2})}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{\gamma_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-46.39015pt\raise-16.18054pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.13391pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta_{\gamma_{1}}^{\phi(\gamma_{2})}\otimes\eta_{\gamma_{2}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 58.53535pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 88.53535pt\raise-33.3789pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{2}|_{\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}

(5.6)

commutes for each pair
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\Gamma_{1}
of composable morphisms.

We denote the category of Freed–Moore’s
\phi
-twisted super extensions of
\Gamma
by
\mathrm{FM}(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Interestingly, twisted super extensions also form a bicategory under a natural and more general kind of morphisms.
These more general morphisms do not appear in
[
20
]
but will be useful in order to understand the relation to graded-equivariant super 2-line bundles,
and also clarify the role of twisted vector bundles, see Proposition
5.20
. However, there is an untwisted (i.e.,
\phi=1
)
version of these more general morphisms in
[
17
, Section 2]
.
The situation is fully analogous to the setting of algebras, which may be regarded as a category (with algebra homomorphisms), but also as a bicategory (with bimodules).

Definition 5.13
.

Let
(\Gamma,\phi)
be a graded Lie groupoid and
(\mathcal{L}_{1},\lambda_{1})
and
(\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})
be
\phi
-twisted super extensions of
\Gamma
.
A
1
-morphism

(\mathcal{L}_{1},\lambda_{1})\to(\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})
is a pair
(\mathcal{W},\rho)
consisting of a super vector bundle
\mathcal{W}
over
\Gamma_{0}

and an isomorphism

\rho\colon\ \mathcal{L}_{2}\otimes s^{*}\mathcal{W}\to t^{*}\mathcal{W}^{\phi}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{1}

of super vector bundles over
\Gamma_{1}

(5.7)

of vector bundle morphisms over

is commutative.

is the 1-morphism given by
the super vector bundle

over
\Gamma_{0}
and the isomorphism of vector bundles
