It is straightforward to complete this structure to a bicategory, which we denote by
\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(\Gamma,\phi)
, the bicategory of super extensions of the graded Lie groupoid
(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Proposition 5.14
.

The bicategory
\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(\Gamma,\phi)
is framed under Freed–Moore’s category of twisted super extensions
\mathrm{FM}(\Gamma,\phi).

Proof.

Given a refinement
\eta\colon(\mathcal{L}_{1},\lambda_{1})\to(\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})
, we associate to it the 1-morphism
(\mathcal{W},\rho)\colon\allowbreak(\mathcal{L}_{1},\lambda_{1})\to(\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})

whose super vector bundle
\mathcal{W}
over
\Gamma_{0}
is the trivial one and whose map
\rho\colon\mathcal{L}_{2}\to\mathcal{L}_{1}
is
\eta^{-1}
.
This obviously preserves the composition. Moreover, it is clear that
(\mathcal{W},\rho)
is invertible and hence admits an adjoint.
∎

Now we are in position to establish the relation between the bicategory
\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(\Gamma,\phi)

of
\phi
-twisted super extensions and our bicategory of twistings
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Theorem 5.15
.

Let
(\Gamma,\phi)
be a graded Lie groupoid. There is a canonical equivalence of framed bicategories

71.7716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 71.7716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(\Gamma,\phi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.25711pt\raise-16.46724pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.25711pt\raise-24.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-37.12856pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\Gamma,\phi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.12856pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 61.12856pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma,\phi)}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(5.8)

between the framed bicategory of
\phi
-twisted super extensions of
\Gamma
and the framed bicategory
of super
2
-line bundles over
(\Gamma,\phi)
whose underlying super
2
-line bundle is trivial.

Proof.

In order to define the vertical functors on object level, we compare Definitions
5.9
and
3.3
.
First, we note that trivial super 2-line bundles canonically pull back and complex conjugate to trivial super 2-line bundles.
Then, the data of the 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P}\colon\mathscr{I}\to\mathscr{I}
over
\Gamma_{1}
(cf. (
3.2
))
reduces to a super line bundle
\mathcal{L}
over
\Gamma_{1}

and the 2-isomorphism
\psi\colon\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathscr{P}^{\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\phi}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}\Rightarrow c^{*}\mathscr{P}
(cf. (
3.3
)) reduces to an isomorphism

\lambda_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\colon\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}}^{\phi(\gamma_{2})}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{2}}\to\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2}}
of super line bundles so that
(
3.4
) becomes (
5.5
). In other words, a triple

(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P},\psi)
is the same as a
\phi
-twisted super extension
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
of
\Gamma
. Hence, we define
the vertical functors in diagram (
5.8
) on object level by mapping
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)\to(\mathscr{I},\mathcal{L},\lambda)
.

A similar comparison can be done on the level of refinements, 1- and 2-morphisms; we will not spell this out in too much detail, but we write down the definitions
that should be compared. To define the functor
\mathrm{FM}(\Gamma,\phi)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\Gamma,\phi)
on the level of morphisms, we note that, for trivial underlying 2-line bundles,
the data of Definition
3.12
reduces to a 2-isomorphism
{\eta\colon\mathscr{P}_{2}\Rightarrow\mathscr{P}_{1}}

which, by the description above, is the same as an isomorphism of super line bundles
\mathcal{L}_{2}\to\mathcal{L}_{1}
over
\Gamma_{1}
that is – due to (
3.7
) – compatible
with the maps
\lambda
in the sense of (
5.6
).
In other words,
\eta^{-1}\colon\mathcal{L}_{1}\to\mathcal{L}_{2}
is precisely a refinement of
\phi
-twisted super extensions of
\Gamma

in the sense of Definition
5.12
.

To define the functor
\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(\Gamma,\phi)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma,\phi)
on the level of 1-morphisms, we note that Definition
3.5

reduces – for trivial underlying 2-line bundles – to Definition
5.13
. More precisely,
the 1-morphism
\mathscr{B}\colon\mathscr{I}\to\mathscr{I}
over
\Gamma_{0}
of (
3.5
) is the same as a super vector bundle
\mathcal{W}
over
\Gamma_{0}
, and the 2-isomorphism
\eta\colon\mathscr{P}_{2}\circ s^{*}\mathscr{B}\Rightarrow t^{*}\mathscr{B}^{\phi}\circ\mathscr{P}_{1}

of (
3.6
) reduces to an isomorphism
{\rho\colon\mathcal{L}_{2}\otimes s^{*}\mathcal{W}\to t^{*}\mathcal{W}^{\phi}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{1}}

of super vector bundles over
\Gamma_{1}
such that (
3.7
) becomes (
5.7
).
Similar reasoning holds on the level of 2-morphisms.

It is also easy to check that both constructions result in functors and moreover in an equivalence of categories and an equivalence of bicategories, respectively.
∎

Remark 5.16
.

Theorem
5.15
together with our cohomological classification Theorem
3.22
shows that
\phi
-twisted super extensions (up to 1-isomorphisms in the sense of Definition
5.13
) are classified by the kernel of the homomorphism

p_{0}\times p_{0}\colon\ \check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}((\Gamma,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt)\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{0},\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(\Gamma_{0},\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt),

since this homomorphism projects to the underlying super 2-line bundle over
\Gamma_{0}
,
which is trivial in Theorem
5.15
. In particular, in the case of an action groupoid
(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})
for a graded Lie group
(G,\varepsilon)
acting on a smooth manifold
X
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{h}_{0}\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathrm{ker}\bigl(p_{0}\times p_{0}\colon\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(X/\!\!/G,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}\bigl((X/\!\!/G,\phi_{\varepsilon}),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt\bigr)\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}\bigl(X,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt\bigr)\bigr);

which is in line with a rigorous combination of the ideas sketched in
[
20
, Remark 7.28]
and the discussion of the ungraded (
\phi=1
) case in

[
17
, Section 2.2.1]
.
Even more special, if

\Gamma=\mathrm{B}G
(see Example
5.11
), we get

\mathrm{h}_{0}\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(\mathrm{B}G,\phi)\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}\bigl((\mathrm{B}G,\phi),\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt\bigr).

In the remainder of this section, we
embed the twisted super vector bundles of Freed–Moore
[
20
, Definition 7.23]
into our framework.

Definition 5.17
.

Let
(\Gamma,\phi)
be a graded Lie groupoid and let
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
a
\phi
-twisted super extension of
\Gamma
.
A
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
-twisted super vector bundle
over
\Gamma
is a super vector bundle
\mathcal{W}
over
\Gamma_{0}

together with an isomorphism of super vector bundles

\rho\colon\ (\mathcal{L}\otimes s^{*}\mathcal{W})^{\phi}\to t^{*}\mathcal{W}

(5.9)

over
\Gamma_{1}
such that the following diagram

(5.10)

of vector bundle morphisms commutes for each pair
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\Gamma_{2}
.

Remark 5.18
.

Comparing this with
[
20
, Definition 7.23 (iv)]
, we see that (
5.9
) coincides with
[
20
, equation (7.24)]
and diagram (
5.10
) is what is demanded as an analog of diagram
[
20
, equation (7.16)]
.
The requirement that “the map
\rho
is even or odd according to
\vartheta(\gamma)
” is implemented here automatically as
\rho
is a super vector bundle homomorphism, and
\vartheta
is the grading of
\mathcal{L}
in our setting.
There are two minor differences to
[
20
]
:

•

The map
\rho
in
[
20
]
is only required to be linear,
not an isomorphism; however, since we are talking about equivariance, after all, our choice appears quite naturally.

•

We have formulated all definitions throughout the paper in a smooth setting, whereas the regularity is partly not clearly stated in loc.cit.

The following definition establishes the natural kind of morphism between
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
-twisted vector bundles; this notion does not appear in
[
20
]
.

Definition 5.19
.

Let
(\Gamma,\phi)
be a graded Lie groupoid, let
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
be a
\phi
-twisted super extension of
\Gamma
, and let

(\mathcal{W},\rho)
and
(\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
be
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
-twisted super vector bundles. A morphism
(\mathcal{W},\rho)\to(\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
is a morphism of super vector bundles
\xi\colon\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
over
\Gamma_{0}

that is compatible with
\rho
and
\rho^{\prime}
in the sense that the following diagram is commutative

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 41.48282pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.44933pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{L}\otimes s^{*}\mathcal{W})^{\phi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.47205pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.64377pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-41.48282pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.37001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(\operatorname{id}\otimes s^{*}\xi)^{\phi}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.8521pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 85.64377pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{t^{*}\mathcal{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 97.88684pt\raise-15.2361pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.99155pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t^{*}\xi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 97.88684pt\raise-24.9722pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-28.8521pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathcal{L}\otimes s^{*}\mathcal{W}^{\prime})^{\phi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.42517pt\raise-39.56836pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.21725pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.8521pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.8521pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 82.8521pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{t^{*}\mathcal{W}^{\prime}.}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}

We denote the category of
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
-twisted super vector bundles by
\mathrm{FM}\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{(\mathcal{L},\lambda)}(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Proposition 5.20
.

There is an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{FM}\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{(\mathcal{L},\lambda)}(\Gamma,\phi)\cong\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(\Gamma,\phi)}((\mathcal{L},\lambda),I_{\Gamma,\phi}).

Proof.

This is clear by comparing definitions and slight rephrasing.
The isomorphism (
5.9
) can equivalently be understood
as an isomorphism

\mathcal{L}\otimes s^{*}\mathcal{W}\to(t^{*}\mathcal{W})^{\phi}

which by duality is then equivalent to an isomorphism

s^{*}\mathcal{W}^{*}\to(t^{*}\mathcal{W}^{*})^{\phi}\otimes\mathcal{L}
.
Hence, we assign
\bigl(\mathcal{W}^{*},(\rho^{*})^{-1}\bigr)\in\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(\Gamma,\phi)}((\mathcal{L},\lambda),I_{\Gamma,\phi})

to the object
(\mathcal{W},\rho)\in\mathrm{FM}\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{(\mathcal{L},\lambda)}(\Gamma,\phi)
. It is straightforward to compare the respective compatibility diagrams and
extend this assignment into an equivalence of categories.
∎

Corollary 5.21
.

There is an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{FM}\text{-}\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{(\mathcal{L},\lambda)}(\Gamma,\phi)\cong\mathrm{s}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{(\mathscr{I},\mathcal{L},\lambda)}(\Gamma,\phi).

Thus, the twisted vector bundles of Freed–Moore are precisely our
(\mathscr{I},\mathcal{L},\lambda)
-twisted super vector bundles in the sense of Definition
4.2
.

Proof.

We combine Proposition
5.20
and Theorem
5.15
, using the fact that
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma,\phi)
is a full sub-bicategory of
\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
.
∎

5.3
Freed–Hopkins–Teleman’s twistings

We show in
Theorem
5.26
that the twistings of Freed, Hopkins, and Teleman are equivariant super bundle gerbes, and hence, in particular, equivariant super 2-line bundles.
To start with, the twistings of
[
17
]
are based on the notion of a central super extension of a Lie groupoid.

Definition 5.22
.

Let
\Gamma
be a Lie groupoid. The bicategory of
central super extensions of
\Gamma
is defined as

\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Gamma):=\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(\Gamma,1)
,
i.e., a central super extension of
\Gamma
is a
\phi
-twisted super extension of
\Gamma
in the sense of
Definition
5.9
, for the trivial grading
\phi=1
.

This definition has been given originally in
[
17
, Section 2]
; here we present it – for economical reasons – as a special case of our bicategory extension of Freed–Moore’s category of
\phi
-twisted super extensions, carried out in Section
5.2
.

Remark 5.23
.

Likewise, as the bicategory extension
\mathrm{FM}^{bi}(\Gamma,\phi)
is framed under Freed–Moore’s original category
\mathrm{FM}(\Gamma,\phi)
, the bicategory
\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Gamma)
is framed under the category
\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\Gamma):=\mathrm{FM}(\Gamma,1)
.

The following definition is a smooth version of Freed–Hopkins–Teleman’s definition of twistings, see
[
17
, Section 2.3]
.

Definition 5.24
.

Let
\Gamma
be a Lie groupoid. An
FHT-twisting
on
\Gamma
is a quadruple
(\Lambda,T,\mathcal{L},\lambda)
consisting of a Lie groupoid
\Lambda
, a weak equivalence
T\colon\Lambda\to\Gamma
and a central super extension
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
of
\Lambda
.
A morphism
(\Lambda_{1},T_{1},\mathcal{L}_{1},\lambda_{1})\to(\Lambda_{2},T_{2},\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})
is an equivalence class
[\Omega,S,\mathcal{W},\rho]
represented by a Lie groupoid
\Omega
, a weak equivalence
S\colon\Omega\to\Lambda_{1}\times_{\Gamma}\Lambda_{2}
and of
a 1-morphism

(\mathcal{W},\rho)\colon\ S^{*}\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\lambda_{1})\to S^{*}\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})

in
\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Omega)
.
The equivalence relation identifies
(\Omega,S,\mathcal{W},\rho)
with
(\Omega^{\prime},S^{\prime},\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
if there exists a smooth functor
G\colon\Omega\to\Omega^{\prime}
,
a smooth natural transformation
S\cong S^{\prime}\circ G
, and a 2-isomorphism

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 39.70906pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-32.32643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 43.44167pt\raise-38.78171pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.0228pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{G^{*}(\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 89.70914pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 89.70914pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{G^{*}S^{\prime*}\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

in
\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Omega)
, where the vertical 1-morphisms are the ones induced by the natural isomorphism
S\cong S^{\prime}\circ G
under a procedure analogous to our Lemma
3.15
.
We denote the category of FHT-twistings on the Lie groupoid
\Gamma
by
\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
.

We recall the fibre product of Lie groupoids used above in Definition
A.9
.

The composition of two morphisms
[\Omega,S,\mathcal{W},\rho]\colon(\Lambda_{1},T_{1},\mathcal{L}_{1},\lambda_{1})\to(\Lambda_{2},T_{2},\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})
and

\big[\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{S},\allowbreak\smash{\tilde{\mathcal{W}},\tilde{\rho}}\big]\colon(\Lambda_{2},T_{2},\mathcal{L}_{2},\lambda_{2})\to(\Lambda_{3},T_{3},\mathcal{L}_{3},\lambda_{3})
in
\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
consists of the fibre product Lie groupoid
\Omega\times_{\Lambda_{2}}\tilde{\Omega}
, the weak equivalence

\textstyle{\Omega\times_{\Lambda_{2}}\tilde{\Omega}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S\times_{\Lambda_{2}}\tilde{S}}
\textstyle{(\Lambda_{1}\times_{\Gamma}\Lambda_{2})\times_{\Lambda_{2}}(\Lambda_{2}\times_{\Gamma}\Lambda_{3})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{14}}
\textstyle{\Lambda_{1}\times_{\Gamma}\Lambda_{3},}

and of the 1-morphism given by the composition of the pullbacks of
(\mathcal{W},\rho)
and
\bigl(\tilde{\mathcal{W}},\tilde{\rho}\bigr)
to
\Omega\times_{\Lambda_{2}}\tilde{\Omega}
.
The identity 1-morphism of an object
(\Lambda,T,\mathcal{L},\lambda)\in\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
can be represented by the diagonal functor
\Delta\colon\Lambda\to\Lambda\times_{\Gamma}\Lambda

together with the trivial 1-isomorphism
\Delta^{*}\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{L},\lambda)\cong\Delta^{*}\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
.

Remark 5.25
.

Our Definition
5.24
is a smooth version of the original definition of
[
17
]
.
More precisely, in that original version, the groupoids
\Lambda
and
\Omega
are
topological
groupoids, and the central super extension
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
is a
continuous
version of smooth central super extensions. Moreover, the functors
T
and
S
are
local
equivalences instead of weak equivalences.
A continuous functor
{F\colon\Gamma\to\Lambda}
between topological groupoids is called a
local equivalence

[
17
, Definition A.4]
if it is
topologically essentially surjective (this is analogous to the first point of Definition
A.1
, with “surjective submersion” replaced by “locally split map”) and if the induced map on hom-sets

F_{x,x^{\prime}}\colon\ \mathcal{H}\!{\rm om}_{\Gamma}\bigl(x,x^{\prime}\bigr)\to\mathcal{H}\!{\rm om}_{\Lambda}\bigl(F_{0}(x),F_{0}\bigl(x^{\prime}\bigr)\bigr)

(5.11)

is a homeomorphism for all objects
x,x^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{0}
, where
\mathcal{H}\!{\rm om}_{\Gamma}(x,x^{\prime})\subseteq\Gamma_{1}
and analogously
\mathcal{H}\!{\rm om}_{\Lambda}(F_{0}(x),F_{0}(x^{\prime}))
carry the subspace topologies. One can show that weak equivalences are always local equivalences, but the converse may not hold.
Indeed, it is claimed in
[
17
, Remark A.5 and Example A.7]
that a continuous functor is a
local
equivalence if and only if it induces equivalences on stalks between the topological stacks represented by the groupoids; whereas a functor is a
weak
equivalence if and only if it induces an equivalence between the stacks.
Denoting the category of original twistings, as
defined in

[
17
]
, by
\mathrm{FHT}^{\rm top}(\Gamma)
; our Definition
5.24
yields a subcategory

\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)\subseteq\mathrm{FHT}^{\rm top}(\Gamma).

(5.12)

This subcategory inclusion (
5.12
) is essentially surjective. This follows from the classification results:
[
17
, Corollary 2.25]
shows a bijection

\mathrm{h}_{0}\mathrm{FHT}^{\rm top}(\Gamma)\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\Gamma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}^{2}(\Gamma,\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(2.0,0.0){\text{\lx@text@underline{\hskip 11.69603pt}}}\end{picture}\mathbb{C}^{\times}\hskip-0.42677pt).

On the other hand, Theorem
5.26
and Proposition
3.19

imply that
\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
has the same classification. Thus, for a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
, our smooth version of FHT-twistings coincides with the original ones.

Theorem 5.26
.

Let
\Gamma
be a Lie groupoid. There is an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)\cong\mathrm{h}_{1}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma))\subseteq\mathrm{h}_{1}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma))

between the category of Freed–Hopkins–Teleman twistings and the homotopy 1-category of super bundle gerbes over
\Gamma
.

Proof.

The equivalence is split into two functors

\mathrm{h}_{1}(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma))\to\mathrm{h}_{1}\bigl(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{F}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)

which are both equivalences. The category in the middle is the homotopy category of the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)
, which is obtained by considering the presheaf of bicategories
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}
of trivial super bundle gerbes, stackifying it, and evaluating it on the Lie groupoid
\Gamma
. Below we describe it explicitly.
By definition of a bundle gerbe (see
[
45
]
), there is an equivalence
{\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}\cong\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}}

between 2-stacks on the category of smooth manifolds, which extends to an equivalence of their extensions to Lie groupoids. This shows the first equivalence. The functor
\mathcal{F}
is constructed below; the proof that it is an equivalence is given in Proposition
5.30
.
∎

The construction of the functor
\mathcal{F}
faces two main challenges:

1.

The translation between the language of bundle gerbes and the language of central super extensions.

2.

The transformation of surjective submersions
and their fibre products into weak equivalences and their fibre products.

The first challenge is taken by the reduction of Theorem
5.15
to ungraded Lie groupoids, which we state (using Remark
3.11
) as the following corollary.

Corollary 5.27
.

The bicategories of
central super extensions and of trivial super bundle gerbes over a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
are canonically equivalent,

\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Gamma)\cong\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma)
.

Remark 5.28
.

The equivalence of Corollary
5.27
was obtained separately by Mertsch

[
36
, Proposition B.3.7]
.
In
[
36
, Theorem B.3.13]
, a further comparison between the category of FHT-twistings and super bundle gerbes
was obtained for global quotient groupoids
\Gamma=X/\!\!/G
. The main insight of Mertsch was that the definition of FHT-twistings
resembles the plus construction described in
[
45
, Proposition 9.1]
. The idea of our construction is similar; the main difference is that
we do not restrict to global quotient groupoids, and use weak equivalences instead of local equivalences. According to
[
36
, Lemma B.3.10]
, the notions of local and weak equivalences coincide for certain global quotient groupoids.

The second challenge is taken manually in course of the construction of the functor
\mathcal{F}
.
We first recall a concrete description of the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)
obtained in
[
45
, Proposition 9.1]
.
We use the notion of coverings groupoids
\pi\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma
from Definition
A.4
, for a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
and a surjective submersion
\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
. We also use the terminology that a
refinement
of a surjective submersion
\pi\colon Y\to M
is another surjective submersion
\zeta\colon Z\to M
together with a surjective submersion
Z\to Y
such that the diagram

is commutative. In that notation, the description of
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)
is the following:

1.

An object is a quadruple
(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)
, where
\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
is a surjective submersion and
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P},\psi)
is an object in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma^{\pi})
(in the triple notation of Definition
3.3
).

2.

A 1-morphism
(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)\to\bigl(Y^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}\bigr)
is again a quadruple
(Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta)
, where

\zeta\colon Z\to\Gamma_{0}
is a refinement of
Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y^{\prime}\to\Gamma_{0}
and

(\mathscr{B},\eta)\colon\ \zeta_{Y}^{*}(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P},\psi)\to\zeta_{Y^{\prime}}^{*}(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})

is a 1-morphism in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}\bigl(\Gamma^{\zeta}\bigr)
(in the notation of Definition
3.5
). Here, we have denoted the projections from
Z
to
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
by
\zeta_{Y}
and
\zeta_{Y^{\prime}}
, respectively; the passage from refinements to the corresponding weak equivalences can be visualized as follows:

3.

The composition

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 25.41264pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-25.41264pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.46297pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.41273pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 85.41273pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl(Y^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 155.9132pt\raise 6.7728pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.0228pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(Z^{\prime},\zeta^{\prime},\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 210.48799pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 210.48799pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(Y^{\prime\prime},\pi^{\prime\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

of 1-morphisms is defined over the fibre product

, considered as a refinement of

, together with the 1-morphism

.

4.

The identity morphism of an object
(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)
is defined over the fibre product
Z:=Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y
, considered as a refinement
\zeta\colon Z\to\Gamma_{0}
of itself; its 1-morphism over
\Gamma^{\zeta}_{0}=Z
is
{\mathscr{B}:=i^{*}\mathscr{P}}
, where
i\colon Z\to\Gamma^{\pi}_{1}
is defined by
(y_{1},y_{2})\mapsto(y_{2},\operatorname{id},y_{1})
, and its 2-isomorphism
\eta
over
\Gamma^{\zeta}_{1}=Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}\Gamma_{1}\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y
is

\eta:=i_{2}^{*}\psi^{-1}\circ i_{1}^{*}\psi

where the two maps
\smash{\Gamma^{\zeta}_{1}}\to\Gamma^{\pi}_{2}
are given by

\displaystyle i_{1}(y_{1},y_{2},\gamma,y_{4},y_{5}):=((y_{2},\gamma,y_{5}),(y_{5},\operatorname{id},y_{4}))\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle i_{2}(y_{1},y_{2},\gamma,y_{4},y_{5}):=((y_{2},\operatorname{id},y_{1}),(y_{1},\gamma,y_{4})).

5.

Let
(Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta),\bigl(\tilde{Z},\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\mathscr{B}},\tilde{\eta}\bigr)\colon(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)\to\bigl(Y^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}\bigr)
be 1-morphisms.
A 2-morphism
(Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta)\Rightarrow\bigl(\tilde{Z},\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\mathscr{B}},\tilde{\eta}\bigr)
is an equivalence class of triples
(W,\omega,\xi)
consisting of
a refinement
\omega\colon W\to\Gamma_{0}
of
Z\times_{Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y^{\prime}}\tilde{Z}\to\Gamma_{0}
and of a 2-morphism

\xi\colon\omega_{Z}^{*}(\mathscr{B},\eta)\Rightarrow\omega_{\tilde{Z}}^{*}\bigl(\tilde{\mathscr{B}},\tilde{\eta}\bigr)

in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma^{\omega})
;
the various refinements and corresponding weak equivalences are depicted in the following commutative diagram:

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.74963pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&&\cr&\cr&&&&&&\cr&\cr&&&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 28.57405pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern The equivalence relation identifies
(W,\omega,\xi)\sim\bigl(\hat{W},\hat{\omega},\hat{\xi}\bigr)
if there is a refinement of the fibre product of
W
and
\hat{W}
over
Z\times_{Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y^{\prime}}\tilde{Z}
, on which the pullbacks of
\xi
and
\hat{\xi}
coincide. Since we are only interested in the homotopy 1-category, the details of this equivalence relation, as well as horizontal and vertical composition of 2-morphisms, are not relevant.

Remark 5.29
.

Under this description of the bicategory
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)
, the functor
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma)\to\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)
is the following. If
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{P},\psi)
is a super bundle gerbe over
\Gamma
, where
\mathscr{G}=(Y,\pi,\mathcal{L},\mu)
is its super bundle gerbe over
\Gamma_{0}
,
then
\pi^{*}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{P},\psi)
is a super bundle gerbe over
\Gamma^{\pi}
, containing the super bundle gerbe
\pi^{*}\mathscr{G}
over
\Gamma^{\pi}_{0}=Y
. The surjective submersion of that bundle gerbe has a canonical section, which induces a canonical trivialization
\mathscr{T}\colon\pi^{*}\mathscr{G}\to\mathscr{I}
. This, in turn, induces a 1-isomorphism
\pi^{*}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{P},\psi)\cong(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(\Gamma^{\pi})
, whose codomain is an object in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma^{\pi})
. Thus, the quadruple
(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
is an object in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)
.

Now we are in position to construct the functor

\mathcal{F}\colon\ \mathrm{h}_{1}\bigl(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)\bigr)\to\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
.
To an object in
(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)\in\mathrm{h}_{1}\bigl(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)\bigr)
we assign the object
(\Gamma^{\pi},\pi,\mathcal{L},\lambda)\in\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
consisting of
the weak equivalence
\pi\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma
and the
central super extension
(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
of
\Gamma^{\pi}
that corresponds to the super bundle gerbe
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P},\psi)
over
\Gamma^{\pi}
under the equivalence of Corollary
5.27
.
To a morphism

[(Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta)]\colon\ (Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)\to\bigl(Y^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}\bigr)

in
\mathrm{h}_{1}\bigl(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)\bigr)
we assign the equivalence class

\big[\Gamma^{\zeta},S,\mathcal{W},\rho\big]
, where
S\colon\Gamma^{\zeta}\to\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}
is the weak equivalence obtained from the refinement map
Z\to Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y^{\prime}
and the weak equivalence of Lemma
A.13
, and
(\mathcal{W},\rho)
is the 1-morphism between central super extension that corresponds to
(\mathscr{B},\eta)
under the equivalence of Corollary
5.27
.

First, we need to check that this is well-defined. Indeed, let
(Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta)
and
\bigl(\tilde{Z},\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\mathscr{B}},\tilde{\eta}\bigr)
be two representatives of the same 1-morphism
\mathrm{h}_{1}\bigl(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)\bigr)
, i.e., there exists a 2-isomorphism
(W,\omega,\xi)
between them. The refinement diagram in
5
above
yields, together with Lemma
A.13
, a strictly commutative diagram of weak equivalences

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.19446pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 85.8068pt\raise-55.05998pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{S}}$}}}\kern Moreover,
\xi
is a 2-isomorphism
\xi\colon F^{*}(\mathscr{B},\eta)\Rightarrow\tilde{F}^{*}\bigl(\tilde{\mathscr{B}},\tilde{\eta}\bigr)
.
The equivalence relation imposed in Definition
5.24
on the morphisms of
\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
identifies then

\bigl(\Gamma^{\zeta},S,\mathcal{W},\rho\bigr)\sim(\Gamma^{\omega},U,F^{*}(\mathcal{W},\rho))\sim\bigl(\Gamma^{\omega},U,\tilde{F}^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{W}},\tilde{\rho})\bigr)\sim\bigl(\Gamma^{\tilde{\zeta}},\tilde{S},\tilde{\mathcal{W}},\tilde{\rho}\bigr);

this shows that our functor is well-defined.

Next we prove that identity morphisms go to identities.
Let
(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)\in\mathrm{h}_{1}\bigl(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)\bigr)
, with
\mathcal{F}((Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi))=:(\Gamma^{\pi},\pi,\mathcal{L},\lambda)
. Let
\operatorname{id}_{(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)}=(Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta)
be the identity morphism, as explained in
4
.
Thus,
\mathcal{F}(\operatorname{id}_{(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)})=\big[\Gamma^{\zeta},S,\mathcal{W},\rho\big]
, where
S
is the weak equivalence
\Gamma^{\zeta}\to\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi}
of Lemma
A.13
. We consider the functor
G\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma^{\zeta}
defined on objects by
y\mapsto(y,y)
, and analogously on morphisms. Let
i
,
i_{1}
,
i_{2}
be the maps
considered in
4
; then, we have
(i\circ G)(y)=(y,\operatorname{id},y)=\operatorname{id}_{y}
and thus, via Remark
3.4
,
G^{*}i^{*}\mathscr{P}=\operatorname{id}^{*}\mathscr{P}\cong\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{I}}
. Moreover, we have
(i_{1}\circ G)(y_{1},\gamma,y_{2})=((y_{1},\gamma,y_{2}),(y_{2},\operatorname{id},y_{2}))
, and similarly,
(i_{2}\circ G)(y_{1},\gamma,y_{2})=((y_{1},\operatorname{id},y_{1}),(y_{1},\gamma,y_{2}))
, showing that

G^{*}(\mathscr{B},\eta)\cong(\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{I}},\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{P}})=\operatorname{id}_{(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P},\psi)}
.
Under the equivalence of Corollary
5.27
, this implies
G^{*}(\mathcal{W},\rho)\cong\operatorname{id}_{(\mathcal{L},\lambda)}
in
\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Gamma^{\pi})
. The diagram

\textstyle{\Gamma^{\pi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\zeta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi}}

is strictly commutative; hence, we have the following equality of morphisms in
\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)

\mathcal{F}(\operatorname{id}_{(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)})=\big[\Gamma^{\zeta},S,\mathcal{W},\rho\big]=[\Gamma^{\pi},\Delta,\operatorname{id}_{(\mathcal{L},\lambda)}]=\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{F}((Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi))}.

This shows that
\mathcal{F}
preserves identities.

It remains to show that
\mathcal{F}
is compatible with composition. Suppose

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 25.41264pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-25.41264pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.46297pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.41273pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 85.41273pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigl(Y^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 155.9132pt\raise 6.7728pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.0228pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(Z^{\prime},\zeta^{\prime},\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 210.48799pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 210.48799pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(Y^{\prime\prime},\pi^{\prime\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime})}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

are 1-morphisms and
\bigl(\tilde{Z},\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\mathscr{B}},\tilde{\eta}\bigr)
is their composition as defined in
3
.
We compute

\mathcal{F}([Z^{\prime},\zeta^{\prime},\mathscr{B}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}])\circ\mathcal{F}([Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta])=\big[\Gamma^{\zeta^{\prime}},S^{\prime},\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}\big]\circ\big[\Gamma^{\zeta},S,\mathcal{W},\rho\big]

in
\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
, which consists of the fibre product Lie groupoid
\Gamma^{\zeta}\times_{\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}}\Gamma^{\zeta^{\prime}}
, the weak equivalence

\textstyle{\Gamma^{\zeta}\times_{\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}}\Gamma^{\zeta^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S\times_{\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}}S^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\bigl(\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}\bigr)\times_{{}_{\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}}}\bigl(\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime\prime}}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{14}}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime\prime}},}

and of the 1-morphism given by the composition of the pullbacks of
(\mathcal{W},\rho)
and
(\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
to
\Gamma^{\zeta}\times_{\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}}\Gamma^{\zeta^{\prime}}
.
On the other hand, we set
\big[\Gamma^{\tilde{\zeta}},\tilde{S},\tilde{\mathcal{W}},\tilde{\rho}\big]:=\mathcal{F}\bigl(\big[\tilde{Z},\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\mathscr{B}},\tilde{\eta}\big]\bigr)
. Lemma
A.13
provides a weak equivalence
\Gamma^{\tilde{\zeta}}\to\Gamma^{\zeta}\times_{\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}}\Gamma^{\zeta^{\prime}}
under which the 1-morphisms coincide. This implies

\big[\Gamma^{\tilde{\zeta}},\tilde{S},\tilde{\mathcal{W}},\tilde{\rho}\big]=\big[\Gamma^{\zeta^{\prime}},S^{\prime},\mathcal{W}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime}\big]\circ\big[\Gamma^{\zeta},S,\mathcal{W},\rho\big]

in
\mathrm{FHT(\Gamma)}
, showing that
\mathcal{F}
respects the composition. It remains to prove the following.

Proposition 5.30
.

The functor
\mathcal{F}
is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

We prove the claim in the usual three parts. We start by proving that
\mathcal{F}
is essentially surjective.
Let
(\Lambda,T,\mathcal{L},\lambda)
be a FHT-twisting. According to Proposition
A.8
, there is a surjective submersion
\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
and a strong equivalence
F\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Lambda
such that
T\circ F\cong P^{\pi}
.
Let
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P},\psi)\in\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma^{\pi})
correspond to the central super extension
F^{*}(\mathcal{L},\lambda)
of
\Gamma^{\pi}
, under the equivalence of Corollary
5.27
.
Then,
(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)
is an object in
\mathrm{h}_{1}\bigl(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)\bigr)
, which we claim to be an essential preimage of
(\Lambda,T,\mathcal{L},\lambda)
under
\mathcal{F}
.
By definition of
\mathcal{F}
, we have
\mathcal{F}(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)=(\Gamma^{\pi},\pi,F^{*}(\mathcal{L},\lambda))
. It remains to prove that

(\Gamma^{\pi},\pi,F^{*}(\mathcal{L},\lambda))\cong(\Lambda,T,\mathcal{L},\lambda)
in
\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
. Indeed, an isomorphism between them is given by the Lie groupoid
\Omega:=\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi}
,
the weak equivalence

S:=\operatorname{id}\times_{\Gamma}F\colon\ \Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Lambda,

constructed via Lemma
A.12
,
and the isomorphism

S^{*}\operatorname{pr}_{1}^{*}F^{*}(\mathcal{L},\lambda)\cong S^{*}\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{L},\lambda)

of central super extensions of
\Omega
, which is induced from the equality

F\circ\operatorname{pr}_{1}\circ S=\operatorname{pr}_{2}\circ S
of functors
{\Omega\to\Lambda}
.

Next we show that the functor
\mathcal{F}
is full.
Let
(Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)
and
\bigl(Y^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}\bigr)
be objects in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)
and let

[\Omega,S,\mathcal{W},\rho]\colon\ \bigl(\Gamma^{\pi},\pi,\mathcal{L},\lambda\bigr)\to\bigl(\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}},{\pi^{\prime}},\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime}\bigr)

be a morphism between their images in
\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
. By definition,
S\colon\Omega\to\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}
is a weak equivalence.
By Proposition
A.8
, there exists a surjective submersion

\psi\colon\ W\to\bigl(\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}\bigr)_{0}=Y\hskip-1.49994pt\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79312pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99509pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\pi}}}}\times^{{\kern-17.1201pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79312pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99509pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt{\pi^{\prime}}}}}Y^{\prime},

and a strong equivalence
F\colon\bigl(\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}\bigr)^{\psi}\to\Omega
such that
S\circ F\cong{\psi}
. Thus, we have

[\Omega,S,\mathcal{W},\rho]=\big[\bigl(\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}\bigr)^{\psi},{\psi},F^{*}(\mathcal{W},\rho)\big].

Next we consider the weak equivalence
H\colon\Gamma^{\tilde{\pi}}\to\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}
of Lemma
A.13
, where
\tilde{\pi}
is the projection
\tilde{\pi}\colon Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y^{\prime}\to\Gamma_{0}
, and pull back
\psi
along
H
, see Lemma
A.7
. This yields a surjective submersion
\zeta\colon Z\to Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y^{\prime}
and a weak equivalence
G\colon\bigl(\Gamma^{\tilde{\pi}}\bigr)^{\zeta}\to\bigl(\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}\bigr)^{\psi}
. Noting that
\bigl(\Gamma^{\tilde{\pi}}\bigr)^{\zeta}=\Gamma^{\tilde{\pi}\circ\zeta}
, we obtain another equality

\big[\bigl(\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}\bigr)^{\psi},{\psi},F^{*}(\mathcal{W},\rho)\big]=\big[\Gamma^{\tilde{\pi}\circ\zeta},H\circ{\zeta},G^{*}F^{*}(\mathcal{W},\rho)\big].

The right-hand side is the image under
\mathcal{F}
of the 1-morphism
(Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta)
, where
(\mathscr{B},\eta)
corresponds to the 1-morphism of central super extensions
G^{*}F^{*}(\mathcal{W},\rho)
under the equivalence of Corollary
5.27
.

Finally, we prove that
the functor
\mathcal{F}
is faithful.
Let

(Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta),\bigl(\tilde{Z},\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\mathscr{B}},\tilde{\eta}\bigr)\colon\ (Y,\pi,\mathscr{P},\psi)\to\bigl(Y^{\prime},\pi^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}\bigr)

be morphisms in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)

such that their images under
\mathcal{F}
coincide, i.e.,
\big[\Gamma^{\zeta},S,\mathcal{W},\rho\big]=\smash{\big[\Gamma^{\tilde{\zeta}},\tilde{S},\tilde{\mathcal{W}},\tilde{\rho}\big]}
in
\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma)
. This means that there is a smooth functor
G
, a smooth
natural transformation
\kappa
fitting into the diagram

, and thus, a smooth natural transformation

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Gamma^{\tilde{\zeta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53575pt\raise-33.34222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.99998pt\raise-3.47221pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\lambda}$}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.19446pt\raise-33.34222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.83788pt\raise-58.05553pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-67.98004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 13.19446pt\raise-67.98004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}.}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(5.14)

Now, the diagram in (
5.13
) is a sub-diagram of the one in (
5.14
), and
by Remark
A.2

2
, all functors in both diagrams are weak equivalences. Using Remark
A.2

3
, this implies that there is also a smooth natural transformation
in the complementary sub-diagram,

\textstyle{\Gamma^{\omega}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{\tilde{Z}}}
\textstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{Z}}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\zeta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\Gamma^{\tilde{\zeta}}.}

The pullback of
\phi
along
\omega_{Z}
hence gives a 2-isomorphism in
\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Gamma^{\omega})
between
\omega_{\tilde{Z}}^{*}\bigl(\tilde{\mathcal{W}},\tilde{\rho}\bigr)
and
\omega_{Z}^{*}(\mathcal{W},\rho)
. Under the equivalence of Corollary
5.27
, it corresponds to a 2-isomorphism in
\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}(\Gamma^{\omega})
between
\bigl(\tilde{\mathscr{B}},\tilde{\eta}\bigr)
and
(\mathscr{B},\eta)
. This proves that

[Z,\zeta,\mathscr{B},\eta]=\big[\tilde{Z},\tilde{\zeta},\tilde{\mathscr{B}},\tilde{\eta}\big]

in
\mathrm{h}_{1}\bigl(\mathrm{s}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{+}(\Gamma)\bigr)
.
∎

5.4
Moutuou’s real twistings

In this section, we compare our twistings to the real twistings of Moutuou, see
[
40
,
41
]
, which are defined over real groupoids. We associate to each real Lie groupoid a graded Lie groupoid,
and lift this to associate to each real twisting a graded-equivariant super 2-line bundle.

We recall that a
real space
is a topological space
X
together with a continuous involution
{\tau\colon X\to X}
. A
real map
between real spaces is a continuous map that commutes with the involutions.
We will work here in a smooth setting, where
X
is a smooth manifold and
\tau
is smooth, and call this a
real manifold
.
The notion of a real vector bundle was introduced by Atiyah in
[
2
]
. A
real vector bundle
over a real manifold
(X,\tau)
is a complex vector bundle
{\pi\colon E\to X}
in which
E
is a real manifold such that
\pi
is real, and, for all
x\in X
, the involution
{E_{x}\to E_{\tau(x)}}
is anti-linear.
A morphism of real vector bundles is a real morphism of vector bundles.
We denote the category of real vector bundles over
(X,\tau)
by
\mathscr{R}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X,\tau)
. Before we continue with real twistings, we remark that real vector bundles are included in our setting, namely, in Definition
4.2
.

Proposition 5.31
.

For
(X,\tau)
a real manifold, there is a canonical isomorphism of categories

\mathrm{R}\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X,\tau)\cong\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X/\!\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi),

where
X/\!\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is the action groupoid
associated to the involution
\tau\colon X\to X
, and
\phi
is the grading induced by the projection
X\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

Proof.

An object in
\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X/\!\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi)=\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}_{2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(X/\!\!/\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi)}((\mathscr{I},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id}),(\mathscr{I},\operatorname{id},\operatorname{id}))
consists according to Definition
3.5
of a vector bundle
\mathcal{W}
over
X
together with a linear isomorphism

\mathcal{W}_{x}\to\overline{\mathcal{W}_{\tau(x)}}
such that the composition
\mathcal{W}_{x}\to\overline{\mathcal{W}_{\tau(x)}}\to\mathcal{W}_{x}
is the identity. Put differently, we have an antilinear
map
\mathcal{W}_{x}\to\mathcal{W}_{\tau(x)}
which turns
\mathcal{W}
into a real vector bundle. It is straightforward to complete this to the claimed isomorphism.
∎

Next we continue with twistings, and first recall from
[
41
]
that a
real structure
on a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
is a smooth functor
\tau\colon\Gamma\to\Gamma
such that

\tau^{2}=\operatorname{id}
holds. A Lie groupoid together with a real structure is called a
real Lie groupoid
.
Moreover, a
real functor
between real Lie groupoids
(\Gamma,\tau)\to(\Gamma^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
is a smooth functor
F\colon\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
such that

\tau^{\prime}\circ F=F\circ\tau
holds. We denote the category of real Lie groupoids and real functors by
\mathscr{R}\mathscr{L}\text{ie}\mathscr{G}\text{rpd}
.

The definition of real twistings of Moutuou carries out a real version of Freed–Hopkins–Teleman’s construction recalled in Section
5.3
.
We start by giving the following definition, which is a slight reformulation of
[
41
, Definition 2.5.1]
.
We recall that a central super extension of a groupoid
\Gamma
is a
\phi
-twisted super extension of
\Gamma
in the
sense of
Definition
5.9
(for
\phi=1
), and that central super extensions of
\Gamma
form a category
\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\Gamma):=\mathrm{FM}(\Gamma,1)
, see Remark
5.23
.

Definition 5.32
.

A
real central super extension
of
(\Gamma,\tau)
consists of a central super extension
{(\mathcal{L},\lambda)}
of
\Gamma
together with a real structure
\beta\colon\mathcal{L}\to\overline{\tau^{*}\mathcal{L}}
on the super line bundle
\mathcal{L}

that is compatible with
\lambda
in the sense that the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\gamma_{1}}\otimes\beta_{\gamma_{2}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2}}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\tau(\gamma_{1})}\otimes\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\tau(\gamma_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\lambda}_{\tau(\gamma_{1}),\tau(\gamma_{2})}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\tau(\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2})}}

commutes for all composable
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\Gamma_{1}
.
If
(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)
and
(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
are real central super extensions of
(\Gamma,\tau)
, then an
isomorphism

(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)\to(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})

is a refinement
\eta\colon(\mathcal{L},\lambda)\to(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\lambda)
of central super extensions that is compatible
with
\beta
in the sense that the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\gamma}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\prime}_{\gamma}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\tau(\gamma)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\eta}_{\tau(\gamma)}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}_{\tau(\gamma)}}

commutes for all
\gamma\in\Gamma_{1}
.

We denote the category of real central super extensions of
(\Gamma,\tau)
by
\mathscr{R}\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Gamma,\tau)
.
By construction, it comes equipped with a functor

\mathscr{R}\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Gamma,\tau)\to\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\Gamma)

to the category of central super extensions, see Remark
5.23
; this functor forgets the real structure
\beta
.

Let
(\Gamma,\tau)
be a real Lie groupoid,
(Y,\rho)
be a real manifold, and
\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
be a real surjective submersion. The covering groupoid
\Gamma^{\pi}

of Definition
A.4
attains a real structure
\tau^{\pi}\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma^{\pi}
given on objects by

y\mapsto\rho(y)
and on morphisms by
(y,\gamma,y^{\prime})\mapsto(\rho(y),\tau(\gamma),\rho(y^{\prime}))
. The projection functor
\pi\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma
is a real functor.

Definition 5.33
.

A real functor
F\colon(\Gamma^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})\to(\Gamma,\tau)
between real Lie groupoids is called
real weak equivalence
if there exists a real surjective
submersion
\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
and a real strong equivalence
S\colon(\Gamma^{\pi},\tau^{\pi})\to(\Gamma^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
such that
F\circ S\cong\pi
.

Remark 5.34
.

In Definition
5.33
, a real strong equivalence is an isomorphism in the category
\mathscr{R}\mathscr{L}\text{ie}\mathscr{G}\text{rpd}
.
Real weak equivalences between real Lie groupoids are in particular weak equivalences between the underlying Lie groupoids; this follows
from Proposition
A.8
.

Definition 5.35
.

A
Moutuou real twisting
of a real groupoid
(\Gamma,\tau)
consists of another real groupoid
(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})
, a real weak equivalence

F\colon(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})\to(\Gamma,\tau)
, and a real central super extension
{(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)}
of
(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})
. We denote
the set of Moutuou real twistings by
\operatorname{Mou}(\Gamma,\tau)
.

Remark 5.36
.

Any Moutuou real twisting of a real groupoid
(\Gamma,\tau)
has an underlying FHT-twisting of
\Gamma
(see Definition
5.24
)
using the forgetful functor
\mathscr{R}\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Gamma,\tau)\to\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\Gamma)
and Remark
5.34
.

Remark 5.37
.

Definition
5.35
gives – a priori – only a special case of Moutuou’s twistings, where
{F\colon(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})\to(\Gamma,\tau)}
is allowed to be a
real Morita equivalence

[
41
, Definitions 2.4.1 and 2.6.3]
.
In general, real Morita equivalences, are
spans
of so-called
real generalized morphisms
. However, if

(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\leftarrow}}(\Omega,\omega)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\to}}(\Gamma,\tau)

is a span of real generalized morphisms, then one can first replace
(\Omega,\omega)
by another real Lie groupoid such that the real generalized morphisms
G
and
F
become real weak equivalences in the sense of Definition
5.33

[
41
, Proposition 2.4.9]
.
In a second step, if a general Moutuou real twisting comes with a real central super extension
(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)
of
(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})
, then one may pull back
{(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)}
along
G
to obtain one of the more special twistings we defined in Definition
5.35
. We believe that – under a suitable notion of isomorphisms between Moutuou twistings, analogous to Definition
5.24
– the two steps outlined above will result in isomorphisms, and show that every general Moutuou twisting is isomorphic to one defined above.

In order to compare Moutuou real twistings to super 2-line bundles, we first associate to each real Lie groupoid a graded Lie groupoid, via the following construction. First of all, we recall that a strict action of a finite group
G
on a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
consists of smooth functors
{g_{*}\colon\Gamma\to\Gamma}
, for each
g\in G
, such
that
1_{*}=\operatorname{id}_{\Gamma}
and
(hg)_{*}=h_{*}g_{*}
for all
g,h\in G
. The
semi-direct product

\Gamma\rtimes G
has the manifold of objects
\Gamma_{0}
and the manifold of morphisms
\Gamma_{1}\times G
, with
{s(\gamma,g):=g_{*}^{-1}(s(\gamma))}
and
t(\gamma,g):=t(\gamma)
, and composition
(\delta,h)\circ(\gamma,g):=(\delta\circ(h_{*}(\gamma)),hg)
.
Now let
(\Gamma,\tau)
be a real groupoid. We regard
\tau
as a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action on
\Gamma
, and consider the corresponding semidirect product
{\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
, which we equip with a grading
\phi_{\tau}
by projection to the second factor. We denote this graded Lie groupoid by
\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)
. Likewise, to a real functor
F\colon(\Gamma,\tau)\to(\Gamma^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
we assign the smooth
functor
\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\to\Gamma^{\prime}\rtimes_{\tau^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, defined on objects by
x\mapsto F(x)
and on morphisms by
{(\gamma,g)\mapsto(F(\gamma),g)}
. This functor is even with respect to the gradings
\phi_{\tau}
and
\phi_{\tau^{\prime}}
.
We have the following result.

Lemma 5.38
.

The assignment
(\Gamma,\tau)\mapsto\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau):=(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi_{\tau})
extends to a faithful functor

\mathrm{Gr}\colon\ \mathscr{R}\mathscr{L}\text{ie}\mathscr{G}\text{rpd}\to\mathscr{G}\mathrm{r}\mathscr{L}\text{ie}\mathscr{G}\text{rpd},

and it sends real weak equivalences to even weak equivalences.

Proof.

Let
(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})
and
(\Gamma,\tau)
be real Lie groupoids and
F\colon(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})\to(\Gamma,\tau)
a real weak equivalence.
Thus, there exists a real surjective
submersion
\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
and a real strong equivalence
{S\colon(\Gamma^{\pi},\tau^{\pi})\to(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})}
such that
F\circ S\cong\pi
.
Since functors send isomorphisms to isomorphisms, our functor sends real strong equivalences to even strong equivalences. Moreover, it is straightforward to show that the image of
\pi\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma
,

(\Gamma^{\pi}\rtimes_{\tau^{\pi}}\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi_{\tau^{\pi}})\to(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi_{\tau})
,
is a weak equivalence. Via the 2-out-of-3 property of weak equivalences, this shows that the image of
F
is a weak equivalence.
∎

Remark 5.39
.

The functor
\mathrm{Gr}
is not essentially surjective. Indeed, consider the group homomorphism
\mathbb{Z}_{4}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}
,
x\mapsto x\mod 2
which defines a graded Lie groupoid

(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{4},\bmod 2)
. Since the group extension

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{4}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

is non-split,
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
is not a semidirect product.

Remark 5.40
.

If
(\Gamma,\tau)
is a real Lie groupoid, then the underlying groupoid can be obtained from the associated graded Lie groupoid as the kernel of its grading,

\Gamma=\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)^{\rm even}
.
In particular, there is an even functor
(\Gamma,1)\to\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)
.

Remark 5.41
.

Regarding a real manifold
(X,\tau)
as a real Lie groupoid
(X_{\rm dis},\tau_{\rm dis})
with only identity morphisms, the graded Lie groupoid
\mathrm{Gr}(X_{\rm dis},\tau_{\rm dis})
coincides with the graded action groupoid
\mathrm{Gr}(X,\tau)=X/\!\!/(\mathbb{Z}_{2},\operatorname{id})
associated to
(X,\tau)
considered in Example
3.2

4
.

Next, we compare real central super extensions and super 2-line bundles. Let
(\Gamma,\tau)
be a real Lie groupoid and
(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)
a real central super extension of

(\Gamma,\tau)
. We construct a super 2-line bundle over the associated graded Lie groupoid
\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)
. The underlying super 2-line bundle over
(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{0}=\Gamma_{0}
is
\mathscr{I}
, the trivial one.
We define a super line bundle over
(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{1}
by setting

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{(\gamma,x)}:=\begin{cases}\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}&\text{if}\ x=+1,\\
\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma)}&\text{if}\ x=-1.\end{cases}

It corresponds to the required 1-isomorphism

\mathscr{P}\colon s^{*}\mathscr{I}=\mathscr{I}\to\mathscr{I}=(t^{*}\mathscr{I})^{\phi}
over
(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{1}
. Next we construct the 2-isomorphism
\psi
, which corresponds to a super line bundle isomorphism
\tilde{\lambda}
over
{(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{2}}
, fibre-wise

(\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta})_{(\gamma,x),(\gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime})}\colon\ \overline{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{(\gamma,x)}}^{x^{\prime}}\otimes\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{(\gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime})}\to\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{(\gamma\circ(x\cdot\gamma^{\prime}),xx^{\prime})}.

Considering the four possible signs of
x
and
x^{\prime}
separately,
\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta}
is defined in the following ways:

•

\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}\circ\gamma_{2}},}

x=+1
,
x^{\prime}=+1
,

•

\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma_{1})}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\tau(\gamma_{1}),\gamma_{2}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma_{1}\circ\tau(\gamma_{2}))},}

x=-1
,
x^{\prime}=+1
,

•

\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\beta_{\gamma_{1}}}\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma_{1})}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\tau(\gamma_{1}),\tau(\gamma_{2})}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2})},}

x=+1
,
x^{\prime}=-1
,

•

\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma_{1})}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\gamma_{1}}^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\gamma_{1},\tau(\gamma_{2})}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma_{1}\tau(\gamma_{2})},}

x=-1
,
x^{\prime}=-1
.

One checks that these pieces patch together to an isomorphism of super line bundles over
{(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{2}}
, using the compatibility of
\lambda
with the grading and the real structure
\beta
.
Further, one deduces the associativity property of
\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta}
from the associativity condition of
\lambda
. This finalizes the construction of a super 2-line bundle
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P},\psi)
over
\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)
. We find the following result.

Theorem 5.42
.

The assignment described above extends to an essentially injective and faithful functor

\mathscr{R}\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Gamma,\tau)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\mathrm{ref}}(\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau))

from the category of real central super extensions of
(\Gamma,\tau)
to the category of trivial super
2
-line bundles over

\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)
with refinements as morphisms.

Proof.

Let
\theta\colon(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)\to(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
be an isomorphism of real central super extensions. We construct a refinement
(\mathscr{R},\eta)\colon\bigl(\mathscr{I},\tilde{\mathcal{L}},\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta}\bigr)\to\bigl(\mathscr{I},\tilde{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}},\tilde{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}}\bigr)
. The refinement

\mathscr{R}\colon\mathscr{I}\to\mathscr{I}
of super 2-line bundles over
(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{0}=\Gamma_{0}
is taken to be the identity morphism
of
\mathscr{I}
. The 2-isomorphism
\eta
is then defined fibrewise over
(\gamma,x)\in(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{1}
as

\eta\colon\ \tilde{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{L}},\qquad\eta_{(\gamma,x)}:=\begin{cases}\theta_{\gamma}^{-1}\colon\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\to\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}&\text{if}\ x=+1,\\
\theta_{\tau(\gamma)}^{-1}\colon\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\tau(\gamma)}\to\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma)}&\text{if}\ x=-1.\end{cases}

The compatibility of
\theta
with
\lambda
,
\lambda^{\prime}
and
\beta
,
\beta^{\prime}
ensures that this yields a refinement
(\mathscr{R},\eta)
.
One checks that the assignment is functorial.

It is clear that the functor is faithful, so it remains to show that it is essentially injective.
Let
(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)
,
(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
be real central super extensions of
(\Gamma,\tau)
such that there exists an isomorphism

\eta\colon\bigl(\mathscr{I},\tilde{\mathcal{L}},\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta}\bigr)\to\bigl(\mathscr{I},\tilde{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}},\tilde{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}}\bigr)
. We construct an isomorphism

(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)\to(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
as follows.
The isomorphism
\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
over
\Gamma_{1}
is obtained by pulling back the isomorphism
\eta\colon\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
over
(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{1}

along the inclusion
\iota_{1}\colon\Gamma_{1}\to(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{1}
, noting that
(\iota_{1}^{*}\tilde{\mathcal{L}})_{\gamma}=\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{(\gamma,1)}=\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}
by definition
and similar for
\tilde{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
. The compatibility of
\iota_{1}^{*}\eta
with
\lambda
,
\lambda^{\prime}
is then encoded in the pullback of the compatibility diagram
of
\eta
with
\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta}
,
\tilde{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}}
along the inclusion
\iota_{2}\colon\Gamma_{2}\to(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2})_{2}
. It remains to
check that
\iota_{1}^{*}\eta
is compatible with the real structures. Here we use that the real structures on
\mathcal{L}
,
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
are essentially encoded in

\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta},\tilde{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}}
. In detail, we recover the real structure on
\mathcal{L}
from
\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta}
(and the symmetric braiding)
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{(\gamma,1)}\cong\overline{\mathcal{L}_{(\operatorname{id},-1)}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{(\tau(\gamma),-1)}\cong\overline{\mathcal{L}_{(\operatorname{id},-1)}}\otimes\overline{\mathcal{L}_{(\tau(\gamma),1)}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{(\operatorname{id},-1)}

\displaystyle\cong\overline{\mathcal{L}_{(\operatorname{id},-1)}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{(\operatorname{id},-1)}\otimes\overline{\mathcal{L}_{(\tau(\gamma),1)}}\cong\mathcal{L}_{(\operatorname{id},1)}\otimes\overline{\mathcal{L}_{(\tau(\gamma),1)}}\cong\overline{\mathcal{L}_{(\tau(\gamma),1)}}=\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\tau(\gamma)}}.

The compatibility of
\iota_{1}^{*}\eta
with the real structures then follows from the compatibility of
\eta
with
\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta}
,
\tilde{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}}
.
This shows that
\iota_{1}^{*}\eta\colon(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)\to(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
is an isomorphism of real central super extensions.
∎

In combination with our Theorem
5.15
, we obtain the following result.

Corollary 5.43
.

There is a canonical essentially injective and faithful functor

\mathscr{R}\text{s}\mathscr{E}\text{xt}(\Gamma,\tau)\to\mathrm{FM}(\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau))

from the category of real central super extensions
to the category of Freed–Moore’s twisted groupoid extensions.

Next, we compare Moutuou’s real twistings to ours: we associate to each Moutuou real twisting of a real groupoid
(\Gamma,\tau)
a super 2-line bundle over the associated graded Lie groupoid

\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)=(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi_{\tau})
as follows. Let
(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda},F,\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)
be a real twisting of
(\Gamma,\tau)
.
According to Theorem
5.42
,
the real central super extension
(\mathcal{L},\lambda,\beta)
of
(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})
gives rise to a super 2-line bundle

\bigl(\mathscr{I},\tilde{\mathcal{L}},\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta}\bigr)\in\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Lambda\rtimes_{\tau_{\Lambda}}\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi_{\tau_{\Lambda}})
.
Combining Lemma
5.38

with Theorem
3.16
, the real weak equivalence
F\colon(\Lambda,\tau_{\Lambda})\to(\Gamma,\tau)
induces an equivalence of bicategories

\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Lambda\rtimes_{\tau_{\Lambda}}\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi_{\tau_{\Lambda}})\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi_{\tau})
. The image
(\mathscr{\mathcal{L}},\mathscr{P},\psi)

of
\bigl(\mathscr{I},\tilde{\mathcal{L}},\tilde{\lambda}_{\beta}\bigr)
under this equivalence is a super 2-line bundle over
(\Gamma\rtimes_{\tau}\mathbb{Z}_{2},\phi_{\tau})
. Thus, we have the following result.

Theorem 5.44
.

Every Moutuou real twisting in the sense of Definition
5.35
over a real groupoid
{(\Gamma,\tau)}
gives rise to a super
2
-line bundle over the corresponding graded Lie groupoid
\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)
, in such a way that the diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Mou}(\Gamma,\tau)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{FHT}(\Gamma))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{h}_{0}(\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma))}

commutes. Here, the left vertical arrow is described in Remark
5.36
, the right vertical arrow is pullback along
the even functor
(\Gamma,1)\to\mathrm{Gr}(\Gamma,\tau)
of Remark
5.40
,
and the lower horizontal arrow is induced from Theorem
5.26
.

Remark 5.45
.

We have not attempted to construct a full bicategory of Moutuou’s real twistings here; we expect that a proper formulation of
this bicategory of real twistings would allow us to extend the previous construction to a functor. We note that there is a notion of equivalence among such twistings
defined in
[
41
, Definition 2.6.4]
, which we have not included in our comparison since it is rather intricate.

5.5
Chern–Simons twistings

In this section, we review,
based on
[
16
, Section 4]
, how Chern–Simons theory gives rise to equivariant twistings and a corresponding theory of twisted equivariant vector bundles, and we show that both fit into our general setting.

Let
G
be a compact Lie group. For maps
G^{k}\to G^{l}
between products of
G
with itself, we use a self-explaining notation that combines projections, multiplication, and inversion; e.g.,
p_{1,2\cdot 3,4^{-1}}\colon G^{5}\to G^{3}
denotes the map
(g_{1},\dots,g_{5})\mapsto\bigl(g_{1},g_{2}g_{3},g^{-1}_{4}\bigr)
.
Moreover, the pullback along such a map will be denoted by
p_{1,2\cdot 3,4^{-1}}^{*}(\dots)=(\dots)_{1,2\cdot 3,4^{-1}}
. The following definition is
[
9
, Definition 5.1]
.

Definition 5.46
.

A
multiplicative bundle gerbe over
G
is a bundle gerbe
\mathscr{G}
over
G
together with a 1-isomorphism

\mathscr{K}\colon\mathscr{G}_{1}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{2}\to\mathscr{G}_{1\cdot 2}

over
G\times G
,
and a 2-isomorphism

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.27783pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.36829pt\raise-15.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.27783pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.99998pt\raise-3.47221pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\theta}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.28975pt\raise-0.9571pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.28975pt\raise 0.9571pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.40283pt\raise-24.55121pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
