0.9571pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 103.43759pt\raise-15.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{K}_{1\cdot 2,3}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 103.43759pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-22.40282pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{1}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{2\cdot 3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.59021pt\raise-37.71109pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{K}_{1,2\cdot 3}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.90979pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.27783pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 87.90979pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{1\cdot 2\cdot 3}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

over
G\times G\times G
that satisfies an evident Pentagon axiom over
G^{4}
.

Definition 5.47
.

Let
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{K},\theta)
and
\bigl(\mathscr{G}^{\prime},\mathscr{K}^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}\bigr)
be multiplicative bundle gerbes over
G
. A
1
-morphism

(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{K},\theta)\to\bigl(\mathscr{G}^{\prime},\mathscr{K}^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}\bigr)
consists of a 1-morphism
\mathscr{B}\colon\mathscr{G}\to\mathscr{G}^{\prime}

over
G
and a 2-isomorphism

\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{1}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{B}_{1}\otimes\mathscr{B}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{K}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{1\cdot 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{B}_{1\cdot 2}}
\textstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\mathscr{G}^{\prime}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{K}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{1\cdot 2}^{\prime}}

over
G\times G
, satisfying an evident compatibility condition with
\theta
,
\theta^{\prime}
over
G^{3}
.
If
(\mathscr{B},\xi),\bigl(\hat{\mathscr{B}},\hat{\xi}\bigr)\colon\allowbreak(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{K},\theta)\to\bigl(\mathscr{G}^{\prime},\mathscr{K}^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}\bigr)

are 1-morphisms, then a
2
-morphism

(\mathscr{B},\xi)\Rightarrow\bigl(\hat{\mathscr{B}},\hat{\xi}\bigr)
consists of a 2-morphism
\alpha\colon\mathscr{B}\Rightarrow\hat{\mathscr{B}}
satisfying an evident compatibility
condition with
\xi
and
\hat{\xi}
over
G\times G
.

It is straightforward to show that multiplicative bundle gerbes, together with the 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms form a bicategory
\mathscr{M}\mathrm{ult}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(G)
.

Multiplicative bundle gerbes over
G
are classified up to isomorphism by
\mathrm{H}^{4}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathbb{Z})

[
9
, Proposition 5.2]
. The procedure
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{K},\theta)\mapsto\mathscr{G}
of forgetting the multiplicative structure realizes in cohomology the “transgression” homomorphism

\mathrm{H}^{4}(\mathrm{B}G,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{H}^{3}(G,\mathbb{Z}).

(5.15)

In other words, a bundle gerbe over
G
is multiplicative if and only if its “Dixmier–Douady-class” in
\mathrm{H}^{3}(G,\mathbb{Z})
lies in the image of that homomorphism.

The transgression homomorphism (
5.15
) factors in fact through the
G
-equivariant cohomology of
G
,
\mathrm{H}^{3}_{G}(G,\mathbb{Z})
, where
G
acts on itself by conjugation.
We describe in the following how this factorization is realized on the level of geometric objects. We consider a multiplicative bundle gerbe
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{K},\theta)
over
G
.
As
G
acts on itself via conjugation, we may form the action groupoid
G/\!\!/G
.
A bundle gerbe over
G/\!\!/G
(see Definition
3.3
and Remark
3.9
) can now be constructed as follows.
The underlying bundle gerbe over
G
is
\mathscr{G}
. The 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}}
over
G\times G
is defined by the composition

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.16667pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.16667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.1667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.1667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\!\mathscr{G}_{1}^{*}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{1}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 91.98274pt\raise 6.41666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{1,2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 132.16692pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 106.16689pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 132.16692pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\!\mathscr{G}_{1}^{*}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{1\cdot 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 185.40643pt\raise 7.73389pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50613pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\mathscr{K}^{-1}_{1\cdot 2\cdot 1^{-1},1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 247.41713pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 195.41707pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 221.4171pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 247.41713pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\!\mathscr{G}_{1}^{*}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{1\cdot 2\cdot 1^{-1}}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 343.40355pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 343.40355pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\!\mathscr{G}_{1\cdot 2\cdot 1^{-1}},}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

where the unlabel arrows are given by duality/symmetry in the bicategory of bundle gerbes.
The 2-isomorphism
\psi_{\theta}
over
G^{3}
is induced from
\theta
and diagram (
3.4
) commutes because of the pentagon axiom
required in Definition
5.46
.
We find the following.

Proposition 5.48
.

The assignment
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{K},\theta)\mapsto(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}},\psi_{\theta})
extends to a functor

\mathscr{M}\mathrm{ult}\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(G)\to\mathscr{G}\mathrm{rb}(G/\!\!/G)\subseteq\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(G/\!\!/G)

between the bicategory of multiplicative bundle gerbes over
G
and the bicategory of bundle gerbes over the action groupoid
G/\!\!/G
.

Proof.

Let
(\mathscr{B},\xi)\colon(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{K},\theta)\to\bigl(\mathscr{G}^{\prime},\mathscr{K}^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}\bigr)
be a 1-morphism of multiplicative bundle gerbes. It is tedious but
straightforward to construct a 2-isomorphism
\eta\colon\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}^{\prime}}\circ s^{*}\mathscr{B}\Rightarrow t^{*}\mathscr{B}\circ\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}}
from the 2-isomorphism

\xi
and deduce the coherence property of
\eta
from the one of
\xi
.
Given a 2-morphism
\alpha\colon(\mathscr{B},\xi)\Rightarrow\bigl(\hat{\mathscr{B}},\hat{\xi}\bigr)
, it is also straightforward to show that
\alpha
is compatible with

\eta
and
\hat{\eta}
. Functoriality follows from a standard pasting diagram argument.
∎

Remark 5.49
.

If
G
is finite, and, correspondingly,
\mathscr{G}
is the trivial bundle gerbe, then Proposition
5.48
reproduces a construction outlined in
[
16
, equation (4.5)]
, using that a 1-isomorphism between trivial bundle gerbes
is precisely a line bundle.

The
(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}},\psi_{\theta})
-twisted vector bundles (in the sense of Definition
4.2
)
play an important role in Chern–Simons theory, as we review next, following
[
16
, Section 4]
.
For the rest of the section, we let
G
be a finite group.
Then,
\mathrm{H}^{3}(G,\mathbb{Z})=0
, so every bundle gerbe over
G
is trivial.
Let
(\mathscr{K},\theta)
be a multiplicative structure on the trivial bundle gerbe over
G
. As an isomorphism between trivial bundle gerbes, we may identify
\mathscr{K}
with a line bundle over
G\times G
, and
\theta
with a line bundle isomorphism over
G^{3}
.
Using
(\mathscr{K},\theta)
, the category
\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(G)
of complex vector bundles over
G
can be equipped with a monoidal structure by twisted convolution: if
\mathcal{W}
,
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
are vector bundles, then

(\mathcal{W}\otimes\mathcal{W}^{\prime})_{g}:=\bigoplus_{hh^{\prime}=g}\mathscr{K}_{h,h^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{W}_{h}\otimes\mathcal{W}^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}},

and the associator is defined using
\theta
in a straightforward way.
This yields a (
\mathbb{C}
-linear) tensor category that we denote by
\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(G)^{\otimes(\mathscr{K},\theta)}
.
The Drinfel’d centre
\mathscr{Z}\bigl(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(G)^{\otimes(\mathscr{K},\theta)}\bigr)
is a modular tensor category; via the Reshetikhin–Turaev construction it
determines an extended topological quantum field theory, with the given modular tensor category as its value on the circle.

The category

\mathscr{Z}\bigl(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(G)^{\otimes(\mathscr{K},\theta)}\bigr)
can also be described in terms of the
G
-equivariant structure
(\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}},\psi_{\theta})
obtained from
(\mathscr{K},\theta)
, see
[
16
, Proposition 4.9]
. To recall this, we view
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}}
as a line bundle over
G\times G
, and
\psi_{\theta}
as a line bundle isomorphism over
G^{3}
.
Then, the objects in
\mathscr{Z}\bigl(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(G)^{\otimes(\mathscr{K},\theta)}\bigr)
are vector bundles
\mathcal{W}
over
G
together with an isomorphism

\varphi\colon\mathcal{W}_{1}\to\mathcal{W}_{2\cdot 1\cdot 2^{-1}}\otimes(\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}})_{1,2}
over
G\times G
satisfying a compatibility condition with
\psi_{\theta}
, cf.
[
16
, equation 4.9]
.
Put differently,
\mathcal{W}\colon\mathscr{I}\to\mathscr{I}
is a 1-morphism of trivial bundle gerbes and
\varphi

is a 2-isomorphism
{s^{*}\mathcal{W}\!\Rightarrow t^{*}\mathcal{W}\circ\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}}}
and hence the pair
(\mathcal{W},\varphi)
is precisely an object in

\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}},\psi_{\theta})}(G/\!\!/G,1)
in the sense of Definition
4.2
. A similar construction applies to the morphisms, and yields the following result.

Proposition 5.50
.

There is an equivalence of categories

\mathscr{Z}\bigl(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(G)^{\otimes(\mathscr{K},\theta)}\bigr)\cong\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}},\psi_{\theta})}(G/\!\!/G)

between the Drinfel’d centre
\mathscr{Z}\bigl(\mathscr{V}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(G)^{\otimes(\mathscr{K},\theta)}\bigr)
and the category of
(\mathscr{I},\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{K}},\psi_{\theta})
-twisted
vector bundles over
(G/\!\!/G)
.

5.6
Freed’s twisted invertible algebra bundles

We show in Theorem
5.56
below that Freed’s twisted invertible algebra bundles
[
15
]
, for a subclass of their involved double covers, are the same as graded-equivariant super 2-line bundles.
The following definition is a smooth version of
[
15
, Definition 1.95]
.

Definition 5.51
.

A
double cover
of a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
is a triple
\omega=\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi},\pi\bigr)
consisting of a graded Lie groupoid
\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)
and a weak equivalence
\pi\colon\tilde{\Gamma}\to\Gamma
.

Next we recall the definitions of Freed’s twisted invertible algebra bundles following
[
15
, Definitions 1.59 and 1.99]
.

Definition 5.52
.

Let
\omega=\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi},\pi\bigr)
be a double cover of a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
. An
\omega
-twisted invertible algebra bundle over
\Gamma
is a triple
\mathscr{A}=(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},\lambda)

consisting of the following data:

•

a central simple super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
over
\tilde{\Gamma}_{0}
,

•

an invertible super bimodule bundle
\mathcal{B}
over
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}

whose fibre over
\gamma\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{t(\gamma)}^{\tilde{\phi}(\gamma)}
-
\mathcal{A}_{s(\gamma)}
-bimodule, and

•

an invertible even intertwiner

over

Here, we have employed our notation (
5.4
) saying that
(..)^{\tilde{\phi}}
means complex conjugation when
{\tilde{\phi}=-1}
and no operation otherwise.
For the sake of readability, we will omit writing out the algebra in relative tensor products in the following. We remark that – despite of the terminology – a twisted invertible algebra bundle over
\Gamma
makes no contact with
\Gamma
or the functor
{\pi\colon\tilde{\Gamma}\to\Gamma}
. Next we recall the morphisms, following
[

Definition 5.53
.

Let
\omega=\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi},\pi\bigr)

-morphism

over

-bimodules

(5.16)

We remark that morphisms are only defined between twisted invertible algebra bundles with the same double cover
\omega
. Finally, we recall the definition of 2-morphisms from
[
15
, Definition 1.62 (ii)]
.

Definition 5.54
.

Let
\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{1},\mu^{1}\bigr)
and
\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{2},\mu^{2}\bigr)
be 1-morphisms between
\omega
-twisted invertible algebras
\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}
. A
2
-morphism

\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{1},\mu^{1}\bigr)\Rightarrow\bigl(\mathcal{C}^{2},\mu^{2}\bigr)
is an invertible even intertwiner
\nu
of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{x}
-
\mathcal{A}_{x}
-bimodules,

\nu_{x}\colon\mathcal{C}^{1}_{x}\to\mathcal{C}^{2}_{x}

such that the following diagram of intertwiners is commutative for all
\gamma\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.88808pt\raise-35.35114pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.88808pt\raise-35.35114pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\otimes{\mathcal{C}^{2}}_{s(\gamma)}.}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}\end{gathered}

Freed remarks that
\omega
-twisted invertible algebra bundles over
\Gamma
form a bigroupoid, which we denote by
\mathrm{Fr}(\Gamma)^{\omega}
in order to reflect the terminology appropriately. Let us first note the following result, which translates one-to-one between Freed’s and our setting.

Lemma 5.55
.

Let
\omega=\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi},\pi\bigr)
be a double cover of a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
. There is a canonical equivalence of bigroupoids

\mathrm{Fr}(\Gamma)^{\omega}\cong\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)^{\times}

between Freed’s
\omega
-twisted invertible algebra bundles and the maximal sub-bigroupoid of the bicategory of central simple super algebra bundles over
\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)
.

Proof.

The equivalence is a straightforward comparison of the Definitions
5.52
,

5.53
, and
5.54
with Definitions
3.3
,
3.5
, and
3.6
. We only note that, on the level of 1-morphisms,
the comparison inverts the map
\mu
in Definition
5.53
to get the map
\eta

from Definition
3.5
, and vice versa.
∎

The equivalence of Lemma
5.55
does not properly reflect the meaning of twisted invertible algebra bundles, as they are meant to represent twistings on
\Gamma
and not twistings on
\tilde{\Gamma}
.
In order to resolve this, we will restrict to double covers
\omega=\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi},\pi\bigr)
, where
\tilde{\phi}=\phi\circ\pi
, for a grading
\phi
on
\Gamma
.
We will call double covers of this kind
\phi
-induced double covers
.

We assume that
(\Gamma,\phi)
is a graded Lie groupoid and
\omega=\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi},\pi\bigr)
is a
\phi
-induced double cover of
\Gamma
. We are then in position to construct a 2-functor

\mathrm{Fr}(\Gamma)^{\omega}\cong\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)^{\times}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)\cong\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi),

(5.17)

where the first equivalence is Lemma
5.55
, the arrow is the inclusion of central simple super algebra bundles into super 2-line bundles, and the second equivalence is Theorem
3.16
.
Since the right-hand side in (
5.17
) is independent of the double cover, the 2-functor (
5.17
) extends to the disjoint union over all
\phi
-induced double covers of
(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Theorem 5.56
.

Let
(\Gamma,\phi)
be a graded Lie groupoid. Then, the
2
-functor

\coprod_{\omega}\mathrm{Fr}(\Gamma)^{\omega}\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi),

where the disjoint union goes over all
\phi
-induced double covers of
(\Gamma,\phi)
,
is essentially surjective.

Proof.

Let
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
be a super 2-line bundle over
(\Gamma,\phi)
. Then,
\mathscr{L}
has some surjective submersion
\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
. The corresponding covering groupoid
\Gamma^{\pi}
(see Definition
A.4
) has a natural grading
\tilde{\phi}:=\phi\circ\pi
, where
\pi\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma
, and
\pi\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma
is a weak equivalence. Thus,
\omega:=\bigl(\Gamma^{\pi},\pi,\tilde{\phi}\bigr)
is a
\phi
-induced double cover. The pullback
\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}
is canonically isomorphic to a central simple super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}
. Transferring the equivariant structure along this isomorphism produces a central simple algebra bundle
(\mathcal{A},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
over
\big(\Gamma^{\pi},\tilde{\phi}\big)
; which, via Lemma
5.55
, is a
\omega
-twisted invertible algebra bundle. Since the equivalence of Theorem
3.16
, which is used in the definition of (
5.17
), is established by pullback along the weak equivalence
\pi
, and
\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}\cong\mathcal{A}
, it follows that
{(\mathcal{A},\mathscr{P}^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})}
is indeed an essential preimage of
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
.
∎

We expect that, if one enlarges the definitions above by allowing for non-invertible morphisms and morphisms between twisted invertible algebra bundles for different double covers, then
Theorem
5.56
lifts to an equivalence of bicategories.

5.7
Distler–Freed–Moore twistings

In this section, we want to relate the twistings introduced by Distler, Freed, and Moore to
graded-equivariant super 2-line bundles
[
11
,
12
]
; we will use a version described in the slides
[
14
]
. The following is a smooth version
of that definition.

Definition 5.57
.

Let
\Gamma
be a Lie groupoid and
\omega=\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi},\pi\bigr)
a double cover of
\Gamma
in the sense of Definition
5.51
.
A
DFM
\omega
-twisting
of
\Gamma
is a triple
\tau=(d,\mathcal{L},\lambda)
consisting of a locally constant function

d\colon\tilde{\Gamma}_{0}\to\mathbb{Z}
, a super line bundle
\mathcal{L}
over
\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}
,
and an isomorphism of super line bundles over
\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}
, which fibre-wise over
(g,f)\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}
reads

\lambda_{g,f}\colon\mathcal{L}_{g}^{\tilde{\phi}(f)}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{f}\to\mathcal{L}_{g\circ f}
.
The map
d
is subject to the condition that
d\circ s=d\circ t
, i.e.,

d(s(f))=d(t(f))
for all
f\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}
, and the isomorphism
\lambda
is required
to be associative over
\tilde{\Gamma}_{3}
.

We add the following notion of morphisms between DFM
\omega
-twistings.
A
morphism
of DFM
\omega
-twistings

\tau\to\tau^{\prime}
is a morphism of
\tilde{\phi}
-twisted super extensions of
\tilde{\Gamma}
in the sense of Definition
5.12
, and it is required that the maps
d
and
d^{\prime}
coincide.
We denote the category of DFM
\omega
-twistings of
\Gamma
by
\mathrm{DFM}^{\omega}(\Gamma)
.
By comparison of definitions, we find the following.

Lemma 5.58
.

Let
\Gamma
be a Lie groupoid and
\omega=\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi},\pi\bigr)
a double cover of
\Gamma
.
There is an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{DFM}(\Gamma)^{\omega}\cong C^{\infty}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\mathbb{Z}\bigr)_{\rm dis}\times\mathrm{FM}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)
,
where
C^{\infty}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\mathbb{Z}\bigr)
denotes the set of smooth maps
d\colon\tilde{\Gamma}_{0}\to\mathbb{Z}
such that
d\circ s=d\circ t
.

Next we exhibit the relation to super 2-line bundles, and consider first the functor

C^{\infty}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\mathbb{Z}\bigr)_{\rm dis}\to\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)^{\times}

defined in the following way. The function
d
will select a Morita equivalence class of central simple super algebras, i.e., an element in the Brauer–Wall group
\mathrm{BW}_{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\{0,1\}
. We shall fix representatives:
A_{0}:=\mathbb{C}
for the neutral element, and
A_{1}:=\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathbb{C}u
(where
u
is odd and
{u^{2}=1}
) for the non-trivial element. Then, we assign to
d
the central simple super algebra bundle
\mathcal{A}^{d}:=\tilde{\Gamma}_{0}\times A_{d\text{ mod }2}
.
In order to equip
\mathcal{A}^{d}
with a
\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)
-equivariant structure
(\mathscr{P},\psi)
in the sense of Definition
3.3
, we note that there are canonical super algebra isomorphisms
A_{0}\cong\overline{A_{0}}
(just complex conjugation) and
A_{1}\cong\overline{A_{1}}
, given by
x\oplus yu\mapsto\overline{x}\oplus\overline{y}u
. Thus, we have a bundle isomorphism
\rho\colon\mathcal{A}^{d}\to\overline{\mathcal{A}^{d}}
. The required 1-isomorphism
\mathscr{P}\colon s^{*}\mathcal{A}^{d}\to\bigl(t^{*}\mathcal{A}^{d}\bigr)^{\phi}
is defined to be the identity on connected components with
\phi=1
, and to be induced by the isomorphism
\rho
over connected components with
\phi=-1
, under the assignment of invertible bimodules to algebra isomorphisms via the framing. The 2-isomorphism
\psi
is the identity (using that
\rho^{2}=\operatorname{id}
).

Second, we consider the functor

\mathrm{FM}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\text{ref}}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)\subseteq\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)^{\times}

of
Theorem
5.15
. Together with the symmetric monoidal structure on super 2-line bundles, see also Remark
3.14
, we obtain a functor

\displaystyle C^{\infty}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\mathbb{Z}\bigr)_{\rm dis}\times\mathrm{FM}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)^{\times}\times\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)^{\times}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\otimes}}{{\to}}\mathrm{cs\text{-}s}\mathscr{A}\mathrm{lg}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}^{\mathrm{bi}}\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi}\bigr)^{\times}.

(5.18)

We assume now that
(\Gamma,\phi)
is a graded Lie groupoid and

\omega=\bigl(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\phi},\pi\bigr)
is a
\phi
-induced double cover of
\Gamma
, see Section
5.6
. Combining Lemma
5.58
, Theorem
3.16
, and
the functor (
5.18
), we obtain a functor

\mathrm{DFM}(\Gamma)^{\omega}\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi)
.

Theorem 5.59
.

The functor

\coprod_{\omega}\mathrm{DFM}(\Gamma)^{\omega}\to\mathrm{s}2\mathscr{L}\mathscr{B}\mathrm{dl}(\Gamma,\phi),

where the disjoint union goes over all
\phi
-induced double covers of
(\Gamma,\phi)
, is essentially surjective.

Proof.

Let
(\mathscr{L},\mathscr{P},\psi)
be a super 2-line bundle over
(\Gamma,\phi)
. We first extract the map
d
. On each connected component
c
of
\Gamma_{0}
,
\mathscr{L}
has some Morita class
d_{c}\in\mathrm{BW}_{\mathbb{C}}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
in the Brauer–Wall group of
\mathbb{C}
, see
[
31
, Section 3.1]
.
We regard this as a smooth map
d\colon\Gamma_{0}\to\mathbb{Z}_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}
. The isomorphism
{\mathscr{P}\colon s^{*}\mathscr{L}\to t^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\phi}}
implies that
d(s(\gamma))=d(t(\gamma))
. We let
\mathcal{A}^{-d}
be the central simple algebra bundle over
(\Gamma,\phi)
that corresponds to
-d
via the construction above.
Then,
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{L}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{-d}
is a super 2-line bundle over
(\Gamma,\phi)
with trivial Morita class, i.e., a super bundle gerbe
[
31
, Proposition 3.2.4]
. As in the proof of Theorem
5.56
, we then construct a
\phi
-induced double cover
\omega=\bigl(\Gamma^{\pi},\pi,\tilde{\phi}\bigr)
from the surjective submersion of
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
, so that the pullback
\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
is trivializable. By Theorem
5.15
, it can then be identified with a
\tilde{\phi}
-twisted super extension of
\Gamma^{\pi}
.
∎

Remark 5.60
.

Our functor (
5.18
) above may be used, together with Lemmas
5.58
and
5.55
, to obtain a functor

\mathrm{DFM}(\Gamma)^{\omega}\to\mathrm{Fr}(\Gamma)^{\omega}

that relates Distler–Freed–Moore’s twistings directly to Freed’s invertible algebra bundles.

Appendix A
Appendix: Lie groupoids and weak equivalences

Lie groupoids, smooth functors, and smooth natural transformations form a strict 2-category
\mathscr{L}\text{ie}\mathscr{G}\text{rpd}^{\rm fun}
. The 1-isomorphisms in this 2-category are called
strong equivalences
. There is also a bicategory
\mathscr{L}\text{ie}\mathscr{G}\text{rpd}
whose objects are Lie groupoids, whose 1-morphisms are smooth anafunctors, and whose 2-morphisms are smooth anafunctor transformations, see, for example,
[
33
,
46
,
49
]
.
This bicategory
\mathscr{L}\text{ie}\mathscr{G}\text{rpd}
is equivalent to the bicategory of differentiable stacks
[
47
]
. Smooth anafunctors are also known as bibundles, Hilsum–Skandalis maps, or generalized morphisms; the precise notion is not of relevance for this article. Invertible smooth anafunctors establish Morita equivalences between Lie groupoids.

Every smooth functor induces a smooth anafunctor, this way making up a functor

\mathscr{L}\text{ie}\mathscr{G}\text{rpd}^{\rm fun}\allowbreak\to\mathscr{L}\text{ie}\mathscr{G}\text{rpd}

that is the identity on the level of objects, and fully faithful on 2-morphism level.
A smooth functor is called a
weak equivalence
if and only if its induced smooth anafunctor is invertible, see
[
37
, Definition 58/Proposition 60]
or
[
32
, Lemma 3.34]
, and hence a Morita equivalence.
The following definition characterizes them precisely.

Definition A.1
.

A smooth functor
F\colon\Gamma\to\Lambda
between Lie groupoids is called a
weak equivalence
if
both of the following conditions are satisfied:

•

F
is smoothly essentially surjective: the map

s\circ\operatorname{pr}_{\Lambda_{1}}\colon\Gamma_{0}\hskip-1.49994pt\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08153pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9868pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{F_{0}}}}}\times^{{\kern-28.32295pt\mathchoice{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08153pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9868pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-14.26364pt{F_{0}}\kern 8.071pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08153pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9868pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{F_{0}}}}}\times^{{\kern-28.32295pt\mathchoice{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08153pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9868pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-14.26364pt{F_{0}}\kern 8.071pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08153pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9868pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{F_{0}}}}}\times^{{\kern-20.70094pt\mathchoice{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.09795pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08153pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9868pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt{t}}}}\Lambda_{1}\to\Lambda_{0}

is a surjective submersion.

•

F
is smoothly fully faithful: the commutative square

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern is a pullback diagram in the category of smooth manifolds.

Remark A.2
.

The following facts follow immediately from the fact that weak equivalences become 1-isomorphisms in a bicategory:

1.

If
T,T^{\prime}\colon\Lambda\to\Gamma
are smooth functors, and there is a smooth natural transformation
T\Rightarrow T^{\prime}
, then

T
is a weak equivalence if and only if
T^{\prime}
is a weak equivalence.

2.

Weak equivalences satisfy the 2-out-of-3 property: if
T\colon\Omega\to\Lambda
and
F\colon\Lambda\to\Gamma
are smooth functors,
and any two of the three smooth functors
T
,
F
, and
F\circ T
are weak equivalences, then so is the third.

3.

If
F,F^{\prime}\colon\Omega\to\Lambda
are smooth functors,
T\colon\Lambda\to\Gamma
is a weak equivalence, then there is a smooth natural transformation

T\circ F\Rightarrow T\circ F^{\prime}
if and only if there is a smooth natural transformation
F\Rightarrow F^{\prime}
.

Proposition A.3

(
[
45
, Theorem 2.16]
)
.

Suppose
F\colon\Gamma\to\Lambda
is a weak equivalence between Lie groupoids.
Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a
2
-stack on the site of smooth manifolds. Then, the functor

F^{*}\colon\mathfrak{X}(\Lambda)\to\mathfrak{X}(\Gamma)

is an equivalence of bicategories.

Next we discuss the notion of a covering groupoid and provide some results we use in the main text.

Definition A.4
.

Let
\Gamma
be a Lie groupoid and
\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
be a surjective submersion. The

covering groupoid

\Gamma^{\pi}
has objects
\Gamma^{\pi}_{0}:=Y
and morphisms
\Gamma^{\pi}_{1}:=Y\hskip-1.49994pt\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79312pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99509pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\pi}}}}\times^{{\kern-17.1201pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79312pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99509pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-10.97523pt{\pi}\kern 8.071pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79312pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99509pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\pi}}}}\times^{{\kern-17.1201pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.67632pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79312pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99509pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.071pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-10.97523pt{\pi}\kern 4.54321pt{\pi}}}}Y
, and its structural maps are defined by

s(y_{2},\gamma,y_{1}):=y_{1},\qquad t(y_{2},\gamma,y_{1}):=y_{2},\qquad(y_{3},\gamma_{23},y_{2})\circ(y_{2},\gamma_{12},y_{1}):=(y_{3},\gamma_{23}\circ\gamma_{12},y_{1}).

The
projection functor

P^{\pi}\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma
is given by
P_{0}:=\pi
and
P_{1}(y_{2},\gamma,y_{1}):=\gamma
.

Example A.5
.

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and
\pi\colon Y\to M
a surjective submersion. Then,
\text{\v{C}}(\pi):=M_{\rm dis}^{\pi}
is the usual Čech groupoid.

Lemma A.6
.

Let
\Gamma
be a Lie groupoid. If
\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
is a surjective submersion, then the projection functor
P^{\pi}\colon\Gamma^{\pi}\to\Gamma
is a weak equivalence. Moreover, if
\zeta\colon Z\to Y
is a further surjective submersion, then the evident functor
\Gamma^{\pi\circ\zeta}\to\Gamma^{\pi}
is a weak equivalence.

Proof.

The first statement can be checked directly. The second statement follows from Remark
A.2

2
.
∎

Lemma A.7
.

Let
\Lambda\colon\Gamma\to\Omega
be smooth functor, and let
\pi\colon Y\to\Omega_{0}
be a surjective submersion. Then, there exists a surjective submersion
\zeta\colon Z\to\Gamma_{0}
and a functor
\tilde{\Lambda}\colon\Gamma^{\zeta}\to\Omega^{\pi}
such that the diagram

is strictly commutative. Moreover, if
\Lambda
is a weak equivalence, then
\tilde{\Lambda}
is a weak equivalence.

Proof.

The surjective submersion
\zeta
is the pullback of
\pi
along
\Lambda_{0}
, i.e., we have a pullback diagram

This defines
\tilde{\Lambda}
on the level of objects, and it is straightforward to complete its definition.
∎

Coverings groupoids are important because weak equivalences can be inverted on covering groupoids; see, e.g.,
[
45
, Proposition 5.7]
.

Proposition A.8
.

A smooth functor
T\colon\Lambda\to\Gamma
is a weak equivalence if and only if there exists a surjective submersion
\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
and a strong equivalence

S\colon\Lambda\to\Gamma^{\pi}
such that
T\cong P^{\pi}\circ S
.

Finally, we discuss fibre products of Lie groupoids.

.

and

be smooth functors of Lie groupoids for which either

is a surjective submersion. The
fibre product

\Lambda\times_{\Gamma}\Lambda^{\prime}

is the Lie groupoid whose objects are tuples
(p,\phi,p^{\prime})
, where
p\in\Lambda
,
p^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
are objects and
\phi\colon T(p)\to T^{\prime}(p^{\prime})
is an isomorphism.
Morphisms
(p,\phi,p^{\prime})\to(q,\psi,q^{\prime})
are pairs
{(\alpha,\beta)}
where
\alpha\colon p\to q
and
\beta\colon p^{\prime}\to q^{\prime}
are morphisms such that
the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{T(p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T(\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{T(q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{T^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T^{\prime}(\beta)}
\textstyle{T^{\prime}(q^{\prime}).}

T^{\prime}}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{t}}}}\times^{{\kern-9.78764pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.54321pt{s\circ T^{\prime}}}}}\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}
.

Remark A.10
.

The fibre product of Lie groupoids is a 2-pullback in the bicategory
\mathscr{L}\text{ie}\mathscr{G}\text{rpd}^{\rm fun}
, see
[
39
, Section 5.3]
and

[
33
, Remark 1.18]
for further references.

Remark A.11
.

The following elementary consequence of these definitions is
[
33
, Proposition 1.26]
.
For a weak equivalence
T\colon\Lambda\to\Gamma
and a smooth functor
F\colon\Lambda^{\prime}\to\Gamma
, their fibre product
{\Lambda\times_{\Gamma}\Lambda^{\prime}}
exists.
Moreover, the projection
\Lambda\times_{\Gamma}\Lambda^{\prime}\to\Lambda^{\prime}
is a weak equivalence.

Lemma A.12
.

Let
T\colon\Lambda\to\Gamma
,
T^{\prime}\colon\Lambda^{\prime}\to\Gamma
,
S\colon\Omega\to\Lambda
and
S^{\prime}\colon\Omega^{\prime}\to\Lambda^{\prime}
weak equivalences of Lie groupoids.
Then, there is a weak equivalence

S^{\prime}\times_{\Gamma}S\colon\Omega^{\prime}\times_{\Gamma}\Omega\to\Lambda^{\prime}\times_{\Gamma}\Lambda
.

Proof.

Combine Remarks
A.10
,
A.11
, and
A.2
.
∎

Fibre products of surjective submersions and fibre products of Lie groupoids are compatible in the following way.

Lemma A.13
.

Let
\Gamma
be a Lie groupoid and let

\pi\colon Y\to\Gamma_{0}
and
\pi^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\to\Gamma_{0}
be surjective submersions. Let
Z:=Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y^{\prime}
be the fibre product, and let
\zeta\colon Z\to\Gamma_{0}
be the projection to the base. Then, there is a canonical weak equivalence
\Gamma^{\zeta}\to\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}
such that the diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 8.19446pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.19446pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Gamma^{\zeta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.04422pt\raise-22.39778pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\zeta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.6388pt\raise-26.84314pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.33313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.7638pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 61.33313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.88881pt\raise-28.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.57336pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 28.6388pt\raise-32.57336pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Gamma}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

is strictly commutative.

Proof.

We construct a functor

F\colon\Gamma^{\zeta}\to\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}
. To an object
(y,y^{\prime})\in Y\times_{\Gamma_{0}}Y^{\prime}
we assign the object
F(y,y^{\prime}):=(y,y^{\prime},\operatorname{id}_{\pi(y)})
of
\Gamma^{\pi}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\pi^{\prime}}
. To a morphism
(y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime},\gamma,y_{2},y_{2}^{\prime})
in

\Gamma^{\zeta}
we assign the morphism

((y_{1},\gamma,y_{2}),(y_{1}^{\prime},\gamma,y_{2}^{\prime}))
. This is well-defined, smooth, functorial, and the diagram is obviously commutative. Since the vertical arrows are weak equivalences, the 2-out-of-3 property of weak equivalences (Remark
A.2

2
) shows that
F
is a weak equivalence.
∎
